# Deck: a design system of our own

**Date**: 2026-11-03 18:35
**Summary**: Study luu's default theme and propose MP3 Manager's own design system, "Deck", whose
style evokes old portable audio gear (Walkman, Discman, iPod, hi-fi decks).

**Status**: Proposal, awaiting approval. No app code changed yet.

User request (translated): *"Take a look at luu's design system
(`jgermade/luu`, `web/theme/default`). We'll make our own, with a style of its own. It would be
nice if it evoked old audio equipment: Walkman, Discman, iPod, etc."*

Preview: a static page with the tokens, controls and the library in context was built for review
and shared in the session: https://claude.ai/artifact/UKS4Fy59dtkbK4dpZQYWDw (private, not
committed).

---

## 1. What luu's default theme does

`web/theme/default/` in luu (see its `RECORD/2026-10-21.a-design-system-of-components.completed.md`):

- **A theme is a folder**: `tokens.css` (every value), `components/` (one file each), and a demo
  page that shows them all. A second theme replaces the folder.
- **Tokens by role, not by hue**: grounds (`bg`, `sunken`, `surface`, `field`), inks (`fg`,
  `fg-muted`, `fg-subtle`), four tones (`accent`, `ok`, `warn`, `bad`), each with a `-soft`
  ground. Plus type, space, radii, control heights, shadows and motion.
- **Light on `:root`, dark on `[data-theme="dark"]`**, and any element can be an island of the
  other tone with `data-tone`.
- **Components read tokens with no fallback**: a missing token should look missing, not quietly
  draw a copy of the palette.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion` zeroes the durations once**, in `tokens.css`.
- **App-only tokens live apart** (`luu.css`) from what components need (`tokens.css`).
- **Built beside the app first**; screens move over one at a time.

What we take: the structure and all of the rules above.
What we don't take: the values (palette, type, shape), the jq79 component format (this project is
React), and luu's own surfaces (chat, terminal, context buckets).

## 2. Where mp3-manager stands

- `src/styles.css`: 1718 lines, ~129 top-level classes, 12 colour variables (`--bg`, `--chrome`,
  `--panel`, `--accent`, …) and 56 hard-coded colours outside them. Radii, spacing and type sizes are literals.
- The fonts it names (IBM Plex Sans/Mono, Space Grotesk) are **never loaded**: `index.html` has no
  font link and there is no `@font-face`, so every user gets a system fallback.
- `ThemeService.ts` sets `data-theme` on `<html>`; that stays the switch.
- `index.html` says `lang="es"` and several UI strings are Spanish; both conflict with the
  English-only rule in `AGENTS.md` (see §8).

## 3. Direction

Five rules, which the preview shows:

1. **Evoke, don't copy.** No real product's look: no click wheel, no logos, no chrome photos.
   The references are materials and habits, not a device.
2. **One signal colour.** Signal orange means "this one": the primary key, the selection, a lit
   LED, the playing track. Nothing decorative is orange.
3. **Displays show state.** Now playing, counters and scan progress go in a display window: a
   reflective LCD in the light tone, a VFD in the dark one.
4. **Keys travel.** A pressed key moves 1px down with an inset shadow. Keys sit in banks (a
   recessed well); a latched key stays down with its LED lit.
5. **Printed labels.** Section heads, column heads and field labels are small, wide, uppercase
   and letter-spaced, like the print on a faceplate.

Everything outside those signature elements stays flat and quiet: no textures beyond a faint
brushed line on the faceplate, no skeuomorphic photos.

## 4. Tokens

Prefix `--mm-`. Two tones with the same roles: **Silver** (light: satin aluminium chassis, white
polycarbonate panels, LCD) and **Graphite** (dark: anodised black chassis, VFD).

### Colour

| Token | Role | Silver | Graphite |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `--mm-bg` | page ground | `#e7e9e8` | `#121314` |
| `--mm-chassis` | bars, faceplate, panel heads | `#d4d7d6` | `#1f2022` |
| `--mm-chassis-hi` / `-lo` | chassis highlight / edge | `#eef0ef` / `#b4b9b8` | `#2c2e31` / `#0b0c0d` |
| `--mm-surface` | panels, lists | `#fbfbfa` | `#1a1b1d` |
| `--mm-sunken` | wells, key banks, switch tracks | `#e2e5e4` | `#101112` |
| `--mm-field` | inputs | `#ffffff` | `#0f1011` |
| `--mm-hover` | row under the pointer | `#f0f2f1` | `#242528` |
| `--mm-border` / `-strong` | lines | `#c2c7c6` / `#979d9c` | `#303236` / `#4a4d52` |
| `--mm-key-top` / `-bottom` / `-down` | key face, pressed key | `#f9fafa` / `#e3e6e5` / `#d9dcdb` | `#36393c` / `#27292c` / `#1d1f21` |
| `--mm-fg` / `-muted` / `-subtle` | ink | `#1b1d1d` / `#535a5a` / `#858b8a` | `#ebe9e4` / `#a3a29d` / `#74736f` |
| `--mm-accent` / `-hover` | signal orange, as a fill | `#f05a1a` / `#e04e10` | `#ff6a2b` / `#ff7f47` |
| `--mm-accent-ink` | signal orange, as text | `#b33f0b` | `#ff8a55` |
| `--mm-on-accent` | ink on orange | `#1a1410` | `#140c08` |
| `--mm-accent-soft` | selection | `#fde5d8` | `#3a2015` |
| `--mm-ok` / `-led` / `-soft` | saved, synced | `#2a7a2e` / `#3fbf4a` / `#e2f0e1` | `#6fd27a` / `#5fe06b` / `#15261a` |
| `--mm-warn` / `-led` / `-soft` | unsaved | `#8f5c00` / `#f2a900` / `#f9eed3` | `#ffbf3c` / `#ffbf3c` / `#2b2210` |
| `--mm-bad` / `-led` / `-soft` | failed, delete | `#b82424` / `#e53935` / `#f9e2e0` | `#ff7d73` / `#ff5a4f` / `#2e1614` |
| `--mm-on-bad` | ink on a danger key | `#ffffff` | `#1a0b0a` |
| `--mm-led-off` | unlit LED | `#a3a9a8` | `#3d4044` |
| `--mm-display-bg` / `-fg` | display window | `#bfc9ac` / `#1d2916` (LCD) | `#061210` / `#5ff2d6` (VFD) |
| `--mm-display-dim` | unlit dots and segments | `rgba(29,41,22,.11)` | `rgba(95,242,214,.07)` |
| `--mm-display-glow` | display text-shadow | `none` | `0 0 6px rgba(95,242,214,.55)` |
| `--mm-display-bezel` | frame around the window | `#2b2e2d` | `#000000` |
| `--mm-stripe-1` / `-2` / `-3` | J-card stripes (same in both) | `#f05a1a` / `#f2a900` / `#2bb39f` | same |

Contrast (WCAG 2), checked for every text pair: ink on surface 16.4:1 / 14.2:1, muted ink on
surface 6.8:1 / 6.7:1, muted ink on chassis 4.9:1 / 6.1:1, accent ink on surface 5.6:1 / 7.4:1,
ink on orange 5.4:1 / 6.8:1, LCD 8.8:1, VFD 13.8:1, every tone ink on its `-soft` ≥ 4.5:1.
Only `--mm-fg-subtle` (placeholders, disabled) sits below 4.5:1 (3.4:1 / 3.6:1), as in luu.

### Type

| Token | Face | Job |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `--mm-font-sans` | IBM Plex Sans | reading: titles, names, body |
| `--mm-font-label` | Archivo at `font-stretch: 125%`, 600 | printed labels, headings, keys |
| `--mm-font-mono` | IBM Plex Mono | data: paths, durations, track numbers |
| `--mm-font-display` | Doto, 700 | dot-matrix display: counters, track/total |

Sizes: `--mm-text-label` 10px, `-sm` 12px, `-md` 13px, `-lg` 15px, `-xl` 20px, `-2xl` 28px.
`--mm-tracking-label: .14em`.

### Shape, space and motion

- Space: `--mm-space-1` … `-8` = `.25rem .5rem .75rem 1rem 1.25rem 1.5rem 2rem 3rem`.
- Radii: `--mm-radius-key` 3px, `-md` 6px, `-lg` 14px, `-full` 999px.
- Controls: `--mm-control-sm` / `-md` / `-lg` = 24 / 30 / 36px.
- Shadows: `--mm-key-shadow`, `--mm-key-pressed`, `--mm-well-shadow`, `--mm-shadow-md`, per tone.
- Motion: `--mm-ease: cubic-bezier(.2, .8, .2, 1)`, `--mm-duration-key` 70ms,
  `--mm-duration` 180ms; both `0ms` under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`.

## 5. Components

React, store-free, styled only with `--mm-*` tokens (no fallbacks). Classes are prefixed `mm-`:
the preview hit a collision between an LED dot (`.led`) and a meter variant (`.meter.led`), and a
prefix per component avoids that class of bug.

| Component | Replaces / used by |
| --- | --- |
| `Key` (primary, secondary, ghost, danger; sm/md/lg; square) | every button: `.playback-icon`, `.preferences-trigger`, modal actions, `DetailActionBar` |
| `KeyBank` + latching `Key` with LED | transport in `PlaybackBar`; flat/wizard toggle; future shuffle/repeat |
| `SlideSwitch` (hold switch) | `ThemeToggle`; boolean preferences |
| `Display` + `Counter` | now playing in `PlaybackBar`; scan progress |
| `Meter` (in a display, or as LEDs on the chassis) | the "loading metadata X/Y" text |
| `Lamp` | track and file states, sync status |
| `Reel` (cassette hub spinner) | busy states: scanning, writing |
| `Field`, `TextInput`, `Select`, `Checkbox` | `MetadataEditor`, `PreferencesModal`, the library filter |
| `Tabs` (stripes on the active tab) | `RootTabs` |
| `Panel` (chassis head) and `Modal` | `MetadataEditModal`, `TrackInfoModal`, `PreferencesModal` |
| `ListRow`, `Table` | `TrackWizardList`, `AlbumOverview` |
| `Stripes` | brand mark, active tab, placeholder cover art |

## 6. Layout in the repo

```
src/theme/deck/
  tokens.css          every --mm-* value, Silver on :root, Graphite on [data-theme="dark"]
  fonts.ts            imports the self-hosted faces
  components/
    Key.tsx           one component per file, each with its own Key.css (classes prefixed mm-)
    ...
theme/index.html      demo page, served at /theme/ (second Vite entry in vite.config.ts)
```

Fonts are self-hosted through Fontsource so the app keeps working offline and makes no request
to a third party: `@fontsource/ibm-plex-sans`, `@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono`,
`@fontsource-variable/archivo`, `@fontsource-variable/doto` (four new dependencies).

## 7. Plan

1. **Beside the app**: `tokens.css`, fonts, the components in §5, and the demo page at `/theme/`.
   Nothing in the app changes.
2. **Faceplate**: top bar, `PlaybackBar` (transport + display window), `RootTabs`, `ThemeToggle`.
3. **Library**: `TrackWizardList`, `AlbumOverview`, `DetailActionBar`, scan progress.
4. **Modals and forms**: `MetadataEditModal`, `MetadataEditor`, `TrackInfoModal`,
   `PreferencesModal`.
5. **Clean-up**: remove the old variables and the hard-coded colours from `styles.css`.

Each step is appended here as an extension with its walkthrough and validated with
`npm run build`.

## 8. Cost, rejected options, open questions

**Cost**: four font dependencies (~100–200 KB of woff2, latin subsets only), one extra Vite entry,
and a gradual rewrite of `styles.css` (steps 2–5).

**Rejected**:

- *Skeuomorphic realism* (photo textures, brushed-metal images, 3D knobs): heavy, ages badly and
  costs legibility. Five signature elements carry the style instead.
- *Re-skinning luu's `tokens.css`*: the user asked for a style of our own. We keep its structure
  and none of its values.
- *Porting luu's components*: they are jq79; this app is React.
- *Replicating a specific device* (click wheel, a Walkman's faceplate): it would be a copy, not a
  style.
- *Google Fonts at runtime*: no dependency, but it needs the network and calls a third party from
  a local-first app.

**Open questions for the user**:

1. Name: `deck` (`src/theme/deck/`), or `default` as in luu?
2. Fonts: self-host with Fontsource (four dependencies) or link Google Fonts?
3. Light chassis: silver as proposed, or a dark chassis in both tones, as luu does with its bars?
4. The English-only rule: translate the Spanish UI strings and `lang="es"` as part of this work,
   or in a separate record?
5. Once approved, write the plan in §7 as a Gantt chart under `ROADMAP/2026-11-03/`?

## Extension — 2026-11-03 18:42 — Decisions, revised plan and phase 1 detail

### Decisions (user, translated)

1. **Folder**: `/theme/default/`, as in luu. The theme sits at the front-end root, next to
   `index.html`, the way luu's sits in `web/`, and its demo is served at `/theme/default/`. This
   replaces `src/theme/deck/` in §6. "Deck" stays the name of the style, not of the folder.
2. **Fonts**: download them. The woff2 files (latin subset, which covers English and Spanish) are
   committed to `theme/default/fonts/` with their OFL licences. No Fontsource dependency and no
   request to a third party at runtime. This replaces the four dependencies in §6.
3. **Tones**: Silver is the light tone and Graphite the dark one, as proposed.
4. **Languages**: the UI has English and Spanish texts, with English as the fallback. This needs an
   i18n layer, and the English-only rule in `AGENTS.md` needs an exception for translation files
   (updated in the same commit). It becomes its own step, before any screen moves over, so every
   migrated screen takes its strings from it.
5. **Roadmap**: `ROADMAP/2026-11-03/deck-design-system.md`.

### Revised plan (supersedes the order in §7)

1. Theme beside the app: tokens, fonts, components, demo at `/theme/default/`.
2. i18n: English and Spanish, English fallback; the language follows the browser and can be
   chosen in Preferences.
3. Faceplate: top bar, `PlaybackBar`, `RootTabs`, `ThemeToggle`.
4. Library: `TrackWizardList`, `AlbumOverview`, `DetailActionBar`, scan progress.
5. Modals and forms: `MetadataEditModal`, `MetadataEditor`, `TrackInfoModal`, `PreferencesModal`.
6. Clean-up: remove the old variables and hard-coded colours from `styles.css`.

### Phase 1 detail

```
theme/default/
  index.html          demo document; sets data-theme before the first paint
  demo.tsx, demo.css  the demo: tokens read back from computed style, every component and state
  tokens.css          §4 values: Silver on :root and [data-tone="light"],
                      Graphite on :root[data-theme="dark"] and [data-tone="dark"]
  fonts.css           @font-face for the four families
  fonts/              *.woff2 + OFL-*.txt
  components/         one .tsx + one .css per component, classes prefixed mm-, index.ts barrel
```

- Dark is selected by `data-theme` only, with no `prefers-color-scheme` block: `ThemeService`
  already resolves the system preference and always sets the attribute.
- Components: `Key`, `Led`, `KeyBank`, `KeySelect` (a bank with one key down), `SlideSwitch`,
  `Display` (+ `DisplayRow`, `DisplayLabel`, `DisplayTitle`, `Counter`), `Meter`, `Lamp`, `Reel`,
  `EqBars`, `Legend` (printed label), `Field`, `TextInput`, `Select`, `Checkbox`, `Tabs`, `Panel`,
  `Modal` (native `<dialog>`, like the current modals), `Stripes`, `ListRow`, `Table`.
- Components take their texts as props or children and hold none of their own, except
  accessible names a caller can override, so i18n (step 2) does not reach into the theme.
- `vite.config.ts` gets a second entry (`theme/default/index.html`); `tsconfig.json` includes
  `theme`.
- Nothing under `src/` changes.
- Validation: `npm run build`, and a look at the demo in both tones at desktop and phone width.

## Extension — 2026-11-03 18:52 — Phase 1 walkthrough

- **Fonts**: woff2 files taken from the Fontsource 5.3.0 packages (latin subset) and committed
  to `theme/default/fonts/` with their OFL licences: Plex Sans 400/400 italic/500/600, Plex Mono
  400/500/600, Archivo variable (`wdth` 62–125%, `wght` 100–900), Doto 700. About 255 KB in all;
  Vite inlines Doto (3.9 KB) and hashes the rest. `fonts.css` declares them.
- **Tokens**: `theme/default/tokens.css` with the §4 values. Added while writing the components,
  so none of them carries a literal: `--mm-key-shadow-signal`, `--mm-key-pressed-signal`,
  `--mm-led-inset`, `--mm-display-inset`, `--mm-thumb-shadow`, `--mm-brush`, `--mm-overlay`,
  `--mm-shadow-lg`, `--mm-text-label-sm` (9px), `--mm-text-xs` (11px),
  `--mm-text-display-sm`/`-lg` (17/30px), `--mm-stretch-heading` (112%), weights and leadings.
  The looping animations (Reel, EqBars) scale `--mm-duration-slow`, so reduced motion stops them
  without a media query of their own.
- **Components** (`theme/default/components/`, one `.tsx` and its `.css` each, classes prefixed
  `mm-`, barrel in `index.ts`): `Key`, `Led`, `KeyBank`, `KeySelect`, `SlideSwitch`, `Display`
  (+ `DisplayRow`, `DisplayLabel`, `DisplayTitle`, `Counter`), `Meter`, `Lamp`, `Reel`, `EqBars`,
  `Stripes`, `Legend`, `Field` (+ `fieldNoteId`), `TextInput`, `Select`, `Checkbox`, `Tabs`,
  `Panel`, `Modal`, `ListRow`, `Table`. Field, TextInput and Select share `Input.css`.
- **Choices made on the way**:
  - `ListRow` is a `div` with `role="treeitem"`, not a button: ARIA does not allow `treeitem`
    on a `<button>`. The tree around it owns focus and arrow keys, as `TrackWizardList` does.
  - `Tabs` handles ArrowLeft/ArrowRight/Home/End and keeps one tab in the tab order; the close
    key sits beside each tab, not inside it.
  - `Modal` uses the native `<dialog>` like the current modals; Escape, the close key and a
    backdrop click all call `onClose`.
  - The only texts components hold are overridable accessible names (`Close`, `Close tab`), so
    the i18n step does not reach into the theme.
- **Demo**: `theme/default/index.html` + `demo.tsx` + `demo.css`, a second Vite entry served at
  `/theme/default/`. It shows every colour token of both tones read back from the computed style
  (as `data-tone` islands), type, space and radii, and every component with its variants and
  states; stateful ones print their value. The faceplate's transport drives the display and
  the library section together. The tone switch is stored under `mp3-manager.theme-demo`, not
  the app's key.
- **Config**: `vite.config.ts` gets the `theme` input; `tsconfig.json` includes `theme`.
  `STACK.md` describes the theme; the roadmap marks this step done.
- **Nothing under `src/` changed.**
- **Validation**: `npm run build` (`tsc --noEmit && vite build`) passes. The built demo was
  checked in Chromium at 1280px (Silver, then Graphite with the modal open) and at 400px
  (Graphite): no console errors, no horizontal overflow, all four faces loaded from the repo.

Next: step 2, the i18n layer.

Suggested commit message: `feat(theme): add Deck default theme with tokens, fonts, components and demo`

## Extension — 2026-11-03 18:54 — Proposal: the UI on jq79, in four layers

**Status**: proposal, awaiting approval. Nothing changed for it yet.

User (translated): *"For the UI, how about we use jq79, where:*
- *`components/`: components that depend on no store, only on themselves or on helpers;*
- *`helpers/`: TypeScript functions that depend only on other helpers, ideally with no
  dependencies, and ideally with as much test coverage as possible;*
- *`lib/`: factories, services, stores, etc.; they can depend on helpers and on other `lib/`
  functions;*
- *`views/`: jq79 components that bring the data and the components together."*

### What jq79 is

- npm `jq79` 0.7.7 (luu vendors 0.7.6), ISC, by this project's author. One file, no dependencies.
- A component is an `.html` file: a `<script :setup>` (Svelte-style: `$:` declarations,
  `$emit`, `await $mounted()`, `$destroyed`), a template (`{{ }}`, `:attr`, `:if`, `:each` with
  `:key`, `@event`, slots) and `<style scoped>`. `$reactive` stores with `$on`, `$effect` and
  `$computed`. Proxy-based, fine-grained updates with no virtual DOM.
- It loads two ways. At runtime, with `C79.fetch("./x.html")`: no build, which is what luu does
  because its UI ships inside a Rust binary. Or through its Vite plugin, `jq79/vite`: `.html`
  files import as modules, the imports in a setup script are hoisted so Vite bundles them,
  `<script :setup lang="ts">` has its types stripped, styles go through Vite's CSS pipeline,
  and edits hot-reload. The plugin can also precompile setup scripts (`safeEval`) so the page
  runs without `'unsafe-eval'`.

### Proposal

**Keep Vite, add `jq79/vite`, and replace React.** The four layers ask for TypeScript, which
needs a compile step anyway; Vite already is one, and with the plugin a setup script can
import a `.ts` helper and have it bundled.

```
src/
  helpers/      pure TypeScript; imports only other helpers; no DOM, no stores, no packages
  lib/          stores ($reactive), services, factories: library, playback, selection,
                preferences (theme, language), i18n, VFS, id3, session persistence
  components/   jq79 .html; no store; imports helpers and other components only
  views/        jq79 .html; reads lib/, composes components/
  locales/      en.ts (source and fallback), es.ts
  main.ts       mounts the root view
theme/default/
  tokens.css, fonts.css, fonts/   unchanged
  components/*.html               the Deck components, ported to jq79 (the design system;
                                  src/components/ holds the app's own pieces built from them)
  index.html, demo.html           the demo, as a jq79 page
```

| Layer | May import |
| --- | --- |
| `helpers/` | `helpers/` |
| `lib/` | `helpers/`, `lib/`, `jq79` (for `$reactive`), packages |
| `components/` (app and theme) | `helpers/`, `components/` |
| `views/` | all of the above |

`src/types.ts` (types only, erased at build) stays importable from every layer.

- **The rule is checked, not just written**: a dependency-free script (`scripts/check-layers.mjs`)
  reads the imports of every file under `src/` and `theme/` and fails on an import the table
  forbids. It runs with the tests.
- **Helpers are tested with Node's own runner**, so tests add no dependency:
  `node --test --experimental-strip-types --experimental-test-coverage`, with coverage
  thresholds on `helpers/` (`--test-coverage-lines`, `-branches`, `-functions`). Node runs
  `.ts` directly, so helpers use only erasable syntax (no `enum`, no `namespace`) and import
  each other with the `.ts` extension (`allowImportingTsExtensions`, valid with `noEmit`).
  Tests live beside the helper (`format.ts`, `format.test.ts`).
- **Where today's code goes**: the pure functions at the bottom of `Mp3ManagerView.tsx`
  (`computeCommonMetadata`, `getVariedFields`, `inferDiscLabel`, `trackNumberOrder`,
  `orderDetailPlaylist`, `collectFolderPaths`, `buildWizardRows`) move to `helpers/` with tests;
  its 15 pieces of React state become `lib/` stores; `ThemeService`, `rootSessionStore` and
  `id3` move to `lib/` (`id3` wraps two packages, so it is not a helper; the byte-level parts
  that need no package can become helpers).
- **i18n fits the layers**: lookup with English fallback and `{param}` filling is a helper;
  the chosen language (browser default, saved in Preferences) is a `lib/` store; the texts
  are `locales/en.ts` and `locales/es.ts`.

### Migration: gradual, one screen at a time

React and jq79 can share a page: a jq79 view mounts into an element React renders. So:

1. **Theme in jq79**: port the Deck components and the demo to `.html`; tokens, fonts and almost
   all of the CSS carry over into each `<style scoped>`. The React versions from phase 1 are
   deleted once the port is in.
2. **Layers and tests**: `helpers/` with the functions above and their tests, the coverage
   thresholds and the layer check.
3. **Stores and i18n**: move the view's state into `lib/` stores. React reads them through a
   small hook for as long as it is still there, so the app keeps working between steps.
4. **Views on jq79**, replacing React screens in roadmap order: faceplate, library, modals and
   forms.
5. **Remove React** and clean up `styles.css`.

### Cost and risks

- **Templates are not type-checked.** `lang="ts"` setup scripts lose their types, they are not
  checked. Setup scripts therefore stay thin, and the logic lives in typed, tested
  helpers and `lib/`.
- **jq79 is pre-1.0.** luu has found and upstreamed gaps (a teardown hook, effect batching). Our
  heaviest workload is the library tree: up to a few thousand rows with selection. `:each` with
  `:key` keeps rows that did not change; virtualising the tree, if needed, is ordinary component
  code.
- **Two frameworks for a while**: the bundle is larger until step 5.
- **Phase 1's React components are discarded** after step 1 (~1 000 lines of TSX); the tokens,
  the fonts, the CSS and the demo's content stay.
- `RECORD/2026-08-11.musikmatch-rust-migration.WIP.md` says the desktop build "reuses the React
  UI"; with this it reuses the jq79 one. Tauri serves any static build, so nothing else there
  changes.

### Rejected

- **jq79 without Vite (runtime fetch, as luu does)**: the TypeScript helpers and `lib/` would
  need a compile step of their own, and the bundling and hashed assets would go. luu needs it so
  that `cargo build` never runs node; if a Rust daemon ever has to embed this UI under the same
  rule, the same `.html` files can be loaded that way.
- **Keep React and adopt only the four layers**: the layers do not depend on the framework, but
  the request is jq79, and it is also the stack of luu, the sister project.

### Open questions

1. Migrate gradually through shared stores (as above), or rewrite the views in one go?
2. Coverage thresholds for `helpers/`: 90% lines, branches and functions as the floor?
3. Setup scripts in `lang="ts"` (types stripped, not checked) or plain JavaScript?
4. Precompile setup scripts (`safeEval`) from the start, for a strict CSP in the desktop build?

## Extension — 2026-11-03 19:08 — jq79 approved: decisions and plan for steps 1 and 2

### Decisions (user)

1. Migrate **gradually**, through shared stores.
2. Coverage floor for `helpers/`: **90%** of lines, branches and functions.
3. Setup scripts in **TypeScript** (`lang="ts"`).
4. **Precompile** setup scripts (`safeEval`) from the start.

The layer rules go into `AGENTS.md` now, and the roadmap is rewritten for the new order.

### Step 1: the theme in jq79

- `npm install jq79` (0.7.7). `vite.config.ts` gets `jq79({ safeEval: true })` and
  `build.target: "es2022"`, which `safeEval` needs on Vite 5 for its top-level `await`.
- A `*.html` module declaration, so `.ts` files can import components.
- **What a component cannot style goes in one global sheet.** jq79's scoped styles stop at
  the component boundary, and slot content keeps the parent's stamp. A `<table>` or a
  `<select>` cannot take a `<slot>` either, because the HTML parser moves it out of the table
  and drops it from the select. So:
  - `theme/default/content.css` holds the classes plain markup uses: `.mm-legend`, `.mm-table`
    and its cells, and the display's rows (`.mm-display-row`, `-label`, `-title`). It replaces
    the React `Legend` and `Table` components.
  - `Select` takes its options as data and renders them with `:each`.
  - `Panel` stays a `<section>`. A view that needs a form wraps it in one.
- **Components** (`theme/default/components/*.html`, kebab-case file names as in luu, scoped
  styles, `lang="ts"`): `key`, `led`, `key-bank`, `key-select`, `slide-switch`, `display`,
  `counter`, `meter`, `lamp`, `reel`, `eq-bars`, `stripes`, `field`, `text-input`, `select`,
  `checkbox`, `tabs`, `panel`, `modal`, `list-row`. The interface follows jq79's habits:
  - every prop is declared, so the accessible name is a `label` prop;
  - values go up through `:model` and a `change` event;
  - a key re-emits `click`.
- **The demo** becomes `theme/default/demo.html` (a jq79 page) mounted by `demo.ts`. It covers
  the same ground as the React one.
- The React components, `demo.tsx` and the per-component `.css` files are deleted.
  `src/` does not change.

### Step 2: layers and tests

- `src/helpers/`: the pure functions from `Mp3ManagerView.tsx` (`computeCommonMetadata`,
  `getVariedFields`, `inferDiscLabel`, `trackNumberOrder`, `orderDetailPlaylist`,
  `collectFolderPaths`, `buildWizardRows`) plus `formatTime`. Each file gets a `.test.ts`
  beside it, and the view imports them from there, so its behaviour does not change.
- `npm test` runs `scripts/check-layers.mjs`, then `node --test` with type stripping and
  coverage, failing under 90% lines, branches or functions in `src/helpers/`.
- `tsconfig.json`: `allowImportingTsExtensions`, so helpers import each other as `./x.ts`
  (Node needs the extension, Vite accepts it).

## Extension — 2026-11-03 19:19 — Step 1 walkthrough: the theme in jq79, sized in rem

User, mid-step (translated): *"On mobile I usually set the html font-size in vw, so components
scale linearly."* Adopted in this step, since it is a theme-wide sizing rule (see below).

- **jq79** `^0.7.7` installed. `vite.config.ts` uses `jq79({ safeEval: true })` and
  `build.target: "es2022"`. `src/html-modules.d.ts` declares `*.html` modules as `Component79`.
  The build emits one precompiled `*.html.jq79-[hash].js` per component, as the CSP mode
  expects.
- **Components**, rewritten as `theme/default/components/*.html` with `<script :setup lang="ts">`
  and scoped styles: `key`, `led`, `key-bank`, `key-select`, `slide-switch`, `display`,
  `counter`, `meter`, `lamp`, `reel`, `eq-bars`, `stripes`, `field`, `text-input`, `select`,
  `checkbox`, `tabs`, `panel`, `modal`, `list-row`. Each declares every prop it takes, and
  values go up through `:model` and an event.
- **`content.css`** replaces the React `Legend` and `Table`: `.mm-legend`, `.mm-table` (and
  `mm-num`, `mm-end`, `mm-title` cells), and the display's `.mm-display-row`, `-label` and
  `-title`, for plain markup a component cannot style.
- **Found on the way**: jq79 ignores `:value`, `:checked`, `:selected`, `:text`, `:html` and
  `:class` on a component tag, since they are its own directives. So Meter takes `level` and
  `levelText`, as luu's Progress takes `amount`, and ListRow takes `chosen`. The demo showed it
  as empty meters and unselected rows. `AGENTS.md` now lists the names to avoid.
- **Sizes in rem.** Type, control heights and radii are rem tokens, and every size of 3px or
  more in a component or in `content.css` became rem (base 16px). 1–2px borders, focus rings
  and `@media` conditions stay in px. `tokens.css` sets `html { font-size: var(--mm-root-phone) }`
  at ≤ 480px, with `--mm-root-phone: 4vw`, which is 16px on a 400px screen. Measured: on desktop
  and at 400px a key is 30px and a tree row 28px; at 360px they are 27px and 25.2px (×0.9), at
  430px 32.25px and 30.1px (×1.075). `AGENTS.md` records the rule.
- **Demo**: `theme/default/demo.html` (a jq79 page) mounted by `demo.ts`. It covers the same
  ground as the React one, and the page's base styles are in `index.html`.
- **Removed**: the React components, `demo.tsx`, `demo.css` and the per-component `.css` files.
  `src/` is unchanged apart from the module declaration.
- **Validation**: `npm run build` passes. In Chromium, against the built demo:
  - the transport plays and moves on, and the display follows;
  - KeySelect, the latching key and the switch report their values;
  - a two-digit year shows the error and disables Save, and a valid one enables it;
  - Save lights the Saved lamp, and the select changes;
  - a folder's caret collapses the tree (10 → 5 rows);
  - closing a tab leaves two, and the arrow keys move the open tab;
  - Escape and the close key close the modal, and the tone switch sets `data-theme`.

  No console messages at all. There is no horizontal overflow at 1280px, 430px, 400px or 360px.

Suggested commit message: `feat(theme): port Deck to jq79 components, sized in rem with a vw root on phones`

## Extension — 2026-11-03 19:25 — Step 2 walkthrough: layers, helpers and tests

- **Helpers** (`src/helpers/`, pure TypeScript, no packages, `.ts` imports between them):
  - `metadata.ts`: `emptyMetadata`, `computeCommonMetadata`, `getVariedFields`.
  - `tracks.ts`: `inferDiscLabel`, `trackNumberOrder`, `orderDetailPlaylist`,
    `collectFolderPaths`.
  - `tree.ts`: `buildWizardRows(tracks, expandedFolders)`. It used to take the whole
    `RootWorkspace` and read only its `expandedAlbums`, so the view passes that list now.
  - `snapshot.ts`: `emptySnapshot`, `parseMetaSnapshot`, `serializeMetaSnapshot`. The JSON
    half of `readMetaSnapshot` / `writeMetaSnapshot`; the VFS half stays in the view until
    step 3.
  - `format.ts`: `formatBytes` (from `TrackInfoModal`) and `formatTrackNumber` (from
    `TrackWizardList`).
- **Changed from the plan**: `formatTime` waits for the faceplate step, where its first caller
  lands. `formatBytes`, `formatTrackNumber` and the snapshot parsing moved instead: they are the
  pure functions the app actually has.
- **Behaviour that changed, both found by the tests**:
  - `orderDetailPlaylist`: two tracks without a track number compared as
    `Infinity - Infinity`, which is `NaN`. The sort reads `NaN` as "equal", so the file name
    never ordered them. Now it does.
  - `formatBytes` named no unit below 1 byte or from 1 TB up (`undefined`). It is now clamped to
    Bytes … GB.

  Everything else is moved, not changed.
- **Types** shared by helpers and components moved to `src/types.ts`: `WizardTreeRow` (from
  `TrackWizardList`, which re-exports it), `MetaRecord` and `MetaSnapshot` (from the view).
- **`src/services/ThemeService.ts` → `src/lib/ThemeService.ts`**, ahead of step 3: the layer
  check flagged the view importing from outside the layers, and a service belongs in `lib/`.
- **Tests**: 38, in `src/helpers/*.test.ts`, on `node:test` and `node:assert/strict`, with a
  shared `track()` builder in `fixtures.test-support.ts`. Every helper is at 100% of lines,
  branches and functions. `tsconfig.test.json` type-checks them with `@types/node` (a new dev
  dependency, types only). The main `tsconfig.json` excludes them and gains
  `allowImportingTsExtensions`.
- **`npm test`** = `test:layers` → `test:types` → `test:unit` → `test:coverage`.
- **Found while checking the gates, and fixed**: Node's `--test-coverage-*` thresholds apply to
  the total, and only to files a test loaded. A 75%-covered helper beside well-covered ones
  passed, and so did a helper no test imported. `scripts/check-coverage.mjs` reads the LCOV
  report the runner now also writes (`.tmp/coverage/lcov.info`). It fails on any helper below
  90% in any metric, and on any helper missing from the report. Both cases were run and fail.
- **`scripts/check-layers.mjs`** walks `src/` and `theme/default/`. It applies the table in
  `AGENTS.md`, ignores `import type`, lets tests import `node:` built-ins, and lets components
  import only the frameworks (`react`, `jq79`). A `react` import added to a helper fails it.
- **Validation**: `npm test` and `npm run build` pass. The React app loads in Chromium with no
  console output.

Next: step 3, stores and i18n.

Suggested commit message: `feat(helpers): extract pure helpers with tests, enforce layers and per-file coverage`

## Extension — 2026-11-03 19:33 — Step 3 plan: stores and i18n

User: *"yes, go on with step 3"*. And, mid-step: *"by the way, I think the project is going to be
renamed: jackbird"*. Noted, not acted on: the name is not final. This step keeps the rename
cheap, since the app's name becomes one translation key (`app.name`) and the storage keys live in
one module. Some names hold user data, and renaming them needs a migration, not a find-and-replace:
`.meta/mp3-manager.json` in every opened folder, the `mp3-manager` IndexedDB database, and the
`mp3-manager.*` localStorage keys. The `--mm-` token prefix is cosmetic.

### Stores (`src/lib/`, jq79 `$reactive`)

- `library.ts`: `{ roots, activeRootId, loading, saving, restoring, errorMessage, infoMessage }`.
- `playback.ts`: `{ queue, index, playing }`.
- `ui.ts`: `{ preferencesOpen, editModalOpen, infoModalOpen }`.
- `preferences.ts`: `{ theme, language }`. Each starts from storage, else from the system (the
  colour scheme; the browser's languages). Each is saved when chosen and applied to `<html>`
  (`data-theme`, `lang`), and the theme follows the system until one is chosen. It replaces
  `ThemeService.ts`.
- `storage.ts`: the localStorage keys and safe reads and writes, in one place.
- `react-store.ts`: `useStoreValue(store, key)` returns `[value, set]` for React through
  `useSyncExternalStore`. It reads with `$toRaw`, so React sees plain objects whose identity
  only changes when they are replaced, and never a proxy. It goes away with React.
- The view's `useState` calls become `useStoreValue(...)`. Its handlers stay as they are, and
  move into `lib/` as their screens become jq79 views (steps 4–6). `audioRef` stays a React ref.
- `RootWorkspace`, `GroupingMode`, `PlaybackQueue`, `Theme` and `Language` move to
  `src/types.ts`.

### i18n

- `src/helpers/i18n.ts`:
  - `translate(messages, fallback, key, params)`, with English as the fallback and `{name}`
    filling;
  - `pluralCategory(language, count)` (`Intl.PluralRules`), for keys that end in `.one` /
    `.other`;
  - `pickLanguage(preferred, supported, fallback)`.
  All three are tested.
- `src/lib/locales/en.ts`: every UI string, in English, by key. It is the source, and
  `MessageKey` is its keys. `src/lib/locales/es.ts` is typed `Record<MessageKey, string>`, so the
  compiler rejects a missing Spanish string. A test checks that each translation has the same
  `{params}` as its English source.
- The locales sit under `lib/` rather than `src/locales/`, as planned earlier, so the layer rules
  need no new category: `lib/i18n.ts` imports them.
- `src/lib/i18n.ts`: `t(key, params)` and `tn(key, count, params)`, which read
  `preferences.language`, and a `useT()` hook for React.
- React components cannot import `lib/`, so the view hands each one a `t` prop. Their type is an
  `import type` from `lib/i18n`, which the layer check allows. jq79 views will pass strings to the
  theme components as props, as the theme already expects.
- Preferences gains a language choice, each language named in itself ("English", "Español").
  `index.html` says `lang="en"`, and `<html lang>` and `document.title` follow the language.
- Display strings computed from data go through `t` as well. `AlbumOverview` titles a disc
  "Disc 2" and an untagged album "No album" in the chosen language. It groups by the raw values
  and uses the helpers (`inferDiscNumber`, `trackNumberOrder`) instead of its own copies.
  `inferDiscLabel` becomes `inferDiscNumber`, a number for ordering, with no text in it.
- The view's `detailScope` has a branch for `disc` rows, which `buildWizardRows` never produces
  since folders became the tree. It is dead and holds Spanish text, so it goes.

### Validation

- Unit tests for the new helpers, the locale check, `npm test` and `npm run build`.
- **End to end, before and after.** A temporary script (`.tmp/scripts/`) gives the React app a
  folder of generated MP3s with ID3 tags: OPFS, behind a stubbed `showDirectoryPicker`. It then:
  1. opens the folder and expands the tree;
  2. selects a track, edits its title and saves, checking `.meta/mp3-manager.json`;
  3. reloads, so the session is restored;
  4. toggles the theme.

  It runs on today's build for a baseline. After the change it runs in Spanish (`es-ES`), where
  the text must match the baseline apart from listed corrections, and in English.

## Extension — 2026-11-03 19:44 — Step 3 walkthrough: stores and i18n

- **Stores** (`src/lib/`, jq79 `$reactive`): `library`, `playback`, `ui` and `preferences`, as
  planned. Every `useState` of the view is now `useStoreValue(store, key)`
  (`src/lib/react-store.ts`): `useSyncExternalStore` over `$onAny`, reading through `$toRaw`,
  with a setter that takes a value or a function of the current one, as `setState` does. The
  handlers did not change. `audioRef` is still a React ref.
- **`preferences`** replaces `ThemeService.ts`, which is deleted. The theme behaves as before:
  saved, else the system's, following the system until chosen. The language is new: saved, else
  the first browser language that is English or Spanish, else English. Both are applied to
  `<html>` (`data-theme`, `lang`), and `document.title` follows `app.name`. The keys sit in
  `src/lib/storage.ts`.
- **i18n**:
  - `src/helpers/i18n.ts`: `fill`, `translate`, `pluralCategory`, `translatePlural` and
    `pickLanguage`, with 11 tests at 100%.
  - `src/lib/locales/en.ts` has 118 keys and is the source. `MessageKey` is its keys, and
    `PluralKey` the bases of its `.one` / `.other` pairs.
  - `es.ts` is `Record<MessageKey, string>`. `locales.test.ts` checks the keys, the placeholders,
    the plural pairs, and that each language names itself; it is the first test outside
    `helpers/`, so the runner's glob is now `src/**/*.test.ts`, while coverage still applies
    to `helpers/` only.
  - `src/lib/i18n.ts` gives `createTranslate`, `useT`, `t` and `languageName`. A `Translate` is
    `t(key, params)` with `t.n(key, count, params)` and `t.language`.

  component takes `t` as a prop; its type is an `import type`, which the layer check exempts.
  Preferences gains a Language section: a select of the languages, each named in itself
  (`language.self` in each catalogue, so no non-English text sits outside the Spanish file).

- **The Spanish texts are the old ones**, with two kinds of correction: missing accents
  ("librería", "música", "álbumes", "carátula", "aquí", "ningún", "está", "árbol",
  "selección", "patrón") and plurals ("1 pistas" → "1 pista", "1 albumes" → "1 álbum"), which
  the old code wrote by hand and got wrong for one.

  - `inferDiscLabel` ("Disco N") became `inferDiscNumber`, and the label is now
    `t("album.disc")`.
  - `AlbumOverview` dropped its private copies of the disc and track-number functions for the
    helpers. It groups by the raw tags and titles an untagged album with `album.untitled`.
  - `MetadataEditor` uses `getVariedFields`.
  - The view's `detailScope` branch for disc rows is gone: it was dead (no disc rows exist)
    and held Spanish text.
  - `ThemeToggle.tsx` was used nowhere and is deleted.
- **Rename to jackbird**: nothing renamed (see the plan above). The app's name is the one key

    pass.

  `<audio controls>` inside `PlaybackBar`. It holds:
  - `nowPlaying` (`$computed` from the queue, the index and the roots), and `playQueue`,
    `togglePlay`, `next`, `previous`, `stop`;

    time and progress.

  background verifier replaces them).
- The React view keeps the library, the album sheet and the dialogs (steps 5–6). Their handlers
  call these functions. `audioRef` goes.

The app's top bar becomes a Deck faceplate, built from theme components and reading the stores:

- **Row one**:
  - the brand: the logo and the wordmark;
  - the open-folder key ("Open library" / "Add root", busy while scanning);
  - the transport bank: previous, play/pause, next;
  - the display: state, track n/total, title — artist, time and a position meter;
  - a return-to-list key;
  - the Silver/Graphite slide switch;
  - the preferences key.
- **Row two**: the open roots as `Tabs`, closable.

`src/views/Jq79Island.tsx` mounts a jq79 view into an element React renders: a clone per
mount, destroyed on unmount. `FolderPicker`, `RootTabs` and `PlaybackBar` are deleted.

`src/main.tsx` loads the theme's `fonts.css`, `tokens.css` and `content.css`. This affects the
React screens too. Plex, which `styles.css` always named, now actually loads. On phones
(≤ 480px) the 4vw root also scales the legacy styles' rem sizes.

### Validation

- `npm test` and `npm run build`.
- The end-to-end script, adapted to the faceplate, in both languages. The test library's tracks
  get a few seconds of silent audio, so playback can be followed on the display.
- Screenshots on desktop and phone, in both tones.

Mid-step the user asked (translated) for two more things:

- *"On mobile I'd keep the player always on top, and the file tree and the details in panels
  that fill the screen (except the player), that you swipe to switch from one to the other"*.

### What was built

- **Helpers**, each with tests, 100% covered:
  - `selection.ts`: `nextSelection`. Only `toggle` and `range` are ported: the old code's third
    branch could not be reached, since every caller passed one or the other.
  - `scope.ts`: `detailScope`.
  - `tree.ts`: `treeKeyAction`. With no current row, any arrow selects the first; the old code
    jumped to the second.
  - `albums.ts`: `groupAlbums`. Discs are now ordered by number; they used to come in the order
    they were met.

  78 tests in all.
- **`lib/workspace.ts`** holds the derived values (`activeRoot`, `treeRows`, `detail`,
  `detailAlbums`, `selectedTracks`) and the actions:
  - selection and the tree: `selectTrack`, `selectTreeRow`, `navigateTree`, `clearSelection`,
    `toggleFolder`, `expandAllFolders`, `collapseAllFolders`;
  - the rest: `removeSelectedTracks`, `playFromDetail`, `consolidateTrack`, `openEditor`,
    `showInfo`.
- **Views**:
  - `library.html`: the head with expand and collapse keys; notices; the scan meter; a
    `role="tree"` of `ListRow`s with arrow keys and `aria-activedescendant`; the rename
    placeholder in each track's action slot.
  - `album-sheet.html`: the landing with the logo; the action bar (confirmation before
    delete); albums with cover or striped placeholder, tags, and per-disc `.mm-table`s with
    play/pause keys and bars on the playing row; the prompt.
  - `workspace.html`: the two panes.
- **`ListRow`** gained `lead`, `current`, `rowId` and an `action` slot.
- **Deleted**: `TrackWizardList`, `AlbumOverview`, `DetailActionBar` and `LibraryLanding`, and
  the dead disc-row handling. The React view is down to 371 lines; it was 1599 when this work
  began.
- **Escape clears the selection** when no dialog is open. The 2026-08-11 record describes this
  shortcut, but no commit ever contained it; it is in the workspace now.

### Found on the way

1. **A reactive loop in jq79's `$computed`.** The first end-to-end run logged about 100
   "effect re-woke itself 100 times" errors per library write, and some views stopped
   updating. Reduced in Node with jq79's store alone: a `$computed` that reads another
   `$computed`'s object value wakes itself again. A computed reading the source store, even
   through a plain function, does not.

   The fix:
   - `derive()` works out the active root, its rows and the scope once per change of
     `library.roots` / `library.activeRootId` (memoised on their identity), and every
     computed calls it;
   - object values are boxed (`Opaque`, a class instance, which jq79 leaves untouched);
   - views read them through functions rather than `$:` copies.

   Zero errors since. Worth reporting upstream to jq79.
2. **Class names shared with the legacy stylesheet.** `.library-pane` in `styles.css` (fixed
   height, `overflow: hidden`) cut the phone tree. A scan found 7 class names that jq79 views
   and theme components shared with legacy selectors (`brand`, `empty`, `error`, `field`,
   `library-pane`, `message`, `tag`); all are renamed. Scoped styles do not keep a global
   stylesheet out. Both pitfalls are in `AGENTS.md`.

### Phones (user request)

- The shell is a full-height column on phones too, so the faceplate stays on top: it does not
  move when a pane scrolls, and the page itself never scrolls.
- Below it, the two panes sit in a row with `scroll-snap`, one screen wide each, and a swipe
  moves between them (native scrolling, no script for the gesture).
- A small `KeySelect` above the panes shows which one is in view (from the scroll position)
  and jumps to the other; the jump is not animated under reduced motion.
- The faceplate is shorter on phones (≤ 640px): logo and actions share a row, the wordmark and
  the switch's legends go (its accessible name stays), and the transport stays one row.
- Measured at 400×800: faceplate 182px, panes 400×571. At 360×740: 164px, panes 360×534. A
  touch swipe snaps to Details and the selector follows; the selector's Library key snaps back.
  No overflow and no console output at either size.

### Validation

- `npm test` passes: layers on 75 files; 78 tests; coverage, 9 helpers at 100%. So does
  `npm run build`.
- `.tmp/scripts/e2e-library.cjs`, in `es-ES` and `en-US`:
  - 3 rows, 14 after expand all, the same texts as the baseline;
  - click selects one, and Shift-click selects three ("3 PISTAS SELECCIONADAS"); Escape clears;
  - arrows go Pop → Bjork, Left closes Bjork (14 → 9 rows), Right opens it (14);
  - playing from the sheet lights the row and the faceplate, and the same key pauses;
  - edit and save leave `.meta` equal to the baseline;
  - delete after the confirmation leaves 12 rows and "2 tracks removed from the library.";
  - collapse all leaves 3 rows.

  No console errors.
- `.tmp/scripts/e2e-faceplate.cjs` (selectors updated) passes; so does
  `.tmp/scripts/e2e-mobile.cjs`.

### What is left of React, and the user's question

About 1 060 lines: `Mp3ManagerView.tsx` (371: session restore and save, the background
verifier, saving the editor), five dialog components (~600: `MetadataEditModal`,
`MetadataEditor`, `CoverDropzone`, `TrackInfoModal`, `PreferencesModal`), and the glue
(`App`, `main`, `Jq79Island`, `react-store`). On top of that are the 1 758 lines of
`styles.css`, nearly all unused now.

Agreed with the user that it is now worth doing in one block. The proposal for the next step
replaces steps 6 and 7 with one step:
- dialogs as jq79 views;
- session, verifier and saving into `lib/`;
- a jq79 root instead of React;
- removing React, `react-store`, `Jq79Island` and `styles.css`.

Suggested commit message: `feat(library): library tree and album sheet as jq79 views, swipeable panes on phones`

### Known issue, carried over unchanged

The verifier keeps the `.meta` snapshot object it started with. A save during a run replaces
the root's snapshot; the verifier's next step puts its own back and later writes it to
`.meta`. The edited tracks then go back to their old entries there. If one of them was still
on the verifier's list, its entry gets the new checksum with the old tags, and the next load
would trust it. This only happens when a save lands during a run, and a run only has work
when records are a day old. Proposal for a later step: at each track, the verifier reads the
root's current snapshot and track, and skips a track whose checksum changed since the run
started.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 01:32 — Fixing the verifier: plan

User: *"fix the verifier bug"*, the known issue at the end of the 00:19 extension.

### What goes wrong, exactly

Two paths end with the same bad `.meta` record: a track's **new** checksum paired with its
**old** tags. The record's size and date no longer match the file, so the next load re-reads
the file. The checksum then matches the record, so the load takes the record's tags, which
are the old ones.

1. **A save during a run.** The verifier works from the snapshot object and the track objects
   it captured when the run started. A save replaces the root's snapshot. The verifier's next
   step puts its own snapshot back (in place, mutating it) and later writes it to `.meta`,
   which undoes the save's entries. If an edited track is still on the run's list, the
   verifier reads its new bytes but records them with the tags captured at the start.
   Meanwhile, the save had copied the snapshot when it started, so verifications made during
   the save are lost from `.meta` as well. That second loss is harmless; they are re-verified
   later.
2. **A file changed outside the app.** When the checksum the verifier computes differs from
   the library's, it records it as verified, still with the library's tags.

### The fix

- `helpers/snapshot.ts`, tested:
  - `checksumOf(track)`;
  - `applyVerification(root, trackId, readFrom, found, nowIso)`, which gives the root after
    one verification, without mutating anything. The track and its record take the checksums
    and the time, and keep the track's **current** tags, size and date. If `found.file` says
    the file changed (its tags, size and date as read now), they take those instead.

    It gives `null` when the result no longer applies: the track is gone, or its checksum is
    no longer `readFrom`, the one it had when its file was read (a save or a rescan recorded
    the file since).
- `lib/meta.ts`: `flushMeta(rootId)` writes the root's snapshot **as it is when the write
  starts**. Writes for a root are queued, so two never overlap and the last one written is
  the latest state. The save and the verifier both write through it.
- `lib/verifier.ts`:
  - It keeps only the root's id and the list of track ids. At each track it reads the current
    root and track.
  - It waits while a save is under way.
  - If the computed checksum differs from the track's, it reads the tags from the same bytes
    and the date from `vfs.stat`.
  - It drops the result if a save started meanwhile, the run was cancelled, or
    `applyVerification` says it no longer applies.
  - It never mutates the snapshot, and writes through `flushMeta`.
- `lib/editor.ts`:
  - It merges its records into the root's **current** snapshot when it finishes, not the
    copy taken when it started.
  - It updates the library first, then writes `.meta` through `flushMeta`. The files are
    already written by then, so the library should show them even if `.meta` fails. The
    error is still reported, and the dialog stays open.

### Validation

- `npm test`, with the new helper tests.
- A new end-to-end check, `.tmp/scripts/e2e-verifier-race.cjs`, run first on the current build
  to show the failure, then on the fix:
  1. age every `.meta` record so that the verifier has work;
  2. slow `crypto.subtle.digest` once the folder is open, so the run lasts long enough;
  3. reopen the folder, and save a new title on the last track on the run's list while the run
     is under way;
  4. after the run, close and reopen the folder.

  The tree must show the new title, and the track's `.meta` record must have the new title
  with the file's checksum.
- A second case: change a file's tags behind the app's back (rewrite its bytes in OPFS), age
  its record, reopen. After the run, its record carries the new tags with the new checksum.
- The earlier end-to-end scripts (library, background) still pass.

### Not in this fix (noted)

- The initial scan (`hydrateRoot`) also keeps its own snapshot object and writes `.meta`
  directly, so a save made while a folder is still being scanned has the same race. A rescan
  cancels the verifier, so the verifier is not involved. The same tools (`flushMeta`, merging
  into the current snapshot) would fix it, as a separate step.
- A save records `Date.now()` as the file's date instead of the file's own, so every edited
  file is re-read once on the next load. It is harmless, since the record's tags are right.

---

## Extension — 2026-11-04 01:42 — Fixing the verifier: walkthrough

Done as planned in the 01:32 extension, with one addition (the retry, below).

### Reproduced first

`.tmp/scripts/e2e-verifier-race.cjs` on the build before the fix (`da2d766`), with the target
`Pop/Bjork/Homogenic/CD2/01 - Joga live.mp3` ("Jóga (live)"), the last on the run's list:

- `race`: a new title saved 5.6 s after the reopen, mid-run. After the run, the record had
  the old title with the new file's checksum, and the reopened folder showed "Jóga (live)".
  The save was lost.
- `external`: the target's bytes replaced by another track's ("So What") 0.9 s after the
  reopen. After the run, the record had "Jóga (live)" with the new checksum, and the reopened
  folder still showed "Jóga (live)".

### What changed

- `helpers/snapshot.ts`: `checksumOf`, `Verification` and `applyVerification`, as planned.
  The 7 new tests cover:
  - checksums and time recorded with the tags kept;
  - nothing else touched and nothing mutated;
  - the file's tags, size and date taken when it changed;
  - a record created when missing;
  - `null` for a track that is gone, or one recorded again since its file was read.
- `lib/meta.ts`: `flushMeta(rootId)`, as planned. Writes are chained per root and write the
  library's snapshot as it is when each one starts. A failed write does not block the next,
  and a root's entry is dropped once its last write is done.
- `lib/verifier.ts`:
  - It keeps the root's id and the list of track ids.
  - At each track it waits out a save, then takes the root and track as they are now, reads
    the file and computes its checksums. If they differ from the library's, it reads the tags
    from the same bytes and the date with `vfs.stat`.
  - It records through `applyVerification`, unless the run was cancelled or a save is under
    way.
  - It writes through `flushMeta` and no longer mutates any snapshot.
  - *Added during the work:* if a save started while a track's file was being read, the
    verifier reads that track again once the save is done (up to 3 times), instead of
    dropping it until the next run. Without this, a save made mid-run left one track
    unverified (5 of 6 records fresh, in every timing tried).
- `lib/editor.ts`: the records go into the root's snapshot as it is when the save ends, the
  library is updated first, and `.meta/` is written through `flushMeta`. The dialog closes
  only when that write worked too.
- `STACK.md` describes the verifier and `flushMeta`.

### Validation

- `npm test` passes: 101 tests; coverage, 10 helpers at 100%. So does `npm run build`.
- `.tmp/scripts/e2e-verifier-race.cjs race`, with the save at 3.9, 5.0, 7.5, 9.3 and 10.2 s
  into a run that reaches the target at about 10 s. Every time:
  - the record has "Changed During Run" with the file's checksum;
  - the reopened folder shows it;
  - all 6 records are freshly verified;
  - no console output.
- `.tmp/scripts/e2e-verifier-race.cjs external`: the record and the reopened folder show
  "So What", with the file's checksum; all 6 fresh.
- `.tmp/scripts/e2e-library.cjs` (both languages): the same as before the fix, apart from the
  "Last verified" time. `.tmp/scripts/e2e-faceplate.cjs`: identical.
  `.tmp/scripts/e2e-background.cjs`: the verifier re-verifies all 6 aged records.

### Still open (from the plan)

- The initial scan has the same race with a save made while a folder is still being scanned.
  It can move to `flushMeta` and merging into the current snapshot as a separate step.
- A save records `Date.now()` as the file's date, so each edited file is re-read once on the
  next load (harmless).

Suggested commit message: `fix(verifier): never record over a save or pair a new checksum with old tags`

## 2026-11-12 21:00 — The display: a taller spectrum, "00" all dimmed

  leading zeros but the last digit, and every digit when all are zeros, so "00" is all dimmed.
- Reported with them: "the spectrum does not work". In the browser it does (checked playing a
  generated tone: the bands move). On Android it cannot yet: since E6b the phone's native player
  (Media3) plays every track and `readSpectrum()` returns null for it, as the page never sees its
  samples. Giving it one needs the player to tap its audio (an `AudioProcessor`) and hand the
  bins to the page; not done here.

## 2026-11-12 21:30 — The spectrum moves in every band

Reported: the bass looked saturated and barely moved. Both analysers (the page's `AnalyserNode`
and `crates/audio`'s, which copies it) scaled -100 to -30 dB to 0–255: music's bass sits above
-30 dB, so it was pinned at the top, and the treble, quieter by nature, barely lit.

- **Headroom.** Both now span -100 to -10 dB (`SPECTRUM_DECIBELS` in `src/helpers/spectrum.ts`;
  `minDecibels`/`maxDecibels` on the page's analyser; `MAX_DB` in `crates/audio/src/spectrum.rs`).
- **Tilt.** Music, like pink noise, loses about 3 dB an octave. `spectrumBands()` takes a `tilt`
  (dB per octave, `SPECTRUM_TILT` = 3) and lifts each band by it from the range's middle: the bass
  lowered, the treble raised, so every band moves around the same height. A silent band stays
  dark. Tested: bins falling 3 dB an octave come out level (within 0.08).

## 2026-11-12 23:20 — The format with its quality: bits and kHz

The owner expected the format together with its quality (bits and kHz); in the browser the
display showed only "MP3", as nothing told it more.

- **`src/helpers/stream-info.ts`** (tested, fixtures and built frames): `streamInfo()` reads a
  track's audio from the start of its file, past an ID3v2 tag (its footer too): a FLAC's
  STREAMINFO (rate, depth, channels), or an MP3's first layer III frame (rate, bitrate, channels;
  MPEG-1, 2 and 2.5), the frame taken only where the next one follows it, and a VBR file's mean
  bitrate from its Xing or VBRI header. Checked against ffprobe on generated files (CBR 320, VBR,
  mono 22.05 kHz, FLAC 16/44.1 and 24/96, an ID3 in front of a FLAC, a 3 MB cover in front of an
  MP3). `streamQuality()` writes "24 bit · 96 kHz", "320 kbps · 44.1 kHz", "VBR 245 kbps · 48 kHz";
  `kilohertz()` keeps a rate's decimals ("22.05").
- **`src/lib/player.ts`**: on each `setSource`, `learnStream()` streams the start of the track's
  URL (a local file's blob or the service's HTTP alike), as much as the parser needs (up to
  16 MB, for a tag with a large cover), cancels the rest, and keeps it in `stream` ({ key, info }).
  Where the URL cannot be read (a phone's `file://`) it stays null.
- **`soundingParts()`** now gives three parts: the codec, the quality (the file's own, else what a
  native output decodes: "24 bit · 96 kHz"), and in a native output the route ("bit-perfect",
  "DAC"…). The display shows the codec in large dots, the quality's two halves one under the other
  ("24 bit" over "96 kHz", units in their own case), and the route dimmer under them.

## 2026-11-13 06:30 — The compact mode: the plan (C1, C1b, C2, C3)

The owner's ask of 2026-11-12, planned in `ROADMAP/2026-11-13/desktop-compact-mode.md`; on
2026-11-13 the owner added Picture-in-Picture for the web (C1b) and approved the plan. How it is
built:

- **One display, two places (C1).** The faceplate's display (the mark at the left that turns
  between format, spectrum and DAC; the title and the artist; the clock; the meter) moves out of
  `faceplate.html` into a view of its own, `src/views/deck-display.html`, with its logic; the
  faceplate and the compact view both place it, so every later change to it reaches both. Its
  spectrum's bands come as a prop (the faceplate's 16 or 6 by the page's width; the compact view's
  by its own), since the compact view may sit in another window, whose width is not the page's.
- **The compact view**, `src/views/compact.html`: the chassis (the faceplate's brushed metal),
  the display, the transport keys (`DeckKeys` with `place="compact"`: previous, play, next) and
  the keys of the mode (back to the full view; on the desktop, "on top"). Under the display, where
  the window is tall enough (a container query on its height), the queue's next track. It fills
  whatever holds it; its own sizes are rem, so it scales as the rest.
- **Where it shows** (`src/lib/compact.ts`, the store and the actions; `ui` is not touched):
  - the desktop app: the window itself turns compact (C2);
  - a browser with `documentPictureInPicture`: the page stays as it is, and the view goes to a
    floating window (C1b);
  - any other browser: the page itself turns compact (`compact.mode === "page"`), for a narrow
    window.
  `app.html` shows the compact view in place of the faceplate and the workspace in the first and
  the last case; in the second it keeps both, and the view lives in a slot moved to the floating
  window. A key on the faceplate's keys (`DeckKeys`, in the header only) turns it, labelled by
  where it goes.
- **Picture-in-Picture (C1b).** `documentPictureInPicture.requestWindow()` from the key's click,
  at 30rem × 8rem in the page's pixels. The slot (a plain `div` that holds the compact view's
  `:if`) moves into the floating window's body, so jq79's markers move with it and its bindings
  and listeners carry on (one document's nodes, adopted by the other, the same script). Styles:
  jq79 puts its components' scoped styles in `document.adoptedStyleSheets`, constructed sheets that
  another document cannot adopt, so their rules are copied as text into `<style>` elements there,
  with the page's own `<style>` and `<link rel="stylesheet">`; and checked again every half second
  while it is open, as a component first drawn after the copy (a DisplayIcon) brings its sheet
  late. `<html>`'s `data-theme` and `lang` are copied and followed. On `pagehide` the slot goes back
  where it was and the mode ends. The audio stays in the page: nothing to hand over.
- **The window (C2)**, through `@tauri-apps/api/window` (already a dependency): before turning
  compact, the full window's place and size (and whether it was maximized) are kept; then the least
  size goes down (`setMinSize`), the size to the compact one (30rem × 7rem, by the root's font size),
  no decorations, and on top if chosen; back, the reverse, to the place and size it had. The deck's
  chassis drags the window: a press on it that is not on a key, a button or the meter starts
  `startDragging()` (no `data-tauri-drag-region`, whose double-click maximizes). The capability
  `default.json` allows the window calls it uses, each by name (`core:window:allow-set-size`,
  `-set-min-size`, `-set-position`, `-set-decorations`, `-set-always-on-top`, `-start-dragging`,
  `-maximize`, `-unmaximize`).
- **Kept, and the keyboard (C3).** In `localStorage` (`jackbird.compact`): whether the app was
  compact, its compact place and size, "on top", and the full window's place and size. The desktop
  app starts compact if it was closed so (the window opens at its full size and turns within its
  first frames; making the window in Rust at the compact size is left for later, if the flash
  shows). Ctrl+Shift+M (Cmd+Shift+M on macOS) turns it both ways, in the page and in the floating
  window. The media keys need nothing: they reach the page, which keeps playing.
- **Tests**: what is pure (the sizes from the root's font size, the saved state's parsing, the
  shortcut's match) goes to `src/helpers/compact.ts` with its tests. The views are checked in
  Chromium: the page mode, and Picture-in-Picture (Playwright's Chromium has the API; headless it
  may refuse the window, then checked by hand). The window's part cannot be run here (no display
  for WebKitGTK); `cargo check` and the capability's schema, and the desktop CI, check it builds.

As planned at 06:30, with these differences found on the way:

- **Sizes**: at 30rem the display's title showed seven letters ("JACKET…"), so the compact window is
  **34rem × 7rem** (612 × 126 at the 18px root) and its least 20rem × 6rem; the floating window is
  34rem × **8.75rem**, room for the next track. The next track's container query is on the
  chassis' content box (`min-height: 6.75rem`), which leaves out its padding: the floating window
  shows it, the desktop window at 7rem does not until it is made taller.

  keeps its place and its scroll.

  (`src/lib/sources/check.ts`, `checkCompact()`) and `scripts/desktop-check.mjs` asks that, while
  compact, the window is bare and smaller (under 200 logical pixels tall), and after, back to its
  size with its title bar. It runs on each system in `desktop.yml`, which this session cannot.

What is where:

  (`bands` a prop); `faceplate.html` places it with 16 or 6 bands as before.

  (the transport alone) and, in the header, the compact key (a window with a picture in its

- `src/views/app.html`: the compact player in place of the faceplate and the workspace, and the
  slot (`#compact-slot`). `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`: the window calls, by name.
- Texts `compact.*` in English and Spanish; storage key `jackbird.compact`.

- **The page** (`documentPictureInPicture` removed): the key reads "Compact view
  (Ctrl+Shift+M)"; the page turns into the player, the library hidden; at 612 × 160 the next
  track shows; pause and next work from its keys; Ctrl+Shift+M turns it back.
- **Picture-in-Picture** (headless Chromium opens it): the floating window opens with the view and
  46 copied styles, drawn as in the page; its pause key pauses the page's music (the faceplate's
  key turns to Play); "Back to the library" closes it and the slot is home, empty.

  not shown (the header's keys are not).

## 2026-11-13 08:20 — The compact window comes back short on macOS

`desktop.yml` ([run 43179234665](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43179234665))
passed on Linux and Windows. It failed on macOS: the window turned compact (612 × 126, bare) and
came back 1024 × 645 where it had been 1024 × 674, 29 pixels short, the title bar's height.

macOS lays out a title bar turned on after the call that asks for it. The size set right after was
measured against the bare frame, so the bar came out of the content.

`src/lib/compact.ts`'s `sizeTo()` now sets the inner size, waits a moment, reads it back, and
sets it again (up to three times) until it is the one asked for. It is used for the full window's
return and for a kept compact size.

Commit: `fix(compact): the window comes back from compact at its size on macOS`

---

## 2026-11-13 08:40 — macOS sizes a window from its bottom edge

08:20's fix did not hold
([run 43186792251](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43186792251)): the window
still came back 1024 × 645 instead of 1024 × 674. Setting the size again did not help, so the cause
was not a late title bar.

The cause is the direction of the resize. On macOS a window is sized from its bottom edge: the
bottom stays put and the top moves.

- Growing back from 126 to 674 pushed the window's top up past the screen's.
- macOS cut the window short to keep it on screen, and moving it afterwards did not give the
  height back.
- The runner's screen is small: the 1280 × 800 window already opens at 1024 × 674.

In `src/lib/compact.ts`:

- `placeAndSize()`, which replaces `sizeTo()`: on macOS it first moves the window down by as much
  as it will grow, so it grows up into place. Then, everywhere, it sizes the window, puts it at its
  place, and checks the inside, up to three times. Elsewhere a window is sized from its top, so
  nothing changes there.
- Turning compact, the window is put back at the full window's top left. On macOS it would
  otherwise land where the full window's bottom was.

Commit: `fix(compact): on macOS the window grows up into place when it comes back from compact`

---

## 2026-11-13 09:00 — The title bar comes late on macOS

08:40's fix did not hold either
([job 131082086236](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43188646850/job/131082086236)):
still 1024 × 645. Three different orders of moving and sizing all gave exactly the title bar's 29
pixels less.

So the size was being set against the bare frame, and the bar was taken out of the content later.
Tauri (tao) turns a macOS window's title bar on asynchronously. The inside read right after sizing
was still the bare one's, so the check of 08:20 passed too early, and the bar came afterwards.

In `src/lib/compact.ts`:

- `windowFull()` waits 200 ms after turning the title bar on.
- `placeAndSize()` sizes the window, places it, waits, and reads the inside. It is done only when
  the inside is right on two reads 300 ms apart. It tries up to four rounds, waiting 100, 300, 600
  and then 1000 ms.
- It still grows the window up into place on macOS (08:40).

Commit: `fix(compact): wait for macOS's title bar before trusting the window's size`

---

## 2026-11-13 09:20 — Is it the screen? The check measures it

09:00's fix gave the same 1024 × 645
([job 131084465786](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43189363409/job/131084465786)).
Four rounds of sizing, the longest waiting a second, all ended at 645. So timing is not the cause.

The likeliest cause left is the screen:

- The runner's screen is small: a window asked to be 1280 × 800 opens at 1024 × 674 inside, about
  703 tall with its title bar.
- macOS lets a window open past the screen's work area (the screen less the menu bar and the
  Dock), but does not size one past it afterwards.
- So back from compact, the window is as tall as the work area allows: 645 inside, about 674 in
  all.

On a screen with room, the window comes back at its size, as it does on Linux and Windows.

This is no longer guessed:

- **The check** (`checkCompact()`) also reports, before, during and after, the whole window's
  height (`outerHeight`) and the work area's (`workHeight`, from `currentMonitor().workArea`), all
  in logical pixels.
- **`desktop-check.mjs`** takes the window as back at its size in either case:
  - its inside is the one it had; or
  - it had opened taller than the work area, and now it fills the work area's height exactly, at
    the same width.

  If the work area's numbers do not bear this out, the check still fails, now showing them.
- **`placeAndSize()`** stops when it reads the same wrong size twice: the screen will not give
  more, and more rounds would only wait longer.

Commit: `fix(compact): a window taller than the screen comes back as tall as its work area; the check reports it`

---

## 2026-11-13 09:35 — The measured cause: the inside is not the same measure on macOS

09:20's report
([job 131087598830](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43190298832/job/131087598830))
gave the numbers, and they disprove the screen idea:

|        | inside | whole window | work area |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| before | 1024 × 674 | 1024 × 674 | 677 |
| after  | 1024 × 645 | 1024 × 677 | 677 |

- Before, the window fit: 674 whole in a 677 work area. Its inside was reported as the whole
  window.
- After the title bar was turned off and on, macOS counts a 32-pixel bar outside the inside.
- Asked to make the inside 674 again, the window would have been 706 tall, more than the work
  area. macOS stopped it at 677, which left 645 inside.

The mistake was keeping and restoring the inside. It is not the same measure before and after the
title bar comes and goes. What a person sees is the whole window, so that is what is now kept and
restored:

- **`boundsOf()`** keeps the whole size (`outerSize`), for the full window and for the compact
  one; `Bounds` says so.
- **`placeAndSize()`** gives the window a whole size. Tauri sizes a window by its inside, so each
  round asks for the whole size less what the frame takes at that moment. The frame is read again
  each round, since the bar comes back asynchronously. The checks are on the whole size: right on
  two reads, or stop at the same wrong size twice.
- **`desktop-check.mjs`** compares the whole window before and after, strictly, within 2 pixels.
  09:20's allowance for a window cut to the work area is gone: it was not the cause, and the
  strict comparison now holds on every system. The report keeps `outerWidth`, `outerHeight` and
  `workHeight`.

Commit: `fix(compact): keep and restore the whole window's size, not its inside; the check compares it strictly`

---

## 2026-11-13 09:45 — Linux under Xvfb reports no frame at first

`d070001` broke the Linux check
([job 131090456121](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43191135466/job/131090456121)).
Under `xvfb-run` there is no window manager, and at the start `outerSize()` reports 0 × 0 (the
inside reported 1280 × 800). So the kept "whole size" was 0 × 0, and the window came back at its
least size (612 × 480, after the compact width).

Fixed in `src/lib/compact.ts`:

- `wholeSize()` gives the whole size where the system reports one, else the inside, which with no
  frame is the whole window.
- `boundsOf()` and `placeAndSize()` use it.

The check's `outerWidth` and `outerHeight` fall back the same way.

Commit: `fix(compact): where no frame is reported (X with no window manager), the inside is the whole window`

  `Meter` component takes `pitch` (a segment's width in rem; a `ResizeObserver` counts how many
  fit, `segments` is the count until it is measured). The display uses `.625rem`: some 90
  segments on a 1280px faceplate (it was 40, each long), some 20 in the compact card.

## 2026-11-13 14:10 — The sample music opens at once and streams; the buffer on the meter; an empty start tidied; a marquee; the light wordmark's metal

The owner's asks of 2026-11-12, in the conversation (the approval):

- **The sample music opens at once** (`src/lib/sources/samples.ts`). It waited for its four
  tracks (17 MB) to be copied into the page's private storage, one after another, before showing
  anything. Now the folder opens as soon as it is asked for (the index is read at the start), with
  every track the index names; the copies run in the background, one after another (side by side
  they took the bandwidth a track played meanwhile needs: at 250 KB/s the stream did not start).
  The source wraps the browser folder's: `walk()` gives a track still on its way as an empty file,
  `inspect()` and `save()` wait for its copy, and **`openMedia()` streams a track not yet copied
  from its served URL** (the `<audio>` element reads it by ranges as it plays); a copied one plays
  from the copy. A file an interrupted copy left empty counts as missing. In `src/lib/roots.ts` a
  track's record takes its size and date from the inspection (the file as read), not from the walk.
- **The buffer on the display's meter**: `playback.buffered`, the second the buffer reaches
  (`src/helpers/buffered.ts` + tests: the end of the buffered range the position is in, the length
  when it reaches the end), from the element's `progress`, `timeupdate`, `durationchange` and
  `seeked`; a native player has none. `Meter` takes `ahead` and lights the segments past the level
  up to it at half strength; the display shows it only while the buffer is short of the length,
  so a local file (whole at once) shows nothing.
- **Nothing open: no roots' bar** (`.tabs.compact`): hidden unless a root is open, coming back,
  or there is something to import (`faceplate.html`); the welcome is the page then.
- **The welcome's last option had no room under it on a phone**: its body was stretched to the
  scrolling box's height and overflowed it, so the box's bottom padding sat above its end. The
  body grows with what it holds and holds the bottom space itself (`workspace.html`).
- **"Probar música de ejemplo"** (Spanish; the English stays "Try the sample music").
- **A marquee on the display while nothing is open** (`deck-display.html`): the title's line runs
  `faceplate.marquee` ("Welcome to jackbird · Your music, its tags in order · Choose a folder, or
  try the sample music"), the text twice in a row slid by one copy, its pace by its length; still
  and cut under reduced motion; read once by screen readers. Once a root is open, "Nothing
  playing" as before.
- **The wordmark's metal in the light tone**: dark at its edges with a light reflection across
  its middle (it was light with a dark band); the dark tone keeps its own.

Checked in Chromium: with the MP3s held 5 s, the four rows show in 0.15 s and a track plays from
the stream at 1.8 s; throttled to 1 MB/s, it plays and the meter shows the buffer ahead; the
welcome on a 375 × 480 phone scrolls to 25px under its last key; the marquee runs and gives way
once the samples open; the wordmark in both tones. `npm test` passes. The jq79 warnings about
`item` in the tabs, seen in the console, were there before these changes.

Commit: `feat(samples): open at once and stream; the buffer on the meter; an empty start tidied; a marquee; the light wordmark's metal`
