## 2026-11-13 17:40 — Plan: the album artist (awaiting the owner's approval)

The owner's question of 2026-11-12: should the tags have "Artist" and "Album artist"? They exist:
ID3v2's `TPE2` (named "Band/orchestra" by the standard, used as the album artist by iTunes,
foobar2000, Mp3tag and MusicBrainz Picard; `TP2` in v2.2) and the Vorbis comment `ALBUMARTIST`
(some programs write `ALBUM ARTIST`; read both, write `ALBUMARTIST`). The plan, across the
layers that carry the six fields today:

1. **The tag crates** (`crates/tags`): `Field::AlbumArtist`; MP3 reads `TPE2` (`TP2` in v2.2) and
   writes `TPE2`; FLAC/Ogg read `ALBUMARTIST` then `ALBUM ARTIST`, write `ALBUMARTIST` and drop
   the spaced one. The shared fixtures (`fixtures/tags/make.mjs`) get files with it and without.
2. **The wire** (`crates/tags-wasm` and `crates/api`, `src/helpers/tagwire.ts`): the field in the
   tags' JSON, optional on the way in (an older service or snapshot sends none).
3. **The family library** (`crates/store`: one's own version, the safety copies) and the
   service's OpenSubsonic answers (`albumArtist`, which its clients already know).
4. **The web** (`src/types.ts` `TrackMetadata.albumArtist`, `src/helpers/metadata.ts`, `.meta/`
   snapshots, which read an older record's missing field as empty): the editor's form gets
   "Album artist" beside "Artist" (Album to its own row with Year), in English and Spanish.
5. **Where it counts**: the albums are grouped by album and album artist when there is one
   (else the artist, as now), so a compilation is one album; Albums by artist goes by the album
   artist; the queue's "one disc" (`isOneAlbum`) goes by it too, before the folder fallback; the
   album sheet's heading shows the album artist when there is one.

Checks: the crates' tests (`cargo test --workspace`, clippy, fmt), the wasm build, `npm test`,
and the editor in Chromium (read, edit and save an MP3's and a FLAC's album artist).

## 2026-11-13 18:30 — CI: builds on pull requests; a push asks for one by its message

The owner's ask of 2026-11-12 (the approval): the CI's builds only on a pull request, and a way to
run a given one for testing by the commit's message.

- `audio`, `desktop`, `mobile`, `phone-release`, `service`, `ship`, `tags`: a push no longer runs
  them by its paths. Each job without `needs` is gated: it runs unless the event is a push whose
  last commit's message lacks `[ci <name>]` (`audio`, `desktop`, `mobile`, `phone`, `service`,
  `ship`, `tags`) and `[ci all]`. Asked for, it runs whatever paths the push touched. A job with
  `needs` follows the one it needs.
- Pull requests run them by their paths, as `desktop`, `service` and `tags` did; `audio`,
  `mobile` and `ship` get the same `pull_request` paths their push had. `phone-release` (the signed
  release build) is not run on pull requests: `mobile` builds the phone there; it runs from the
  release, by hand, or by `[ci phone]`.
- By hand (`workflow_dispatch`) and from the release (`workflow_call`, where the event is the
  release's) nothing changes. `build.yml` (pull requests and `main`) and `release`/`deploy` are as
  they were.
- A push still starts these workflows, so they show as skipped runs when not asked for (no
  runner time). Only the last commit of a push is read: put the tag in the one pushed last.

The YAML parses; the gates' expressions are GitHub's `contains()` on `head_commit.message`. Not
run here (no Actions): the first push with `[ci tags]` checks it.

Commit: `ci: builds on pull requests; a push runs one by "[ci <name>]" in its last commit's message`

## 2026-11-13 23:40 — Proposal: the tracks' durations (awaiting approval)

The owner's note of 2026-11-12: the tracks show no duration. They have none to show: a `Track`
carries no length; only the playing song's is known, from the player. Plan:

1. **Read it when a file is inspected**, in Rust, once, from the head the inspection already
   reads (crates/tags, pure, with tests on `fixtures/tags/`): FLAC from STREAMINFO (total samples
   ÷ rate); MP3 from the first frame's Xing/Info or VBRI header (frames × samples per frame ÷
   rate), else, constant bitrate, from the audio's bytes (the file less its ID3v2 and ID3v1) ÷ the
   bitrate. Unknown stays unknown, never guessed as 0.
2. `crates/core`'s `Inspection` and `crates/api`'s JSON carry `durationMs`; the web app's source
   gets it through `crates/tags-wasm`; `Track` and `.meta/mp3-manager.json` keep it (`duration`,
   in seconds).
3. A library scanned before this has no durations: a track without one is read again once, as a
   stale one is, in the background scan.
4. Shown as `m:ss` (`h:mm:ss` past the hour), mono and subtle, as the number is: in the album
   sheet, a column before the ▶ / bars; and in the library tree at the row's end (`meta`).

Commit (when done): `feat(library): the tracks' durations, read with their tags`

## 2026-11-12 19:00 UTC — The tree without carets; a phone's library one level at a time

The owner's orders of 2026-11-12:

1. "On desktop, to simplify the nesting, we take the caret out, and the folder's icon does its
   job."
2. "The library on mobile: no nesting, only a flat list of each level, and on entering a level the
   open levels pile up from the top down. For folders and groups (artists, albums, etc.) each row
   opens its content, with a key with an arrow at its right that opens it in detail. For tracks
   we always show the checkbox (on mobile there is no hover)."

**The row** (`theme/default/components/list-row.html`): the caret and its column are gone. A
folder's glyph (drawn open or closed, as before) is what opens and closes it, `toggle`, with the
room between it and the row's left edge; a click anywhere else on the row is `select`. Each level
stands 1rem in from the one that holds it (it was .875rem, with a box one step back under its
folder's glyph, which made up for the caret's empty column). `flat` and `caret-only` are gone
(there is no column to drop); `no-toggle` makes the glyph part of the row, for a list that opens
folders its own way.

**A phone's levels** (at 720px and below, as the panes take turns):

- `src/helpers/levels.ts` (tested): from the tree's own visible rows, `levelsOf()` gives the open
  folders from the top one down, one per level (the first open one of each level), and the rows
  the last of them holds; `openOnEntering()` and `openOnLeaving()` give the root's open folders
  after a folder is entered (it and the ones above it, no other of those shown) or an open one is
  left (the ones above it). The folders the list does not show, another view's groups kept in the
  same list, are left as they were. It is the same state as a computer's tree
  (`expandedAlbums`), so a window made narrow or wide keeps where it was.
- `src/lib/workspace.ts`: `treeLevels`, `enterLevel()` (the folder becomes the current row, so
  the album sheet is on it), `leaveLevel()` (the folder above becomes current; at the top, none:
  the whole root); `navigateTree()` takes the levels, so the arrows move over what a phone lists
  and open and close as a tap does.
- `src/views/library.html`: on a phone the open levels are rows stacked at the top of the list
  (open folders, on the sunken ground, staying under the head while the list scrolls), and under
  them the level's rows, none indented. A tap on a folder enters it; a tap on an open level
  leaves it, and the ones under it. Every folder's row, open or not, ends in its ❯, which opens it
  in Details (it was the whole row's tap), and its ⋮. A track's row is as it was: a tap plays, its
  box picks, and the box is always there where there is no hover (17:30 UTC above).

Checked in Chromium on a made-up library (three artists' folders, albums, a second disc's
folder, a loose track). A computer, 1300px: no carets, a folder opens from its glyph and by a
click on its row, levels 1rem apart. A phone (Pixel 7, touch): the top level, then Kestrel Lane,
Low Tide Radio and Disc 2 entered one after another, each stacked under the last and the list
only that level's rows; ❯ on Low Tide Radio opens its album in Details; back in the library, a
tap on Low Tide Radio's level leaves it and Disc 2, and one on Kestrel Lane goes back to the
top; a loose track plays on a tap and its box picks it. `npm test` passes (`levels.ts` 100%).

`docs/usage.md` (The library) says so.

Commit: `feat(library): the tree without carets, a folder's glyph opens it; on a phone one level at a time, the open ones stacked`

## 2026-11-12 20:30 UTC — The macOS app's slowness: the splitter and the loading; its scrollbars; the tracks' numbers centred

The owner's notes of 2026-11-12: "the native macOS app is rather slow, the data's loading as much
as the UI; dragging the library/details separator lags, while in Chrome it is perfect"; "can the
scrollbar in the macOS app not hide itself, as the browser's does?"; and, on a picture of a
phone's rows: "align the track numbers vertically".

Measured in Chromium on a made-up library of 1,200 tracks (40 artists, 120 albums) with the whole
root in the sheet, a change of the split's width costing about 75 ms of layout and style each
(WebKit, the macOS app's webview, feels it more). Found and changed:

- **The split was an inherited custom property** (`--split` on the panes): each change made every
  element under them, the 1,200 rows, work out its style again. `src/views/workspace.html` now
  writes the grid's columns and the separator's place on those two elements (`splitColumns()`,
  `splitterLeft()`); phones, whose panes are a flex row, are not touched by them. 75 ms → 1 to
  13 ms.
- **The drag forced a layout per move** (the root's size and the room read after each write): they
  are measured when the drag starts, and the width is written once a frame.
- **Albums and rows out of view are not laid out** (`content-visibility: auto`, with their last
  height kept: `src/views/album-sheet.html`'s albums, `list-row.html`'s rows).
- **The loading laid the library out again every ten files** read: on a large library that was
  most of its time. `src/lib/roots.ts` flushes what was read every 400 ms at most (and at the end).

Not measured on a Mac (none here): how much of the app's slowness these were. Tauri's calls
(a file's tags read in Rust, six at a time) are as they were.

**The scrollbars** (`theme/default/base.css`, `src/main.ts`): the app marks the macOS app's page
(`html.mac-app`); there, with a pointer, the bars are thin, and their thumb shows only while the
pointer is over what scrolls, as near as a page's styles come to the system's bars that hide. Not
seen working here: headless Chromium draws no scrollbars; to be checked on the Mac.

**The numbers** (`list-row.html`): a track's number, beside its box where there is no hover, is
centred on the row's height (it sat on the title's line). Checked in Chromium on a phone.

`npm test` passes.

Commit: `perf(workspace): the split written on the grid, a frame at a time; off-screen albums and rows not laid out; the loading flushed by time. The macOS app's scrollbars shown when pointed at; the tracks' numbers centred`

---

## 2026-11-12 21:00 UTC — jq79 0.7.12: `$batch` for the queue's writes

The owner's note of 2026-11-12, after drafts of four jq79 issues (writes woken one at a time; no
windowed list; scoped styles losing a `var()` shorthand; a directive-named prop ignored silently):
"fixed in v0.7.12". Checked in 0.7.12's source: `$batch`, `:window` (a windowed `:each`, fixed row
height in pixels, `$scrollTo`), scoping on the source text instead of the CSSOM, and a warning
for a prop named like a directive. No issue to file.

- `package.json`, `package-lock.json`: jq79 ^0.7.12.
- `src/lib/player.ts`: `playQueue()` and `apply()` write the queue and its index in one `$batch`;
  the `queueMicrotask` that made `nowPlaying` load once per change is gone (checked in Chromium:
  unbatched, a `$computed` of the queue's current item passes through the old index's item in the
  new queue; batched, it changes once, to the final one).
- `src/lib/mirror.ts`: the order and the status are applied in one `$batch` each; the order is
  sent once per change without its `queueMicrotask` (one message per change on the channel, where
  intermediate states used to go out).
- `AGENTS.md`: the jq79 notes say so.

Not taken up yet: `:window` for the library's tree and the album sheet. It needs rows of one fixed
height in pixels; the tree's rows are one line (folders) or two (tracks), in rem, and the sheet is
albums of different lengths. A list of one kind of row (the queue's, the songs view) could use it.
`scripts/check-styles.mjs` still checks the shorthands: harmless, and jackbird's styles keep to it.

Checked in Chromium: the landing page's two frames (play, a track from the phone, previous, pause,
both in step), the phone's levels. `npm test` passes.

Commit: `chore(deps): jq79 0.7.12; the queue's writes in one $batch, no hand-made coalescing`

## 2026-11-12 23:10 UTC — The Android icon whole; a themed icon for light and dark

The owner's notes of 2026-11-12, on a picture of the app's info on Android (the bird cut by the
round mask): "the app's icon comes out cropped" and "could the app's icon have a light/dark theme?".

- **Cropped**: Android masks the adaptive icon's 108dp foreground to a shape that keeps only a

## 2026-11-13 19:35 UTC — Safari, the display's lines, the compact window, and opening a folder fast

What was done in this session, in order (each its own commit on `claude/magical-archimedes-3rip1u`).

**The landing page in Safari.** The window's screenshots were lazy and had no size: Safari left
them unloaded at 0×0, so the stage (which sizes the embedded app's iframe) was the window bar's
height, and the app was squashed into a strip. Their width and height are written now, the phone's
too (`fix(landing): …`).

**The display's lines.** The welcome and its hint always ran as a marquee, the text twice, even
when they fit. Both lines now go through the title's measurement (an unseen copy against the room
the row leaves, the artist's less the clock): each scrolls only when it does not fit, a song's
artist too (`fix(display): …`).

**A folder in Safari and Firefox.** They have no File System Access. Where the browser has a folder
file input (`webkitdirectory`), the folder opens through it, read-only (`lib/sources/picked.ts`,
`helpers/picked.ts`): it plays and its tags are read; the menus and the selection dialog leave out
what changes files (`canChange()`, lib/editor.ts), a save is refused; `.meta/` and playlists stay
in memory; it is neither kept in the session nor among the recent folders. The welcome and the
dialog warn of it before the key, and say so once it is open; a browser with neither (a phone's)
shows a warning in place of the key (`feat(web): …`).

**The compact window (macOS first).** The on-top key lights its pin, no LED, as shuffle and repeat
do; kept on top, the window is on every desktop too (`setVisibleOnAllWorkspaces`, its permission
in the capability); on top by default (a saved state with no `onTop`, or none, is on); the turn
between full and compact is drawn unseen (the content hidden at once, the window resized, shown
again once drawn at its size: no squeezed full layout, no compact one blown up); the key back to
the full app is the compact key's picture opened, an arrow out.

### Opening a folder, fast

The owner: opening folders is slow in the macOS app; "the logic should be in memory"; "could it
be needless rerenders?". Measured here in the desktop app on Linux (WebKitGTK, software rendering,
`target/bench`, a check folder ending in `#bench` timed by a throwaway patch of `check.ts`), on a
library of 240 MP3s over 20 albums (covers of 45 to 300 KB) and on five copies of it (1,200):

| | before | covers once | now |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 240, first opening, tags shown | 172 s | 175 s | 1.1 s (checksums at 2.2 s) |
| 240, reopened | 7.9 s | 7.3 s | 2.2 s |
| `.meta/mp3-manager.json` | 29.4 MB | 174 KB | 174 KB |
| 1,200, first opening | did not finish in 400 s | | 4.3 s (checksums at 6.3 s) |

(Reopened, the session also reopens the folder: it is opened twice, in every column.)

1. **Each cover kept once.** Every track carried its album's picture as a data URL: twelve copies
   per album in memory, in every update of the library, in the page, over the IPC, and in `.meta/`,
   read whole at each opening. The library holds references now, `jackbird-cover:<name>`, the name
   made from the bytes (two FNV-1a hashes and the length, and the type's extension: `coverName` in
   helpers/cover.ts, `cover_name` in crates/api, the same test vectors). `lib/covers.ts` keeps each
   picture once, as bytes, draws it through a blob URL made when first shown (`coverSrc`), reads
   one only named from the folder's `.meta/covers/<name>`, and writes it there before the snapshot
   that names it (one that cannot be written stays in the snapshot whole). A source's results come
   with references (`withCovers`); a cover given by reference is written whole when it is not the
   track's own (crates/api's `edit_for` keeps a cover only when it is the same string). An older
   `.meta/` is taken by reference and written again so; a track whose picture is missing from
   `.meta/covers/` has its tags read again. The phone's lock screen takes a data URL, made from the
   bytes in memory (`coverDataUrlNow`).
2. **Tags first, checksums after.** `crates/core` has `inspect`'s two halves: `read_tags` (the head
   only) and `checksums` (the whole file, streamed). The library shows with the tags; the verifier
   works the missing checksums out in bulk at once (`applySums`, one pass), and leaves a file that
   changed since its tags were read to its one-at-a-time pass, which reads its tags again: a new
   checksum is never recorded with old tags.
3. **In bulk, in Rust.** `src-tauri/src/scan.rs`: `scan`, every file's tags at once on a few threads
   (2 to 8), sent in batches over a channel (every 150 ms or 256 files); each cover met for the
   first time written into `.meta/covers/` by Rust (sent in the batch only when it cannot be
   written), so no picture crosses the IPC; `checksums`, the same way, on up to 4 threads. The last
   batch says `done`: the interface waits for it, not for the command's answer, which can come
   first. `hydrateRoot` uses it where the source has it (`scanTags`): one call for the folder, a new
   file coming in with its tags (no empty row drawn first), the count from the walk (`total`).
4. **The rerenders.** Profiled in Chromium: a quarter of the time was `Intl.PluralRules` made anew
   for each plural, by the menus; every update of the library built every row's menu, and drew its
   items, closed. `Menu` takes its items as a function, made and drawn only when it opens; the rules
   are made once per language. The browser's tag engine keeps a cover from its bytes, never through
   a data URL. The update after the tags went from 1.3 s to 0.9 s here, the one after the
   checksums from 1.1 s to 0.1 s; what is left is one drawing of the library, slow in this
   software-rendered WebKit.

Also: two lookups that were linear per file (removed tracks, a track's checksum now) go through a
set and a map. The desktop check (`JACKBIRD_CHECK_FOLDER`) saves a title into a file of its folder:
pointed at `fixtures/tags` it changes a fixture (it did here once, restored).

Not done: `.meta/covers/` keeps a picture no track names any more; the songs view's other views
still draw every album's tracks in Details at once.

### Next: the library without its songs (the owner, 2026-11-13)

"An option in Settings that does not show the songs in the library, so that on mobile it changes
to Details"; "when the files are not shown in the library, we remove the key that switches
between library and Details; to go back to the library we use the breadcrumb". The owner chose the
tree without songs (not removing the Songs view).

Plan:

- `preferences.songsInTree` (default on), saved; a switch in Settings, under the tone.
- Off: the tree shows its folders and groups only (`helpers/folders.ts`): no track or disc rows, in
  every view but Songs (which is the songs). Each row says whether it is a leaf (holds no folder or
  group: a folder with no subfolder, an album; an artist in Album artists holds albums). The scope
  and the breadcrumb are still worked out from every row; what is shown and walked with the keys is
  the folders.
- A computer: a folder shows in Details as now; a leaf has no toggle.
- A phone: a leaf opens in Details; a folder with subfolders is entered, as now. The faceplate's key
  between Library and Details is not shown; Details' breadcrumb goes back to the library, at the
  crumb's folder.

### Done: the library without its songs

As planned above. `preferences.songsInTree` (saved as `jackbird.songsInTree`, on until turned
off; Settings → Library, "Songs in the library"). Off, `derive()` (lib/workspace.ts) gives the
views `withoutSongs(rows)` (helpers/folders.ts, tested: the folders and groups, each a leaf or

### Proposal: the sheets over a phone's keyboard (awaiting approval)

The owner, with two screenshots: "the behaviour with the keyboard is not right". In the Android
app, "Add to the library → A jackbird server" shows only the sheet's head above the keyboard, its
field under it; in Chrome on Android, editing a track's tags, the sheet's foot (Cancel, Save) is
cut by the keyboard.

Why: a sheet sits at the bottom of the layout viewport, `90dvh` tall at most, and a phone's
keyboard does not shrink that viewport: in Chrome it lays over the page (`interactive-widget`'s
default, `resizes-visual`); in the app, Android 15 draws edge to edge and the webview keeps its
size, the keyboard over it.

Plan:

- `index.html`: `interactive-widget=resizes-content` in the viewport meta, so in Chrome on Android
  the page (and `dvh`) shrinks to what the keyboard leaves.
- The app: the plugin's insets listener (`PlayerPlugin.kt`) also reads the keyboard
  (`WindowInsetsCompat.Type.ime()`) and sends it with the edges, as `keyboard`;
  `lib/safe-area.ts` sets `--mm-keyboard` on the root (0 when hidden).
- Elsewhere (Safari on iOS, which has no `interactive-widget`): `lib/safe-area.ts` works the
  keyboard out of `visualViewport` (the layout viewport's height less the visual one's, while a
  field has the focus) into the same `--mm-keyboard`.
- `Modal` as a sheet: it rests on top of the keyboard (`margin-bottom: var(--mm-keyboard, 0px)`),
  at most as tall as what is left (`max-height: calc(100dvh - var(--mm-keyboard, 0px) - safe top -
  a margin)`); the navigation bar's room only when the keyboard is down. Its head and its foot
  stay; the body scrolls, and the field with the focus is scrolled into it (`scrollIntoView`
  with `block: "nearest"` on `focusin`, once the keyboard is up).
- Checked in Chromium with a phone's size and a simulated keyboard (the viewport shrunk), and in the
  Android emulator's check (`scripts/phone-check.mjs`) where it can open a sheet with a field.

As proposed (2026-11-14 00:30):

- `index.html`: `interactive-widget=resizes-content`.
- The Android app: `PlayerPlugin.kt`'s insets listener adds `keyboard`, how much of the webview
  the keyboard covers (its `ime()` inset less what lies below the webview; 0 when the window was
  made smaller instead), in CSS pixels; `lib/safe-area.ts` sets `--mm-keyboard` and dispatches

  (else `100dvh` less the keyboard) less the status bar and a margin, and 90dvh; the navigation
  bar's room less the keyboard. A field taking the focus inside it is scrolled into view
  (`block: "nearest"`) on `mm-keyboard`, or 400 ms after.

## 2026-11-14 02:50 UTC — The spectrum: finer, with its peaks

The owner: the usual corrections for a Web Audio spectrum (bands spaced logarithmically, -90 to
-10 dB, smoothing 0.5–0.65 instead of 0.8, a lift of 3 dB an octave, an FFT of 8192 points instead
of 2048 or less), and "I would like the spectrum's steps to be fine, and that it marks the peaks,
with a line that falls slowly".

Already so: the bands are spaced logarithmically (40 Hz to 16 kHz, helpers/spectrum.ts) and lifted
3 dB an octave from the range's middle. Not yet: the FFT is 1024 points, the smoothing 0.8, the
range -100 to -10 dB; the display has 8 segments a column, and no peaks.

Plan:

- The analysis, the same in the three places that make it (Web Audio in lib/player.ts,
  `crates/audio/src/spectrum.rs` for the desktop app's native output, `Spectrum.kt` for the
  phone's): 8192 points (4096 bins: 5.9 Hz each at 48 kHz, so the bass bands are each made of
  bins of their own), smoothing 0.6, -90 to -10 dB. The constants in helpers/spectrum.ts
  (`SPECTRUM_FFT`, `SPECTRUM_SMOOTHING`, `SPECTRUM_DECIBELS`), and the same numbers in Rust and
  Kotlin; the desktop's pipe keeps the last 8192 frames (was 2048).
- The display (`Spectrum`): fine steps, a segment about 2 CSS px tall with a hairline between
  (as many as the box's height holds, 8 at least), instead of 8. Each column's peak: a line one
  segment tall at the highest level it reached, held for 0.6 s, then falling slowly (0.4 of the
  height a second), never below the column. The bars fall as before. The peaks fall to nothing
  when the music stops, as the bars do.

### Done: the spectrum, finer, with its peaks

As planned above. helpers/spectrum.ts: `SPECTRUM_FFT` 8192, `SPECTRUM_SMOOTHING` 0.6,
`SPECTRUM_DECIBELS` -90 to -10; lib/player.ts's analyser takes them. `crates/audio`: `SIZE` 8192,
smoothing 0.6, -90 dB, the pipe keeping 8192 frames (`RECENT`); its test reads 4096 bins, the
tones at bins 82 and 163, after 250 ms (the 8192 frames read). `Spectrum.kt`: the same numbers.
`Spectrum` (the display): segments about 2 CSS px with a device pixel between, as many as its
height holds (8 at least); each column's peak held 600 ms, then falling 0.4 of the height a
second, never below the column, drawn as one lit segment; the drawing goes on until the peaks
have fallen.

Checked: `npm test`; `cargo test -p jackbird-audio` (the spectrum's test included), clippy and
fmt; in Chromium with the sample music, the display's spectrum in fine steps with its peaks over
the columns. Not checked here: the phone's (Kotlin; the mobile workflow builds it).

Commit: `feat(spectrum): 8192 points, smoothing 0.6, -90 dB; fine steps and peaks that fall slowly`

## 2026-11-13 23:55 UTC — Plan: the menu's veil takes its press; the detail's tracks follow the rows' height, no line under the last

The owner, three things:

1. "Clicking the overlay to close, the element behind the overlay receives the click."
   Reproduced in Chromium on the theme's demo, at a phone's width: with a `Menu` open as a sheet,
   a tap on the dimmed page closes it **and** presses the key under the finger. The veil is the
   popover's `::backdrop`, which takes no pointer events, so the press lands on the page; the menu
   closes on `pointerdown` and the click that follows goes to whatever is there. (`Modal` and
   `SubModal` are not affected: a modal dialog makes the page inert, and the sub-modal's veil is an
   element of its own.)
   - `theme/default/components/menu.html`: when the menu is a sheet (720px and below), a press
     outside it closes it as before, but stops there (`stopPropagation`), and the click that
     follows it is taken in the capture phase (`stopPropagation`, `preventDefault`), once. If no
     click follows (the press became a scroll: `pointercancel`; or nothing a moment after
     `pointerup`), nothing is left listening.
   - On a computer nothing changes: there is no veil there, and a click outside the list closes it
     and does what it does, as menus on the web do.
2. "The rows' height choice should also apply to the detail's tracks." The album sheet's tracks
   are a `mm-table` whose rows are as tall as their padding makes them, whatever the density.
   - `src/views/album-sheet.html`: its cells take `--mm-row` as their height (one line, as a tree
     row) and no vertical padding, so they are 1.75 / 2.25 / 2.75 rem (compact / comfortable /
     spacious), as the tree's.
3. "In the detail, remove the line under the last track."
   - `src/views/album-sheet.html`: the last row of each disc's table has no bottom border (the
     theme's `mm-table` keeps its faint one elsewhere).

## 2026-11-14 00:40 UTC — Proposal: the display's spectrum, a second look and the peaks' fall, in Settings (awaiting approval)

The owner: "on the LCD's spectrum, add another style to the preferences: the current one, and
columns made of fine lines. And an option for how fast the peaks fall: no peaks, fast fall
(current), slow fall, very slow fall."

Today (`theme/default/components/spectrum.html`) each column is a stack of fine horizontal
segments (about 2px each), lit up to its level over unlit ones; over it, its peak, one lit segment
held 600 ms at the highest it reached, then falling 0.4 of the height a second.

### The two looks

- **Segments** (the current one, the default): as it is.
- **Lines**: each column drawn as fine *vertical* lines, one device pixel wide with one device
  pixel between them, as many as the column's width holds; lit from the bottom to the level,
  smoothly (not in steps of a segment), over the same lines unlit to the top. Its peak, a short
  horizontal line (a device pixel or two tall) across the column's lines. Columns keep the gap
  they have today between them, so the bands still read apart.

### The peaks

| Choice | Held | Then falls (of the height a second) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| None | — | no peaks drawn |
| Fast (the current one, the default) | 600 ms | 0.4 |
| Slow | 1 s | 0.15 |
| Very slow | 1.6 s | 0.06 |

The columns themselves fall as they do today in every case; only the peaks change.

### How

- `src/types.ts`: `SpectrumLook = "segments" | "lines"`, `PeakFall = "none" | "fast" | "slow" | "very-slow"`.
- `src/helpers/spectrum.ts` (tested): `SPECTRUM_LOOKS`, `PEAK_FALLS`, `spectrumLookOf` and
  `peakFallOf` (a saved value, else the default), and `peakMotion(fall)` → `{ hold, speed }` or
  `null` for none.
- `src/lib/preferences.ts`: `spectrumLook` and `peakFall`, saved as `jackbird.spectrumLook` and
  `jackbird.peakFall` (`STORAGE_KEYS`), with `setSpectrumLook` and `setPeakFall`.
- `theme/default/components/spectrum.html`: two props, `look` ("segments" | "lines") and
  `peaks` (`{ hold, speed }` or `null`), read on each frame, so a change shows at once, playing
  or not. It still imports nothing (the view hands it `peakMotion(...)`).
- `src/views/deck-display.html`: passes them from the preferences.
- Settings → General, after "Rows": a section "Display spectrum" with two grouped choices
  (`Choice`, as the tone and the rows): the look (Segments, Lines) and the peaks (None, Fast,
  Slow, Very slow). The look's two options may show as small icons of each look if that reads
  better; names to start with.
- `src/lib/locales/en.ts` and `es.ts`: the section's title and hint, and each option's name
  (Spanish: "Espectro de la pantalla"; "Segmentos", "Líneas"; "Picos": "Sin picos", "Caída
  rápida", "Caída lenta", "Caída muy lenta").

Checked in Chromium with the sample music: both looks at a computer's width and a phone's, in
both tones, and each peak speed; `npm test`.

## 2026-11-15 22:55 UTC — The songs view's rows, the sheet's way back, and the window's own keys (the owner's asks)

The owner, with pictures of the desktop app:

1. **The songs view's rows.**
   - **What was wrong.** In a narrow library the title was cut to "A…", with the artist under it,
     while "Artist - Album" took the row's end (its meta).
   - **The fix.** The title above, and "Artist - Album" under it, with no meta at the end.
     `library.html`'s `rowSub` and `rowMeta`, for the songs view only (its rows' meta is that
     line already, `helpers/views.ts`).
2. **The sheet's way back** (a narrow window, the songs not in the tree).
   - **What was wrong.** "‹ Library" before the view's glyph.
   - **The fix.** The words go: the ‹ and the glyph are one key, in the accent, and go back.
     Without a way back the glyph is drawn as before.
3. **The window's own keys on the faceplate (macOS).**
   - **The window.** Its title bar is see-through (`TitleBarStyle::Overlay`, the title hidden),
     so the close, minimise and zoom keys sit on the faceplate's top left, over its brushed metal.
   - **Dragging.** The faceplate's bare metal drags the window (`data-tauri-drag-region`; the
     capability `core:window:allow-start-dragging` is there for the compact view).
   - **The compact view.** It takes the decorations away and gives them back
     (`src/lib/compact.ts`). Back, the title bar is made see-through again
     (`setTitleBarStyle("overlay")`, and its capability).
   - **Elsewhere.** Windows and Linux keep their title bars.

### Then, from the owner's pictures of the desktop app (2026-11-15 23:15 UTC)

4. **The wordmark, lower on macOS.** Under the window's keys it sat too close. With the faceplate
   marked `mac-window` (the desktop app on a Mac), it goes .5rem lower.
   - **Narrower than 900px** the top row is the display's (or the wordmark's with the keys). The
     faceplate keeps a strip over it for the window's keys, 1.75rem, and the wordmark is not
     lowered there.
5. **Icons nudged on hover, every other row.**
   - **The cause.** At an 18px root, a row of 2.25rem is 40.5px (two lines, 2.75rem, 49.5px), so
     every other row sat on a half pixel. When a hover started a transition, WebKit re-rounded
     them and their icons moved.
   - **The fix in CSS.** The row tokens (`--mm-row`, `--mm-row-two`, `--mm-row-queue`, at each
     density) are rounded to a whole pixel with CSS `round()`, where the engine has it
     (`@supports`). So is a windowed row's own height (`list-row.html`).
   - **The fix in the windowed lists** (Songs and the queue). They round the same way, through
     `rowPixels` (`helpers/density.ts`, tested), so `:window`'s arithmetic and the rows agree.
   - **Then, still moving in Artists.** A 41px row (2.25rem rounded) put a 16px icon, centred,
     11.5px down. The heights are now rounded to an even number of pixels (`round(…, 2px)`,
     `rowPixels` the same), and the icon sits 11px down.
   - **Measured** in Chromium: rows of 40px (one line) and 50px (two lines), with the icons on
     whole pixels.
6. **Album covers in the library** (the owner's ask, option A of two).
   - **Where.** Albums, Compilations and Albums by artist's albums draw their cover in place of
     the album's glyph. So does a folder in Files whose tracks all show one cover: an album's
     folder, its discs' folders, an artist's folder of one album.
   - **The rest.** Without a cover the glyph stays; artists and playlists keep theirs.
   - **The size.** The cover is 1.75rem, rounded to an even number of pixels (32px). The row is
     as tall as two lines.
   - **The line under the name.** "Album artist · year" (in Albums by artist the year alone, the
     artist being the row above), in place of the meta at the row's end.
   - **The code.** `helpers/views.ts` (`coverOfTracks`, `yearOfTracks`, the rows' `cover` and
     `sub`) and `helpers/tree.ts` (a folder's cover), tested. `ListRow` has a `cover` prop, a URL
     from `coverSrc`; a click on the cover still opens and closes its group.
   - **Checked** in Chromium on the nano's database: "…And Justice for All", "Metallica · 1988",
     its cover, a 50px row.
7. **Every album row lines up** (the owner's picture: a row without a cover, its name further
   left).
   - **In the tag views.** An album's row always has a cover's room (`framed`). Without a cover it
     shows the glyph in a frame of the cover's size.
   - **In Files.** Folders side by side have that room when one of them has a cover.
   - **Checked** in Chromium on the nano's database: names at one x (57.5px), rows of 50px.
   - **The tests** are in `views.test.ts` and `tree.test.ts`.
8. **A phone's open level drew a folder** (the owner's picture: "Metallica" in Albums by artist).
   Its row now has its view's glyph, and its cover.
9. **The desktop app did not follow the window's width** (the owner: resized, it kept the layout
   it opened with).
   - **The five views** that follow a media query from a script (`workspace`, `library`,
     `album-sheet`, `faceplate`, `queue-dialog`) listened to the MediaQueryList's `change` alone.
     In Chromium that follows a resize; in the desktop app on macOS (WebKit) it did not.
   - **`watchMedia`** (`src/lib/media.ts`) also asks the query again, afresh, on each `resize`,
     and tells a change once.
   - **Not reproduced in WebKit here:** Playwright's WebKit would not install on this Mac. For
     the owner to check.
10. **The groups that hold the track playing** (the owner's ask). An artist's, an album's or a
    playlist's row (by its tracks' ids), or a folder in Files (by the track's path), that holds
    the track playing has its name in the accent, as the track's own. It has no bars: those
    stay the track's.
    - **The code.** `library.html`'s `holdsPlaying`, with the playing track's path worked out
      once per change. `ListRow` has a `holdsPlaying` prop and the class `holds`.
    - **Checked** in Chromium with the sample music: "Gemma AI" in Artists and "Sample music" in
      Albums in the accent; in Files and Songs the track alone.
11. **A key to show the track playing** (the owner's ask: the empty place under the queue's key).
    - **The key.** The faceplate's 2 × 2 keys are now the queue, the preferences, the track playing
      (a target's glyph, "Show the track playing in the library") and the compact player. It is
      disabled while nothing plays.
    - **`revealTrack`** (`lib/workspace.ts`) does the work, for this key and the track menu's
      "Show in library", which before opened only Files' folders.
      - It makes the track's root active, and opens the rows above it in the root's view: its
        folders in Files; its artist, its album or both in the others (`trackPlace` in
        `helpers/views.ts`, tested, over the view's rows drawn all open).
      - It makes the track the current row, and the list scrolls it to the middle

    - **Checked** in Chromium with the sample music, a track playing:

    - `npm test` passes.
12. **The iPod's ejection, a toast** (the owner's ask). The library's one message sat over the next
    root's list, the iPod's tab being closed first.
    - **The toast.** `lib/toast.ts` (`showToast`, gone after 5s or on a click) and
      `views/toast.html`: over the app's bottom, centred, above a phone's keys, a polite status for
      screen readers.
    - **Who uses it.** `ejectIpod` says "… is ejected" there.
    - **Checked** in Chromium: shown, then gone after 5s.
13. **The roots' tab on a phone** (the owner's pictures of the desktop app, narrow).
    - **What was wrong.** On a phone the roots were one tab with a select in it
      (`appearance: base-select`). WebKit has no customizable select, so the native one showed, a
      small system box.
    - **The owner's choice.** Tabs and the + key on a phone too, as on a computer. The roots' bar
      is no longer `compact`: its tabs scroll sideways when they do not fit, and the + key stays.
    - **Kept.** Tabs' compact picker stays in the component for other uses, and draws its native

    - **Checked** in Chromium at 390px: the tab, its count and ×, the + key, the panes' key, and
      no sideways scroll.
14. **The library's rows with uneven sides** (the owner's picture: the current row's box further
    from the right edge than from the left).
    - **The cause.** The desktop app on macOS draws its own scrollbar (`html.mac-app
      ::-webkit-scrollbar`, `base.css`), which takes .5rem on the right.
    - **The fix.** The tree's pane keeps that room on both sides (`scrollbar-gutter: stable
      both-edges`). Overlay scrollbars take none either way.
    - **Checked** in Chromium with `mac-app` and a list that scrolls: 9px left, 10px right (the
      pane's 1px border).
15. **The edit dialog says how far its save has gone** (the owner's ask: at the foot's left, "n of
    N" with several files).
    - **The store.** `lib/progress.ts` (`writing`: the file being written, which of how many).
    - **Who fills it.** `saveEdits` (`lib/editor.ts`) and `renameSelected` (`lib/rename.ts`), as
      each file goes; it is emptied when they end.
    - **The foot.** The selection dialog's foot shows, at its left, the file's name, or
      "3 of 12 · name" with several (`editor.progress`). It is cut short rather than wrapped, and
      is a status for screen readers.
    - **Checked** in Chromium, four sample tracks saved: "1 de 4 · Jacket in the Car.mp3" to
      "4 de 4 · Kuroi Shin Kikan.mp3", then empty, and the library's "Metadatos guardados en 4
      pistas.".
16. **Tags from the file's name: the rename tab the other way** (the owner's ask; their choices: a
    switch in the same tab, and `/` for the parent folders).
    - **The switch.** The rename tab has two ways: "Tags → name" (as before) and "Name → tags",
      remembered. The pattern and its templates are the same.
    - **Reading.** `helpers/rename.ts`, `readTags` and `planReads`, tested.
      - The pattern is matched against the whole file name, without its extension.
      - With `/`, against as many folders above it as the pattern has, from the end of the path
        (`%artist%/%album%/%track% - %title%`).
      - `%track%`, `%disc%` and `%year%` take digits alone; the others any text but a `/`. Text
        between the fields is taken as it is, and `%filename%` and an unknown `%word%` take
        anything and give nothing.
      - "03" is written "3", and a total the track had ("3/12") is kept.
      - A file that does not match is left out and marked so; one whose tags would not change is
        marked "no change".
    - **Writing.** Only the pattern's fields change; the other tags (the cover too) stay. It goes
      the way the edit dialog's save goes (the files, the library, `.meta/`, the progress at the
      foot, a stop at the first refusal), through one function both share (`saveMetadata` in
      `lib/editor.ts`).
    - **Not in it, for now.** `/` in "Tags → name" (moving files into folders made from their
      tags) is left for later, at the owner's word.
    - **Done (2026-11-15).**
      - **The tab.** A `Choice` at its top ("Tags → name" / "Name → tags", remembered under
        `renameDirection`). Name → tags previews one column per pattern field, marks "Does not
        match" and "No change", and its key says "Write tags to N files" (`rename.read`).
      - **The code.** `readPlan` and `readSelected` in `lib/rename.ts`. `saveEdits`' loop is now
        `saveMetadata`, which both use.
      - **Checked** in Chromium on the sample music (a throwaway browser's copy). `%artist%
        %title%` read "Against | the Hourglass" and the rest; the four were written, the dialog
        closed, and Artists listed "Against, Jacket, Kuroi, Monologue". `%track% - %title%`
        marked all four "No encaja".
      - **The tests.** Folders with `/` are in `rename.test.ts`. `npm test` passes.
17. **The current row's frame, thinner on its right** (the owner's picture).
    - **The cause.** The frame was an inset shadow, which is not drawn on whole pixels. The
      tree's width is the splitter's share, so its right side often ends on a fraction of a pixel,
      and the shadow's line there came out faint.
    - **The fix.** It is a 1px border on a layer over the row (`::after`), which is snapped.
    - **Checked** in Chromium with the tree 400.37px wide, at scale 2: both sides two device
      pixels of the same ink (155).
18. **The rename pattern's typeahead** (the owner's ask: after a `%`, the fields).
    - **The helpers.** `fieldQuery`, `fieldsFor` and `completeField` (`helpers/rename.ts`,
      tested). A field is being typed after a `%` that opens one (an even number of `%` before
      it), with only a word since. The fields offered begin with it. Putting one in replaces what
      is typed, and a closing `%` after the caret, with `%field%`, the caret after it.
    - **The list.** Under the pattern's field, each field with its name ("%title% · Título").
      - ↑/↓ move, Enter or Tab put it in, and Escape closes the list (not the dialog); a click puts
        it in too.
      - While it shows, the field is a combobox (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`,
        `aria-activedescendant`).
      - `TextInput` is unchanged: the list listens round it.
    - **Checked** in Chromium, typed:
      - "%" offered the eight fields, "%t" `%title%` and `%track%`;
      - ↓ and Enter gave "%track%", then " - %ti" and Tab "%track% - %title%", the caret at its
        end;
      - Escape closed the list and left the dialog open.
19. **Examples under the pattern, not the list of fields** (the owner's ask). The fields
    themselves are offered as a `%` is typed (18).
    - **Tags → name.** Two patterns turned into the first track chosen's name, the first two the
      track fills with no gap, from `%track% - %title%`, `%artist% - %album% (%year%)`,
      `%artist% - %title%`, `%artist% - %album%`, `%title%` (`rename.examples`).
    - **Name → tags.** The deepest of `%artist%/%album%/%track% - %title%`, `%album%/%title%` and
      `%title%` that its folders allow, and the part of its path it reads (`rename.exampleRead`).
    - **Checked** in Chromium on the sample music (no track numbers, no year, no folders):
      - "Por ejemplo: %artist% - %title% → Gemma AI - Against the Hourglass · %artist% - %album%
        → Gemma AI - Sample music";
      - "Por ejemplo: %title% lee «Against the Hourglass»".
20. **The cover's keys in the edit dialog, joined under it** (the owner's ask: like shuffle and
    repeat beside the display).
    - **What changed.** "Choose image" and "Remove" were two keys of words under the sleeve; now
      they are two keys of the bezel's kind (an image's and a bin's glyph, their words as label and
      title), side by side under the sleeve, their top .5rem tucked under its bottom edge, a
      hairline between them.
    - **When there is no cover.** Remove is disabled.
    - **The code.** `src/components/cover-drop.html`, which no longer uses `Key`.
    - **Checked** in Chromium, both tones, a track with no cover: the image key, the bin key dim.
21. **Each file's path in Info** (the owner's ask, the list of several tracks). Under each
    track's title, its path in its root, small, in the mono face, in the muted ink, broken
    anywhere rather than overflowing (`.file-path`).
    - **Checked** in Chromium: three sample tracks, each with "… .mp3" under its title.
22. **`/` in "Tags → name": the folders above the file** (the owner's picture: `/` came out as
    `_`; their wish: the diff with the parent folder). The earlier note (16) left this for later;
    the owner asked for it now.
    - **The rule.** As in "Name → tags", the pattern is laid on the path's end:
      - `%album%/%track% - %title%` names the folder the file is in and the file;
      - the folders above stay;
      - a file with fewer folders gets them made.
    - **The plan.** Each folder's name is made safe on its own. Clashes and empty names are found
      on the whole new path. `RenamePlan` has `shownFrom` and `shown`, the part of the path the
      pattern names, which the preview and its diff show.
    - **The rename.** It makes the new folder first where the source can (`vfs.mkdir`; the desktop
      app's `Root::rename` makes it anyway).
    - **What is left behind.** Only the tracks move: other files in the old folder (a

## 2026-11-16 01:20 UTC — Composer, comment and lyrics; then the files' audio properties (approved)

The owner's ask: tag fields that were missing, the comment first; then lyrics too; and, in
Info, each file's size, format and quality. Bpm, to be worked out from the audio, is a third
step, later.

### Step 1: composer, comment, lyrics

| Field | MP3 (ID3v2.3/2.4; v2.2 read) | FLAC (Vorbis) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Composer | `TCOM` (`TCM`) | `COMPOSER` |
| Comment | `COMM` (`COM`) | `COMMENT` (`DESCRIPTION` read too) |
| Lyrics | `USLT` (`ULT`) | `LYRICS` (`UNSYNCEDLYRICS` read too) |

- **`crates/id3`.** `COMM` and `USLT` share one layout: encoding, a three-letter language, a
  description ended by a terminator, the text. A `Comment` type with `decode_comment` and
  `encode_comment`, tested.
- **`crates/tags`.**
  - `Field` gains `Composer`, `Comment` and `Lyrics`, at the end of `Field::ALL` (the wasm wire
    is in its order), and `Fields` and `Edit` their values.
  - **Reading.** The comment and the lyrics are their frame with no description. iTunes' own
    comments (`iTunNORM`, `iTunSMPB`, …, with descriptions) are not the comment, and are never
    touched.
  - **Writing.** That frame is replaced (its language kept; `eng` for a new one), or removed when
    emptied; the other `COMM`/`USLT` frames stay. Tests in `tests.rs`.
- **The wire** (`crates/tags-wasm`, `src/helpers/tagwire.ts`), `crates/api` (`Metadata`, with
  `#[serde(default)]`, `edit_for`: the new fields only when they changed, as the disc's), and
  `crates/store` (the family library's fields and layers: JSON with `serde(default)`, no
  migration).
- **The interface.**
  - `TrackMetadata` gains `composer`, `comment` and `lyrics`. A `.meta/` from before has them not:
    `readsEveryField`'s fields list grows, so those tracks are read once more and nothing is lost.
  - **The editor.** Composer as a field. Comment as a text area of a few lines. Lyrics as a large
    text area, folded until opened.
  - Info shows them, and the rename patterns take `%composer%` and `%comment%`.

#### Step 1 done (2026-11-16)

- **`crates/id3`.** `Comment`, `decode_comment` and `encode_comment` (`comment.rs`), tested: a
  round trip in each encoding, a description read, a bad language written `eng`.
- **`crates/tags`.**
  - `Field::Composer`, `Comment` and `Lyrics` (`Field::ALL`: 12), in `Fields` and `Edit`.
  - **MP3.** `TCOM` (`TCM`), and `COMM` and `USLT` (`COM`, `ULT`) through `shown_comment`: the
    frame with no description. Written in place (its language kept, `eng` for a new one) or
    removed alone when emptied. Safeguard 3 checks every other `COMM`/`USLT` byte for byte
    (`touched`).
  - **FLAC.** `COMPOSER`; `COMMENT` (`DESCRIPTION` read); `LYRICS` (`UNSYNCEDLYRICS` read).
  - **Tests.** iTunes' `iTunNORM` comment kept byte for byte, only it means no comment, emptied
    ones removed alone, a FLAC's read and written.
  - **The older tests** that took `TCOM` and `LYRICS` as frames the editor does not manage now

  - **The fixtures** already held a comment and lyrics, unread until now. `fixtures/tags/make.mjs`
    expects them, and `cases.json` says them (ffmpeg, which `make.mjs` needs, is not on this

- **The rest.**

### Step 2: the files' audio properties (done)

- **Read from the head.** `crates/tags/src/audio.rs`: `audio(head, layout)` gives the length
  (ms), the bit rate (kbit/s; the average for a variable one), the sample rate, the channels,
  the depth (FLAC) and whether it is variable.
  - **MP3.** The first frame (`mpeg.rs`, moved here from `ipod-sync`, which re-exports it). A
    Xing/Info frame gives the exact length and the average rate. Without one, the audio's bytes
    at the frame's rate.
  - **FLAC.** `STREAMINFO`, after any ID3 tag in front (a `fLaC` inside the tag's text is
    skipped).
  - **The head.** `AUDIO_PROBE` (4 KiB) past the audio's start. `crates/core`'s layout and the
    browser's engine (`readEnd`) give the module that much.
- **Carried.**
  - **The desktop app.** `ScannedJson`/`InspectionJson.audio` (`crates/api`). The store reads it
    from its blob's head.
  - **The browser.** The wire gains a trailing block: a flag, then three u32 and three u8.
    `readAll`/`readBlobAll` give it with the tags.
  - **The iPods.** The desktop app's listing takes the length and rate from its database. The
    web's takes the length, and works out the rate from the size.
- **Kept.** `Track.audio` and `MetaRecord.audio`. A record from before has none (not even
  `null`), so it is read once more.
- **Shown.**
  - **Info.** One file: length, bit rate (VBR said), sample rate, channels, depth. Several: each
    file's line under its path, "MP3 · 320 kbps · 44,1 kHz · 3:17 · 7,5 MB" (`audioLine`).
  - **Duplicates.** The same line. "Best" is lossless first, then the highest bit rate, then the
    largest.
- **Tested.** Rust: the fixtures' length and rates. TS: the wire's block, `audioLine`, `bestOf`
  by bit rate.

### Step 3: BPM, read, written and worked out (approved; done)

- **The field.** `bpm`, a whole number as text, as the others: ID3 `TBPM` (v2.2 `TBP`), FLAC
  `BPM`. On the wire, the API, the store and `TrackMetadata`, as step 1's; in `LATER_FIELDS`, so
  a `.meta/` from before is read once more. The iPods: the desktop app's listing
  reads it from the database (`Song.bpm`, 0 meaning none), as the web's.
- **In the editor.** A short "BPM" field after the composer, with a "Calculate" key beside it.
- **Worked out in the browser** (both apps):
  - `lib/bpm.ts` decodes the file (`decodeAudioData` in an `OfflineAudioContext` at 11 025 Hz,
    mono, so a 5-minute song is ~13 MB).
  - `helpers/bpm.ts`, pure and tested, works out the tempo:
    - from 60 s from the middle of the song (the whole one if shorter);
    - an onset envelope (energy rise in short windows);
    - its autocorrelation over 60–200 BPM, weighted towards 80–160 against half or double tempo;
    - rounded to a whole number.
  - Tested with click tracks at known tempos, and with a click track under noise.
- **Several tracks.**
  - Each is worked out in turn: "n of N" at the footer's left, and it can be cancelled.
  - Each keeps its own value: the field says "several", and Save writes each one
    (`saveMetadata` already takes one metadata per track).
  - A value typed by hand instead goes to them all, as any field.
- **One track.** The value fills the field; Save writes it.
- **Known limits.**
  - A tempo detector errs on some music: half or double tempo, rubato, songs with no beat.
  - The value is a proposal, editable before saving.

#### Step 3, done

- **The field** as proposed. Rust: `Field::Bpm` (`TBPM`/`TBP`, `BPM`), the wire's thirteenth
  field, `crates/api`, the store's layers. The iPod's database: `mhit` 0x7A (`db.rs`), given by
  the desktop app's listing and the web's (`ipod-wasm`).
- **The detector** (`helpers/bpm.ts`):
  - The envelope steps 32 samples (~2.9 ms at 11 025 Hz) over a 128-sample window: the rise of
    the amplitude.
  - Peaks of its autocorrelation over 60–200 BPM, weighted 1 within 80–160. Outside it, ×0.7 at
    once, then less the further an octave away: a click track's half tempo is as strong a peak
    as its tempo, and only the range tells them apart.
  - Told finely at four beats on.
  - No beat (null) when the best peak is under 5% of the envelope's own energy. Steady noise
    gives ~3%, the four samples 8.5–25%, a click track ~90%.
  - Tested: click tracks at 84, 90, 100, 120, 128, 140 and 155 BPM; one under noise; one with
    softer off-beats; one in the middle of a long silence; steady noise, silence and three
    seconds (none).
- **The decoding** (`lib/bpm.ts`):
  - The file's bytes come through the URL it plays from, else its folder's `read`.
  - `decodeAudioData` in an `OfflineAudioContext` at 11 025 Hz, the channels mixed.
- **The editor.**
  - "Calculate" sits inside the BPM field, and turns into "Stop" while it works.
  - One track: the value goes into the field. Several: each value is kept (`editor.bpms`), the
    field says "(Worked out for each)", and Save writes each its own.
  - A value typed, or the field emptied with its override box, goes to them all.
  - Progress is shown at the footer's left: "BPM 2 of 4 · name".
  - Info shows the BPM.
- **Fixed on the way.**
  - A save kept no `audio` in its record, so the file would have been read again on the next
    load.
  - Clippy's `manual_checked_division` in `audio.rs`.
- **Checked** in Chromium on the samples:
  - One track took ~0.5 s and gave 93 BPM.
  - Four took 2.2 s and gave 93, 100, 109 and 123, each saved to its own file and read back
    from it.
  - Not checked in WebKit (the macOS app): its `decodeAudioData` of a FLAC, and `fetch` of an
    `asset:` URL (the folder's `read` is the fallback).
- `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `npm test` and
  `tsc` pass.

Commit: `feat(lists): on a touch screen or the phone layout, a track's number inside its larger box`

### The albums cut on their right after the separator moved: found and fixed (2026-11-16)

- **The owner's pictures.** Moving the separator to narrow the details, the albums stayed as wide
  as before and were cut on their right: no lengths, no ⋮.
- **Reproduced in WebKit** (Playwright's 26.5; `scratchpad/wk-drag.cjs`), with five albums from
  the classic. After dragging the separator from a 940px details pane to 540px:
  - `.sheet` and `.albums` were 540px wide;
  - the albums were still 886px, ending at 1664 in a pane ending at 1300.
  - Chromium does not do it.
- **The cause.** `.albums` is a grid with its one column left implicit, and WebKit kept that
  column's old width. Declaring it (`grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)`) fixed it, and so did
  dropping `content-visibility`, both tried live. The first was kept, since it keeps the
  off-screen albums' saving.
- **Also corrected.** `contain-intrinsic-size: auto 14rem` set both axes, though its comment
  meant the height alone. It is now `contain-intrinsic-block-size`. On its own this did not fix
  the cut.
- **After the fix.** Same drag: the albums end at 1282 in the pane ending at 1300, lengths and ⋮
  in view. `npm test` (558) passes.

Commit: `fix(details): the albums follow the details' width when the separator moves (WebKit)`

## 2026-11-16 — The details' tracks scrolled up and down in the desktop app (the owner, on the Mac)

- **The owner.** On a phone the numbered boxes stay as they are, touch screen or not (already so:
  `(hover: none), (max-width: 720px)`). In the macOS app, `.mm-table-wrap` scrolled vertically.
- **The cause.** `mm-table-wrap` scrolls sideways (`overflow-x: auto`), so its other axis scrolls
  too. `SelectBox`'s hit area (`::before`, `inset: -.375rem`) around the numbered box (2rem) is
  2.75rem tall, more than a row (2.25rem): it stuck out under a disc's last row. Reproduced in a
  bare page: 85px of content in an 81px box.
- **The fix.** `select-box.html`: the numbered box's hit area widens it sideways only
  (`inset: 0 -.375rem`). The same page then has 81px in 81px.
- `npm test` passes.

Commit: `fix(lists): the numbered box's hit area no longer scrolls a disc's tracks up and down`

## 2026-11-17 12:40 UTC — Plan: the queue in four parts; its rows on two lines with their length (the owner's asks; choices await them)

**The owner.**

1. The queue's rows: two lines (the title; "artist - album"), and each track's length in its
   codec's colour, as Details shows it.
2. The queue in four parts: the tracks played, the track playing, the tracks queued on purpose,
   and the tracks suggested.
3. Playing a disc (from a track): the disc's tracks before it are left out, and those after it
   become suggested tracks; "Play next" and "Add to queue" then go to the queued-on-purpose part.

**Where things are now.** `PlaybackQueue` is `{ items, sourceLabel }`, and `playback.index` the
track playing: before it is "played", after it "upcoming". Playing a disc replaces the whole
queue with the disc, from its first track, at the track chosen. "Play next" goes right after the
track playing, "Add to queue" at the very end (after the rest of the disc). A queue that is one
disc (`queueIsAlbum`) shows no grips and no ×, and its played tracks are not dimmed.

**Plan.**

1. **The model** (`types.ts`, `helpers/queue.ts`, tested). A queued item may carry
   `queued: true`: put there on purpose. The four parts are the items before the index (played),
   the index (playing), the `queued` items after it (queued) and the rest after it (suggested).
   One rule holds after every change: right after the track playing come the queued items, then
   the suggested ones, so the list reads in the order it plays.
   - `playNextIn`: the items, marked, right after the track playing (first of the queued part).
   - `addTo`: the items, marked, at the end of the queued part (before the suggested ones).
   - New `playFrom(position, items, at, label)`, what playing a disc or a folder does: the tracks
     played so far and the one that was playing stay as played; the chosen track plays; the queued
     part stays queued, right after it; the disc's tracks after the chosen one become the
     suggested part (the old suggested ones go). Its tracks before the chosen one are left out.
   - New `jumpTo(position, at)`, what a click on a row, Next, Previous and a track's end do: the
     index moves, queued tracks it jumps over stay queued (right after the new track playing),
     and the rule above is restored.
   - `moveTo`: a track dragged into the queued part becomes queued, into the suggested part
     suggested. Played tracks are not dragged, and nothing is dropped among them.
   - `isOneAlbum` and `queueIsAlbum` go: playing a disc no longer leaves its earlier tracks in
     the queue, so the played part is always what was played (dimmed), and every upcoming track
     can be moved or removed.
2. **The order** (`helpers/order.ts`, tested): `upcoming` and `nextIndex` take the queued
   part's length: its tracks play first, in their order, then the suggested ones, in theirs or
   shuffled. Shuffle shuffles the suggested part only.
3. **The player** (`lib/player.ts`): `playTracks(items, at, label)` (over `playFrom`) for
   `workspace.ts`'s `playFromDetail` and `playFromTree`; every write of the index through
   `jumpTo`; the native player's next tracks from the new order. `QueueEntry.state`:
   "played" | "current" | "queued" | "suggested", and it gains `album` and the length and codec
   family. The saved session keeps the mark (`helpers/session.ts`).
4. **The list** (`views/queue-list.html`): a heading over each part that has tracks
   ("Played", "Queued", "Suggested"; the track playing needs none), a row of the list's one
   height (the list is drawn a window at a time, and `:window` takes one row height); each row's
   second line "artist - album" (the root's label after it, " · ", when the queue spans roots);
   at its end its length in its codec's colour (`--mm-codec-<family>-ink`, as in Details), the
   bars in its place on the track playing. New keys in `en.ts` and `es.ts`.

**The owner's calls (my recommendation first):**

- **(a) A queue on purpose, when a disc starts playing:** kept, and played right after the
  chosen track, before the rest of the disc (recommended); or emptied, the disc alone.
- **(b) The played part, when a disc starts playing:** kept, a history across discs
  (recommended); or emptied.
- **(c) A click on a suggested track below the queued ones:** they stay queued, after it
  (recommended); or they count as played.
- **(d) The length in the queue:** plain text (recommended); or a key that opens the track's
  info, as in Details.

### The owner's answers on the queue in four parts (2026-11-17), and the plan as it now stands

- **(a) and (b), a disc started while the queue holds tracks put there on purpose:** ask. A
  dialog with three keys: **Keep in the queue** (what was played and the tracks queued on
  purpose stay; the disc comes in as the track playing and the suggested ones), **Save as
  playlist and replace** (the "Save queue as playlist" dialog; once saved, the queue is replaced
  by the disc; closing it without saving changes nothing), **Replace without saving**. Closing
  the dialog changes nothing. With no track put there on purpose, the queue is replaced without
  asking (the played part goes too).
- **(c), a click on a suggested track below the queued ones:** they count as played (as now).
- **(d):** the length is plain text.

So, in the plan above:

- `playFrom` is the "Keep" choice only. Replacing is a new queue of the disc from the chosen
  track (`replaceWith`). A queue holds tracks on purpose when any of its items carries `queued`
  (`hasQueued`), played ones included.
- `jumpTo` keeps no queued tracks it jumps over: a move of the index only restores the rule (the
  queued tracks right after the track playing), which matters going back (Previous, a click on a
  played track): the tracks that become upcoming again are put queued first, then the rest.
- The prompt: `views/replace-queue-dialog.html` (a `Modal`, `ui.replaceQueue`); the disc waiting
  for the answer is kept in `lib/player.ts`. Its "Save as playlist and replace" opens the
  playlist dialog (`mode: "queue"`); `lib/playlists.ts` tells the player once it has saved, and
  the player then replaces the queue; the dialog closed unsaved drops the disc waiting.
- The list: one listbox with up to four groups, each with a small heading (a legend, not a row)
  and its own windowed list (a `:window` measures its place in the scroller, so several share
  one); the drag finds the row under the pointer (`elementsFromPoint`) instead of counting rows,
  as headings stand between the parts. Dropped on a queued track (or the queued heading) it
  becomes queued; on a suggested one (or that heading), suggested. Alt+Up on the first suggested
  track makes it the last queued one, without moving; Alt+Down on the last queued, the reverse.
- Shuffle: the queued part plays first in its order; the suggested part in the shuffled order.

### Done: the queue in four parts; its rows on two lines with their length (2026-11-17)

- `types.ts`: `QueueItem.queued?` marks a track put in the queue on purpose.
- `helpers/queue.ts`: `queuedCount`, `hasQueued`, `settle` (the queued tracks right after the
  current one, then the suggested ones), `jumpTo`, `replaceWith`, `playFrom` (the "Keep" choice),
  `markQueued`; `playNextIn` and `addTo` mark what they queue (Add to queue now goes after the
  queued tracks, before the suggested ones); `moveTo` takes the part a track lands in.
  `isOneAlbum` goes. Tested.
- `helpers/order.ts`: `upcoming` and `nextIndex` play the queued part first, shuffled or not.
  `helpers/session.ts` keeps the mark across a reload. Tested.
- `lib/player.ts`: `playTracks` (used by `workspace.ts`'s `playFromDetail` and `playFromTree`)
  replaces the queue with the disc from the chosen track, or, when the queue holds tracks put there
  on purpose, asks (`ui.replaceQueue`); `answerReplace`, `queueSaved`, `queueNotSaved`; every
  index change through `goTo` (`jumpTo`); the native player's next tracks follow the new order.
  `QueueEntry` gains `album`, `duration` and `codec`; its states are played, current, queued
  and suggested. `queueIsAlbum` goes, with its special cases in the queue's dialog, panel and the
  footer's menu.
- `lib/playlists.ts`: saving the queue tells the player (a disc waiting then replaces it); its
  dialog closed unsaved drops the disc waiting.
- `views/replace-queue-dialog.html` (new, in `app.html`): Keep in the queue, Save as playlist and
  replace, Replace without saving; closed, nothing changes.
- `views/queue-list.html`: one listbox, a group per part with its heading (the playing one's for
  screen readers only), each part windowed; rows with the title, "artist - album" and the length
  in its codec's text colour (the bars on the track playing); grips only on upcoming tracks; the
  drag lands on the row or heading under the pointer; Alt+Up/Down cross the queued/suggested edge
  by changing part first.
- Keys in `en.ts` and `es.ts`: `queue.played`, `queue.playing`, `queue.queued`,
  `queue.suggested`, `replaceQueue.*`.
- Seen with the sample music at 420px and 1280px: a track played from the library leaves out
  the ones before it and suggests the ones after; Add to queue fills the queued part; a track
  played then asks; Keep gives played, playing, queued, suggested as planned; a drag of a
  suggested track onto a queued one queues it; Next plays the queued part first; Replace without
  saving leaves the disc alone. Layers, styles, types, unit tests (but the two WebAssembly
  tests, which need the modules built) and coverage pass.
