## 2026-11-17 23:20 UTC — Plan: the Android app's page knocked out of place by a two-finger drag (the owner's report; awaiting their approval)

- **The owner.** A two-finger drag knocked the Android app out of place. In their screenshot the
  faceplate is gone above the screen's top, the tab band is cut, and an empty band shows under the
  footer. It stays that way.
- **What moved.** The whole page moved: the faceplate and the footer went up together. Nothing in
  the page that scrolls holds both of them (`.app` > `.top` and `.body` > `.footbar`), so what
  scrolled is the page itself (the document's root) or the webview.
- **Not reproduced here** (no Android device or emulator). In Chromium as a phone (Pixel 7,
  touch, the sample music), the document is never taller or wider than the window. It was watched
  on every frame over each of the library's views, a song playing, the queue's panel opened and
  closed, and the panes swiped. Two-finger drags (touches sent through the DevTools protocol) move
  nothing. The Android webview (wry 0.57.1's `RustWebView`) keeps WebView's defaults: built-in
  zoom off (no pinch zoom), and the viewport meta ignored.
- **Proposal.** Three guards, so that neither the page nor the webview can scroll:
  1. `index.html` (the app's page; base.css also serves the theme's demo, which scrolls):
     `html, body { height: 100%; overflow: hidden; overscroll-behavior: none; }`. With this, no
     gesture scrolls the root, and no overscroll glows or stretches it.
  2. `app.html`: `.app` pinned to the window (`position: fixed; inset: 0`, in place of
     `height: 100dvh`). Then nothing in the document's flow can make it taller than the window,
     so nothing can scroll it, not even `scrollIntoView()` (the library's, which scrolls every
     box around a row). Embedded (`.app.embed`) it keeps its own place.
  3. Android (`PlayerPlugin.load`): `webView.overScrollMode = View.OVER_SCROLL_NEVER`, so the
     webview itself neither glows nor stretches at an edge (Android 12 and later stretch it).
- **To check on the phone** once built: the same two-finger drags, over the list, the faceplate
  and the footer. Also a search with the keyboard up, then closed.

### The two-finger drag on Android: postponed (the owner, 2026-11-17)

- **The owner.** It no longer happens, and they do not know how to make it happen again.
  Postponed until they find out. The plan above stays as it is, not applied.

## 2026-11-19 00:24 UTC — Plan: "Upload a folder" picks files on Android; two folder keys that read as one; the server's tab squeezed out of the roots' bar (the owner's report; awaiting their approval)

Seen in the owner's screenshots of the Android app, on the family library's tab of the server it
is connected to, with a folder of the phone ("Music", 82) open beside it.

### 1. "Upload a folder" picks files on Android

- **The owner.** In "Add music", "Upload a folder" opens Android's picker of files, not of folders.
- **The cause.** The key is `<input type="file" webkitdirectory>` (`views/import-dialog.html`).
  Android's webview ignores `webkitdirectory`: wry's chrome client opens the intent that
  `FileChooserParams.createIntent()` gives, a picker of files. A phone's browser does the same
  (`helpers/picked.ts` already says that there the folder input "is there but chooses no folder").
  So on a phone the key does what "Upload songs" does.
- **The proposal.**
  1. **The Android app.** The key opens Android's folder picker (the plugin's `pick_folder`, as a
     phone's folder root does: E6b-D9), and the songs under the folder are uploaded one at a time,
     with the dialog's progress and report as now. Each song goes from Android's document provider
     straight to the service, streamed by the plugin, so no song's bytes cross to the page (as with
     E6b-D9's tags) and none is held whole in memory:
     - Kotlin: a new command, `folder_upload { tree, path, url, authorization }`
       (`PlayerPlugin.folderUpload`, on the folders' worker): the file opened through the document
       provider, sent by `HttpURLConnection` as `POST` with a fixed-length stream (its size from
       the provider; chunked when it has none), `content-type: application/octet-stream` and the
       session's bearer; it answers `{ status, body }`. The service's `library/upload` already
       streams the body to disk (no size limit to meet). `http://` servers work as they do for the
       page and the player (`usesCleartextTraffic`, E6b).
     - The grant is not kept for an upload: `pick_folder` takes `keep` (default true, as now); with
       `false` it does not call `takePersistableUriPermission`. Android's grant then lasts while
       the app runs, and uploads do not pile up kept grants (Android caps them at 128–512).
     - `crates/player/build.rs` lists `folder_upload`; its permission (autogenerated) joins
       `permissions/default.toml`.
     - TypeScript: `lib/sources/service.ts` gives the upload's URL and bearer for a path
       (`libraryUploadTarget`), and reads the answer as `uploadToLibrary` does (a 401 asks to sign
       in, as any call does). `lib/sources/phone.ts`: `pickPhoneUpload()` (the folder's tree, its
       name and its songs' paths, from `folder_walk`) and `uploadPhoneSong()`. `lib/importing.ts`:
       the device's files and the phone's folder share one loop (progress, report, the library read
       again); `uploadPath` takes the path under what was picked, so a phone's song lands where the
       browser's would: `Uploads/<who>/<folder>/<path in it>`.
  2. **A phone's browser and the iOS app.** No "Upload a folder" key: there it would choose files,
     or nothing. "Upload songs" stays.

### 2. Two keys that read as one

- **The owner.** "Upload a folder" is redundant with "Import a folder of this machine…".
- **Why it reads so.** The second is a folder of the service's machine (owners: the server copies
  the songs from its own disk), but on a phone, or any device that is not the server, "this
  machine" reads as the device.
- **The proposal.** Its name says whose folder it is: "Import a folder of the server…" /
  "Importar una carpeta del servidor…" (`import.machine`). Both keys stay, since they are not the
  same: one sends this device's songs, the other takes them from the server's own disk.
  (The other way, if the owner prefers it: no import key on phones.)

### 3. The server's tab squeezed out of the roots' bar

- **The owner.** The narrow tab with the green stripe is the server's (the one open).
- **The cause.** The roots' bar's tabs scroll sideways (step 13 of 2026-11-15: not `compact` on
  phones), and what follows them never shrinks: the `+` key and "Add music" in words (the `end`
  slot) and the panes' key (`aside`). At the screenshot's 432px they leave the tabs some 270px:
  "Music 82 ×" fills them, and the open tab is scrolled out of view but for its left edge. Nothing
  brings the open tab into view.
- **The proposal (the owner's choice).**
  - The two keys as icons, each with its name as its label and title: **+ folder** (a folder, a
    server, an iPod: `openAdding()`, the `+` key now) and **+ note** ("Add music" to the family
    library: `openImport()`). Everywhere, so the bar reads the same on every width (the `+` key
    already has no words once a root is open).
  - The tabs keep scrolling sideways, and the open one is brought into view whenever it changes or
    the bar's width does (`theme/default/components/tabs.html`). By the strip's own `scrollLeft`,
    not `scrollIntoView()`, which also scrolls every box around it.

### Checks

- `npm test`; `cargo fmt`/`clippy`/`test` for `crates/player`'s list of commands.
- Chromium as a phone (390px and 432px): two roots, the open one the second, in view; the two
  icon keys; the dialog's keys as a phone's browser shows them (no "Upload a folder").
- The Kotlin side builds only in the Android build (`mobile.yml`); there is no Android SDK here.
  To check on the phone: "Upload a folder" opens the folder picker; a folder's songs are uploaded,
  with progress and the report; a big FLAC goes through; cancelling the picker does nothing.

### 2026-11-19 00:50 UTC — 4. Uploads over the home network, not through the tunnel (the owner's ask; part of the plan above)

- **The owner.** When the phone and the server are on the same home network, the songs should go
  straight to the server, not through intergate's proxy.
- **Now.** The app speaks to the server only at the address it was given: outside home, the
  tunnel's (`https://…`, through intergate's server and its SSH tunnel back home). An upload goes
  out to the internet and back in, at the slower of the two links, even with the server in the
  next room.
- **What the service already has.** With sign-in on and the home network allowed (S3-D7), it
  listens on each of the machine's addresses (`main.rs`, looked at again every 30 s), and it signs
  short-lived tokens for one use (the media token, `signer.sign("media", …)`).
- **The proposal.**
  1. **The service** (`crates/service`): a route for editors, `GET /api/library/upload/route`,
     asked through the address in use (the tunnel's, HTTPS). It answers:
     - `ticket`: a token signed for uploads alone (`signer.sign("upload", { person }, 30 min)`),
       which `POST /api/library/upload` takes as `Authorization: Upload <ticket>` and no other
       route takes. On the home network the request is plain HTTP, so the session's bearer never
       travels there: whoever might read the ticket could only upload songs to that person's
       place, for minutes. (As an `Authorization`, it meets the guard's rule that a change without
       an `Origin` carries one.)
     - `addresses`: the home network's addresses it is listening on now (`http://192.168.1.20:4646`,
       IPv6 in brackets); none while the home network is not allowed, so then nothing changes.
  2. **The phone** (the Android app only: a page in a browser outside home is HTTPS, and could not
     call an `http://` address): before a batch, the dialog asks for the route; if it has
     addresses, the plugin tries them (`reach { urls, ticket }`: a request with the ticket that
     only this instance can check, 1.5 s each). A wrong device at that address, or another
     jackbird, does not know this instance's signature, so it does not answer as it. The first
     that answers is where the batch goes:
     - a folder (point 1): the plugin's `folder_upload` is given that address and the ticket;
     - "Upload songs": the page sends each file there (`http://tauri.localhost` is one of the
       origins the service already answers with CORS).
     - If none answers (the phone is away from home), or one stops answering mid-batch (the phone
       left the Wi-Fi), the songs go, or go on, by the usual address with the session, as now.
     - Not where the app already speaks to a home address (`http://192.168.…`): it is there
       already.
     - The dialog says which way they go: "Sending over the home network" / "Enviando por la red
       de casa".
     - A batch that outlasts the ticket asks for another.
  3. **In a container** (Docker Compose, F5): the service does not know the host's address in the
     home network (it sees its own bridge's), and the compose file publishes the port on the
     host's loopback only. So there it offers no address unless the owner names one: a setting,
     `JACKBIRD_LAN=http://192.168.1.20:4646`, with the port published there too (`4646:4646` in
     `compose.yaml`). **A question for the owner**: how their server runs.
- **Checks.** `cargo test` for the service: the route's ticket and addresses; the upload with a
  ticket, a ticket refused anywhere else, an expired one refused. On the phone: at home, a folder
  goes over the home network (the service's log shows the home address); away, through the tunnel.
- **Later, not now.** Playback could take the same way at home (the songs streamed from the home
  address); it is not part of this plan.

### 2026-11-19 00:41 UTC — The owner's answers; point 4 encrypted; the home network in the setup wizard (awaiting their approval of point 4 and of the admins' part of point 2)

(The section above headed "00:50" was written at about 00:35: its time is wrong.)

- **Point 1** ("Upload a folder" on Android): approved. It now shares the plugin's uploader with
  point 4 (below), so "Upload songs" in the Android app goes through it too.
- **Point 2** (the import key): approved as "Import a folder of the server…", **for admins and
  owners** (the owner's choice; owners only until now). So the service lets admins do it too:
  `POST /api/library/import` and `GET /api/folders` (the server machine's folders, for the dialog)
  move from the owners' routes to the admins'. What it means: an admin sees the names of the
  server machine's folders (wherever the service's user can read, from its home) and can copy the
  songs of any of them into the library. Opening such a folder as a tab (`POST /api/roots`) stays
  the owners'. In the interface, `can('admin')` in place of `can('owner')` for the key.
- **Point 3** (the roots' bar): approved as written.
- **Point 4** (uploads over the home network): the owner asks for the songs to be encrypted on the
  home network, with a key agreed over HTTPS. The proposal, revised:
  1. **The key, over HTTPS.** `POST /api/library/upload/route` (editors), through the address in
     use (the tunnel's), answers `{ ticket, key, addresses }`:
     - `ticket`: signed for uploads (`signer.sign("upload", { person, id }, 30 min)`), as before.
     - `key`: 32 bytes, the ticket's own: `HMAC-SHA256(the instance's key, "upload-key" ‖ ticket)`.
       The service keeps nothing: it works the key out again from the ticket on each request.
     - The key is only given over HTTPS (the request came through a trusted proxy that says
       `https`, `Client.secure`); otherwise the answer has no addresses, and the uploads go by the
       usual address. A key never travels in the clear.
  2. **Telling our server apart, on the home network.** The plugin sends each address a random
     challenge with the ticket (`GET http://…/api/library/upload/hello?c=…`, `Authorization:
     Upload <ticket>`). The server answers `HMAC(key, "hello" ‖ c)`; only ours knows the key. The
     ticket travels in the clear here, but without the key it is worth nothing: it opens no route,
     and an upload must be encrypted with the key (below).
  3. **Each song, encrypted.** `POST http://…/api/library/upload/sealed`, `Authorization: Upload
     <ticket>`, no query (the song's path is inside, encrypted). The body:
     - a header: `JBU1` and a random 8-byte prefix, new for each song;
     - then pieces sealed with AES-256-GCM, each its own nonce (the prefix ‖ the piece's number, 4
       bytes) and a mark in its associated data for the last piece (so a cut-off upload is
       refused): first a piece with `{ path, shelf }` as JSON, then the song in pieces of 64 KiB;
     - the service opens each piece as it arrives, writes the song to `tmp/` as now, and refuses
       the whole upload (and removes what it wrote) at the first piece that does not open.
     - Its answer (`added`, `existing`…) carries `HMAC(key, prefix ‖ answer)` in a header, which
       the plugin checks, so no one on the network can change what the report says.
     - On the network only sizes and times show: not the songs, nor their names or folders.
     - Someone who recorded an upload and sends it again within the ticket's 30 minutes would only
       bring the same song to the same place: the library finds it there already ("existing").
  4. **Where the code goes, with no new dependency.** The service: `openssl` (already one of its
     dependencies) for AES-256-GCM; `hmac` and `sha2` (already there) for the key and the proofs.
     The phone: Android's own `javax.crypto` (`AES/GCM/NoPadding`, `HmacSHA256`, from Android 4.4).
     All of it in the plugin, which is why, in the Android app, "Upload songs" also opens Android's
     own picker (`ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT`, several audio files) and its files go through the plugin's
     uploader, as a folder's do. The page never encrypts anything.
  5. **By the usual address** (away from home, or no address answers): the plugin sends the song
     as is to `POST /api/library/upload` with the session's bearer, over the tunnel's HTTPS, as in
     point 1. Mid-batch, if the home address stops answering, the rest go this way.
  6. As before: the dialog says "Sending over the home network"; a batch that outlasts the ticket
     asks for another; nothing changes where the app already speaks to a home address.
  - **Checks.** The service's tests: the key and the ticket; `hello`'s proof; a sealed upload
    added; one with a piece changed, one cut off, one with another ticket's key, and an expired
    ticket, all refused with nothing left in `tmp/`; the answer's proof. A worked example (key,
    prefix, a short song, the bytes it becomes) written into the tests and into a comment in the
    Kotlin uploader, so both sides follow the same bytes; the Kotlin side can only be checked on the
    phone (no Android SDK here).
- **The home network as an option, in the interface and in the setup wizard** (the owner's ask).
  - The interface already has it (People and sign-in…, "Home network", owners). In a container,
    beside it, the address on the home network (`http://192.168.1.20:4646`), a new setting (`lan`),
    since there the service cannot see it; `JACKBIRD_LAN` sets it too.
  - `jackbird-service setup` asks, once sign-in is on: "Reach jackbird from the home network too?
    (y/N)", saying that on the home network sign-in codes and sessions travel unencrypted (the app's
    uploads do not), and that passkeys need HTTPS. In a container it also asks the address on the
    home network, and says to publish the port there in `compose.yaml` (`4646:4646` in place of

## 2026-11-19 01:10 UTC — Proposal: updating the server from the app (the owner's question; awaiting their approval)

- **The owner.** Could the server be updated from the client's interface?
- **What there is.** `jackbird-service update` on the server's machine (it puts the binary that
  runs it in place of the installed one and restarts it), and, in the desktop app, Preferences,
  Service, for the service it installed on that computer. Nothing from another device.
- **The proposal.**
  1. Preferences, **Server**, for owners: the server's version (`/api/info`) and the latest
     release's, and an **Update** key when they differ.
  2. `POST /api/update` (owners): the service downloads its own system's binary from the latest
     release, checks it against a `SHA256SUMS` the release publishes (`service.yml` would add it),
     puts it in place of its own and restarts itself through its service manager (systemd, launchd,
     Windows' service manager), as `update` does. The page waits for `/api/info` to answer with
     the new version.
  3. Where it cannot: in a container (an image, not a binary: the section shows
     `docker compose pull && docker compose up -d`), and installed "with the system", whose binary
     belongs to the administrator while the service runs as the person (the section says to run
     `jackbird-service update` there).
  - It needs an HTTP client in the service, to download (a new dependency, `ureq` with rustls, say):
    the owner's approval first.

### 2026-11-19 01:36 UTC — Updating the server from the app: approved ("Claro, dale"); the plan as it is built

- **The release** (`release.yml`): a `SHA256SUMS` beside the service's binaries (each one's
  SHA-256 and name), attached with them.
- **The service** (`crates/service/src/update.rs`, owners' routes):
  - `GET /api/update`: its version, the latest release's (asked of GitHub's releases API, kept an
    hour) and whether it can update itself (`can`): `yes`; `container` (`JACKBIRD_LISTEN=0.0.0.0`:
    an image is updated by pulling it); `system` (installed with the system: its binary is the
    administrator's); `manual` (not the installed binary: started by hand); `readonly` (its folder
    cannot be written).
  - `POST /api/update`: the asset for its system (`jackbird-service-linux-<arch>-static`,
    `-macos-<arch>`, `-windows-<arch>.exe`, as `service.yml` and `ship.yml` name them), its SHA-256
    checked against `SHA256SUMS` (refused without one), written beside its own binary and moved
    into its place (on Windows the running one is moved aside first, as Windows allows); then it
    answers and, a second later, exits with a failure code, so its service manager starts the new
    binary (systemd's `Restart=on-failure`, launchd's `KeepAlive`; Task Scheduler's restart on
    failure, which waits a minute). Logged as `update`.
  - Its HTTPS client: no new crate. `rustls` 0.23.46, `tokio-rustls` 0.26.6 and
    `rustls-native-certs` 0.8.5, already built for email, become direct dependencies with the
    features already in use (`ring`, no `aws-lc-rs`), and a short HTTP/1.1 GET of its own
    (redirects followed, chunked bodies read). The response's parsing, the asset's name, the sums
    and the versions are pure functions, tested.
- **The app** (Preferences, Server, owners): the server's version; the latest when it is newer,
  with **Update to …** where it can, or what to do where it cannot (the container's
  `docker compose pull && docker compose up -d`; `jackbird-service update` as an administrator
  with the new binary; where it was started by hand). Updating, it waits for `/api/info` to answer
  with the new version (three minutes at most), then says so.

### 2026-11-19 01:36 UTC — Done: updating the server from the app

(The plan above headed "01:36" was written at 01:26.)

- **Changed from the plan.** GitHub's API turned out to be the wrong door: unknown callers may ask
  it 60 times an hour per address, and this check's shared address had spent them (a 403). The
  service now reads the latest release from where `github.com/…/releases/latest` leads (its
  `Location`, `…/releases/tag/v0.1.36`) and downloads `…/releases/download/<tag>/<file>`, which
  have no such limit.
- **The service** (`crates/service/src/update.rs`, `owners()`): `GET /api/update`
  (`{ version, latest, newer, can, error? }`, the latest kept an hour) and `POST /api/update`
  (one at a time; refused with `409` where it cannot or need not, `502` when the download or its
  check fails, nothing written then). Its situation: `situation()` (pure: the container by
  `JACKBIRD_LISTEN`, then the running binary compared with `install::binary_path` for each way,
  then whether its folder can be written). The download: SHA256SUMS, then the binary, its SHA-256
  compared, `put_in_place()` (beside it, executable, moved in; on Windows the running one moved
  aside first); logged as `update`; then it answers and exits with 75 a second later, which
  systemd (`Restart=on-failure`), launchd (`KeepAlive`) and Task Scheduler (restart on failure)
  each take as a reason to start it again. Its HTTPS: `fetch()`, a GET over `tokio-rustls` with the
  system's certificates, redirects followed, `Connection: close`, read to the end (a server's
  missing TLS goodbye tolerated: the length, the chunks and the checksum tell a short answer).
  `rustls`, `tokio-rustls` and `rustls-native-certs` are direct dependencies now at the versions
  and features email already built: `Cargo.lock` gains three edges and no package.
- **The release** (`release.yml`): `SHA256SUMS` of the service's binaries, attached with them. So
  the first release that can be updated to from the app is the next one; until it is out, the
  key's answer is "the release has no SHA256SUMS".
- **The app** (`lib/server-update.ts`, `views/preferences-dialog.html`): Server, for owners,
  **Version**: "It runs 0.1.35; 0.1.36 is out." and **Update to 0.1.36**, or what to do where it
  cannot; updating, it waits up to three minutes for `/api/info` to answer with the new version.
  Asked once each time the section is shown (an effect that read the update's state asked again
  after a failure and wiped its message; and one placed before `section` was defined broke the
  dialog: both found and fixed in the check below).
- `docs/install.md`.
- **Checked.** `cargo test -p jackbird-service` (43, and by hand the ignored
  `it_asks_github_and_downloads_through_a_redirect`: 0.1.36 read from the redirect and its
  21,212,016-byte Linux binary downloaded through GitHub's redirect); clippy; `npm test`. A copy
  built as 0.1.35, put where an install at logon puts it under a home of its own: `/api/update`
  said `can: yes`, `newer: true`; in Chromium as a phone, in Spanish, Server showed "Tiene la
  0.1.35; ya está la 0.1.36." and its key; the key answered "the release has no SHA256SUMS" and the
  binary was left as it was. Not checked: a real replacement and restart under a service manager
  (no release has the sums yet; `put_in_place` is tested on its own).

### 2026-11-19 01:45 UTC — The phone's spectrum ahead of the sound (the owner)

- **The owner.** Streaming, the spectrum seems to run ahead of what plays.
- **The cause.** On the phone the spectrum is taken from the PCM the sink hands Android
  (`BitPerfectSink.handleBuffer`), which is heard only once Android's output has played what it
  holds before it: a quarter of a second or so, more over Bluetooth. (The page's analyser sits on
  the audio element's output; the desktop's takes what its device takes, a few milliseconds ahead.)
- **The change** (`Spectrum.kt`, `BitPerfect.kt`): the ring keeps 2^18 samples (6 s at 44.1 kHz,
  1.4 s at 192) with the song's time just after its last one (the buffer's `presentationTimeUs`
  and the frames taken of it, a buffer taken in several calls counted from where it began). The
  sink's own clock (`getCurrentPositionUs`, which the player asks every few milliseconds while it
  plays and which counts what Android's output still holds) is noted as it is asked; the window
  analysed ends at the sample heard now (the clock carried on by the time since, at most 100 ms).
  With no clock yet, as before: the last samples.
- **Not checked here**: no Android build or device. To check on the phone: a song with a clear
  beat, over the speaker and over Bluetooth; the bars with the beats.

### 2026-11-19 01:45 UTC — Proposals (the owner's asks; awaiting their answers)

**A. "Add to my library" from someone's music shared with one.**

- **Where.** The menus of a folder and of a track (and the selection's) in a tab of someone's music
  shared with one (SH-D6), for editors and up.
- **What.** The songs join one's own library as the same stored files: a new song on one's shelf
  for each, with the same blob (no copy, no space), the same file tags, and the version the sharer's
  library shows (their family version) as one's own library's. Already there (the same audio on
  one's shelf): "already there". A stored file is deleted only once no song on any shelf uses it
  (the trash already counts so), so either library can drop it without the other losing it.
- **The service.** `POST /api/library/adopt { shelf, paths }` (a shelf shared with the caller) →
  the same report as an upload's; `Library::adopt` in `crates/store`.
- **Questions.** (1) Where they land: the same folders as in the sharer's library, or under a folder
  named after them ("From Ana/…")? (2) Their version of the tags, or the file's own?

**B. The server and the quota at the foot of Folders.**

- **What there is.** No quota: the service knows each song's size, not a limit.
- **Proposal.** A quota per person, set by owners in People and sign-in (none by default). At the
  foot of a server library's Folders view (Folders only): the server's name and "3.2 GB of 50 GB"
  (or "3.2 GB" without a quota) with a thin meter; a tap opens its details (in Details): the
  server (name, address, version), one's songs and their size (by format), the quota; for owners
  the disk's size and free space and the last nightly snapshot (F4).
- **The service.** `GET /api/library/usage` → `{ songs, bytes, quota, disk? }`; the quota a column
  of people, set through the people's API (owners).
- **Questions.** (1) A quota per person, or one for everyone? (2) A song in two libraries (A above)
  counts in each, or once? (3) Over the quota: uploads refused, or only a warning?

**C. Emails with the app's look.**

- **What there is.** Plain text only (`crates/service/src/mail.rs`).
- **Proposal.** Each email in two parts, the text as now and HTML for the clients that show it
  (`multipart/alternative`, lettre's builder: no new crate), styled inline (mail clients drop
  stylesheets): a header like the faceplate (the brushed metal's gradient, the wordmark in letters);
  the code large, letter-spaced, in an LCD panel (the display's green and ink, a monospaced face),
  as text that can be copied; for an invitation, an **Open jackbird** key to the instance; a muted
  footer (how long the code lasts, that it can be ignored). Light colours, marked `color-scheme:
  light`, so a dark client does not invert them badly. The sender named "jackbird". Texts as now,
  escaped in the HTML (a name with `<`). Tested: both parts, the escaping; and the three emails

### 2026-11-19 01:56 UTC — Proposal: passkeys in the Android app, natively (the owner's question; awaiting their approval)

- **The owner.** Can passkeys be supported natively (in the app)?
- **Why not now.** The app's page runs in Android's webview, which has no WebAuthn: the app hides
  passkeys (`passkeysHere()`), and its notice says they need HTTPS, which misleads in the app.
- **How Android does it.** Its Credential Manager (`androidx.credentials`) makes and uses passkeys
  for an app as a browser does for a site, the same passkeys (Google Password Manager's): for the
  instance's domain (its RP ID) once the domain says the app may, with a Digital Asset Links file
  at `https://<domain>/.well-known/assetlinks.json` naming the app (`es.germade.jackbird`) and the
  SHA-256 of the certificate it is signed with. A passkey made in the browser then works in the
  app, and the other way round. The app's requests carry the origin
  `android:apk-key-hash:<that certificate's SHA-256, base64url>`, not the site's.
- **The proposal.**
  1. **The plugin** (Kotlin): `passkey_create` and `passkey_get`, each the service's options as
     WebAuthn's JSON in, Credential Manager's answer (the same JSON a browser's credential gives)
     out; a cancel is a cancel. New Gradle dependencies: `androidx.credentials:credentials` and
     `credentials-play-services-auth` (needs the owner's approval).
  2. **The page** (`lib/account.ts`): in the Android app, signing in with a passkey and adding one
     go through the plugin instead of `navigator.credentials`; the rest of the ceremony is the same.
     The notice in the app says what is missing when it is (no address set; an address over plain
     HTTP).
  3. **The service**: serves `/.well-known/assetlinks.json` (only while the instance's address is
     set), and accepts the app's origin beside the site's (`webauthn-rs`'
     `append_allowed_origin`: an exact match, checked here in its code). The certificate's SHA-256:
     the official APK's, kept in the repository (it is public: anyone can read it off the APK), and
     `JACKBIRD_ANDROID_CERTS` for one's own builds.
  4. **The release** (`phone-release.yml`): prints the certificate's SHA-256, to keep it in the
     repository once (an APK signed with a key made for its run, `-testkey`, cannot have passkeys).
- **What it needs of the instance.** Its address set (People and sign-in…, Sign-in) to an HTTPS
  name that Google can reach to read the file (a tunnel's address such as intergate's works); not
  the home network's `http://` address.
- **Later.** iOS has the same with Associated Domains (`apple-app-site-association`) and
  `ASAuthorization`, which needs an Apple developer team's identifier: not part of this.
- **Checks.** The service's tests: the file's content with and without an address, the app's origin
  accepted and another refused. The plugin and the page's path only on a phone: adding a passkey
  in the app, signing in with it, and with one made in Chrome.

**Plan D: the machine's load and temperatures in Preferences, Server** (the owner: *"en la vista de
detalles del servidor, me gustaría tener el uso de la CPU/GPU y las temperaturas"*).

- **Who.** Owners, as the disk (it says what the machine is doing, not one's music).
- **The service.** `GET /api/server/machine` (owners; others 403) →
  `{ cpu: { percent, cores }, gpus: [{ name, percent?, celsius? }], temperatures: [{ label, celsius }] }`;
  what a system does not tell is left out, never made up. Read as the disk is, from what each
  system offers:
  - **CPU use**: Linux `/proc/stat` (two readings, the service keeping the last so an answer needs
    no wait but the first's 200 ms); macOS and Windows as below.
  - **Temperatures**: Linux `/sys/class/hwmon/*/temp*_input` with their labels (`k10temp` Tctl,
    `coretemp` Package, `nvme` Composite, a Raspberry Pi's `cpu_thermal`), falling back on
    `/sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone*`.
  - **GPU**: NVIDIA through `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,utilization.gpu,temperature.gpu
    --format=csv,noheader,nounits` where it is installed (Linux, Windows); AMD on Linux from
    `/sys/class/drm/card*/device/gpu_busy_percent` and its hwmon. Intel's and Apple's: not shown
    (no reading without privileges).
  - **macOS and Windows**, a choice for the owner: **(a)** the `sysinfo` crate (CPU everywhere;
    temperatures on macOS from the SMC, on Windows only where WMI gives them, often not without
    administrator rights); its unsafe code is its own, the workspace's `forbid` stays; or **(b)** no
    crate, as `df`: Linux in full, macOS and Windows CPU only (`top -l 2`, `Get-CimInstance
    Win32_Processor`), no temperatures there.
  - In a Docker container (ship.yml's image): `/proc/stat` is the host's; hwmon only if `/sys` is
    mounted (it is by default, read-only); the GPU only with NVIDIA's runtime.
- **The app.** `lib/machine.ts` (a store, asked every 3 s while Preferences, Server is open, and not
  otherwise). In the Server section, for owners, under the disk: **Machine**: CPU "23 % · 8 cores"
  with a bar (`UsageBar`, max 100), each GPU with its bar and temperature, then the temperatures
  (at most six, the hottest first), in °C; the bar's tone `accent` from 80 °C or 90 %. Texts in
  English and Spanish. Formatting in a helper (`helpers/machine.ts`) with its tests.
- **Tests.** The readers on fixture files (a fake `/sys` and `/proc` in a temporary folder),
  `nvidia-smi`'s lines read (one GPU, two, `[N/A]`), the route for owners alone.

**Plan E: in Folders, one's own folder as the root** (the owner: *"la vista en modo Carpetas usará la
carpeta del usuario como root"*).

### 2026-11-19 09:19 UTC — Plan H: dropping files on the library (the owner's ask; awaiting their approval)

The owner: *"me gustaría poner soltar archivos en la UI, haría un área que cubra la biblioteca y
detalles, al hacer dropover mostraría un placeholder que cubra ese área"*; where: a server's
library, local folders and iPods, *"normalizando las etiquetas y los nombres de archivo"*; on a
server, at the library's root.

**The area.** One drop zone over the library and Details panes (`views/workspace.html`), live
only while files are dragged in from outside (`dataTransfer.types` holds `Files`; a row dragged
within the app does not wake it). Over it, a placeholder covering both panes: a dashed frame, an
icon, and what a drop will do: "Drop to add to <name>" (the active root), or why it cannot: music
shared with you, a folder the browser can only read, an iPod that cannot be written, nothing open.
It goes on `dragleave` out of the area, on `drop` and on Escape. Folders dropped are walked
(`webkitGetAsEntry`), their structure read for the normalization below; only MP3 and FLAC are
taken, the rest counted in the report as left out.

**The desktop app.** Tauri catches files dropped on its window before the page; its handler is
turned off (`disable_drag_drop_handler` on the window, where it is built) so the page receives
them as in a browser. Android: no drag and drop from outside; nothing changes there.

**Normalized as they land** (one helper, `helpers/normalize.ts`, with its tests; the tags written
into each file in the page by the wasm tag engine before it goes anywhere, so the three places
receive the same file):

- **Tags.** Spaces trimmed and collapsed; `3/12` → track 3 (the total kept where the format
  has one); disc likewise; the year cut to its four digits (`2010-05-03` → `2010`); title,
  artist, album, track, disc missing from the tags filled from the dropped path
  (`Artist - Album (Year)/03 - Title.flac`, the patterns the rename tab already reads); nothing
  else changed (no letter case).
- **Names.** `<Album artist or Artist> - <Album> (<Year>)/<NN> - <Title>.<ext>`, as the owner's
  library is laid out; `<D>-<NN>` with more than one disc; "Various Artists" for a compilation;
  "(<Year>)" left out without one; characters no system allows in a name (`/ \ : * ? " < > |`)
  replaced by `-`. A song whose tags and path do not give an artist and a title keeps its
  dropped name, under `Unsorted/`.
- **Where.** A server: the root of one's own music (" (2)" where taken; the same audio already
  there: "already there", as uploads). A local folder (the desktop app, or a browser folder it may
  write): its root, by the same names (a name taken: " (2)"); the copy is new, the original
  untouched. An iPod: sent as the send to iPod does (the iPod names its files itself: the tags
  only).
- **Report.** The import dialog's progress and report, as uploads: added, already there, left
  out (and why).

**Questions.** (1) That layout of names, or the rename tab's pattern last used for the file and a
fixed one for the folder? (2) An MP3 or FLAC whose tags the normalization would change: change
them (the dropped file, as it lands, never the original), as proposed?

### 2026-11-19 — Sheets past the screen's edge in the Android app; the drop dialog's order

- **The cause** (seen in Chromium as a Pixel 7: the page 412px wide, what fixed things are laid out
  in 711px). The display's unseen copies of its lines (`.line-sizer`, absolute, unwrapped, to know
  whether a line must scroll) ran out to the right of the page with a long title; the page grew
  wider than the phone, and Android's webview then widens what anything fixed is laid out in, to
  the page's width: a sheet (a `<dialog>`, the whole "width") went past the right edge, its handle
  off centre, and a drag started off where it showed. The toasts, in `vw`, went with it.
- **Fixed**: the copies are held at their row's right (`right: 0`): they run out to the left,
  which adds no width to a page. And the viewport says `minimum-scale=1.0` (`index.html`): with
  anything else wider than the phone, the webview no longer widens its viewport for it (checked:
  a 900px block, 901px without it, 412px with it; zooming in is still allowed).
- **Checked** in Chromium as a Pixel 7: the page 412px wide, Preferences' sheet from 0 to 412px,
  dragged down by touch (it follows, 80px) and springing back. Not checked on the phone itself.
- **The drop dialog** (the owner): the songs ready for their place first, then the notice of those
  lacking tags and the pattern to read them by, then those songs, with their inputs. A song whose
  last missing tag is typed moves up to the ready ones.

### 2026-11-19 — The drop dialog's bar within each upload: XMLHttpRequest (the owner)

- **Why not `fetch`.** A streamed body (`duplex: "half"`, as fetch-extras' `withUploadProgress`
  does) is Chromium's alone, and over HTTP/2 or 3 only: not Safari, Firefox, the desktop app's
  WebKit nor the iOS app, and not a server at home on plain HTTP/1.1. HTTP/2 through intergate
  (Traefik) would serve Chrome outside only; HTTPS at home (a name for the home address, its
  certificate by DNS) or a jackbird on a VPS in front of the home one were weighed and left: the
  first is a project of its own, the second sends what is added at home out and back in.
- **Done.** `uploadToLibrary` (`lib/sources/service.ts`) sends by XMLHttpRequest, to the
  address and with the session `uploadTarget` gives, its answer read by `uploadAnswer` (a refusal
  a TagError, a 401 asking to sign in, as before); not reached, a TypeError, as `fetch`'s was
  (`importing.ts` tells "not reached" by it). A fourth argument, `progress`, hears the bytes sent
  (`upload.onprogress`). The drop dialog's bar adds the song's bytes sent (`dropping.bytesSent`)
  to those done. "Upload songs" (`importing.ts`) sends the same way, without a bar yet. The Android
  app's plugin uploads are as they were.
- **Checked**: the type check, the build, the layer check. **Not checked**: an upload to a running
  server (none built here); to check: the bar moving within a large file, a refusal, signing in
  again after a 401, the home address unreached and the other one taken.

Commit: `feat(library): uploads by XMLHttpRequest, so the drop dialog's bar moves within each file`

### 2026-11-19 — The macOS app tells a DAC that is the system's default (the owner)

- **Why it did not.** On a computer the display's DAC mark showed only for a device chosen in
  the preferences (`soundingDac`, `lib/output.ts`): the system's default was taken for the
  computer's own output. On a Mac the DAC is usually the default, so it never showed.
- **Now.** The native player's route says whether the device is a DAC of one's own (`Route.dac`,
  `crates/audio`, `#[serde(default)]`): on macOS, by its transport type (CoreAudio's
  `kAudioDevicePropertyTransportType`, through coreaudio-rs's `get_device_transport_type`): USB,
  Thunderbolt, FireWire, PCI or AVB are; built-in, Bluetooth, AirPlay, HDMI, DisplayPort,
  aggregate and virtual devices are not. The default output's or the chosen one's (by name).
  `soundingDac` takes `route.dac`, and still a chosen device where the system does not say
  (Windows, Linux; there `dac` is false).
- **Checked**: `cargo check` and `clippy` of the audio crate for `aarch64-apple-darwin` (its macOS
  code), its tests on Linux, fmt, the type check and the build. **Not checked** on a Mac: a USB
  DAC as the default output showing the mark; the built-in speakers and AirPods not.

### 2026-11-19 — Android: a USB DAC no longer pauses the music as the screen goes off (the owner)

- **The report.** With a USB DAC (HiBy FC3, NICEHCK NK1 MAX) the music paused as soon as the
  screen went off, and ▶ played it again; through the speaker or Bluetooth it went on.
- **The likely cause** (not seen in a log): Android's "becoming noisy" broadcast, which it sends
  when the sound is about to come out of the speaker; some phones reset USB audio as the screen
  goes off, and Media3's own handling (`setHandleAudioBecomingNoisy(true)`) paused.
- **Now** (`PlayerService.kt`): Media3's handling off, and the service's own receiver: it pauses at
  once, as before, then looks for a USB DAC every second for five; there again, it plays on
  (unless the person played or paused meanwhile). With none, it stays paused, as on unplugging a
  headset. Each step is logged (`jackbird`, "becoming noisy: …"), to see in logcat whether this is
  the cause.
- **Risk**: a DAC unplugged that Android still lists a second later would play on through the
  speaker.
- **Checked**: the mobile workflow (`[ci mobile]`). **Not checked**: on the phone with the screen
  off.

Commit: `fix(android): a USB DAC that blinks as the screen goes off no longer pauses the music [ci mobile]`

### 2026-11-19 — Plan (approved by the owner): a server's library in one answer

- **Why it loads bit by bit.** A library of the server is opened as a folder: the app's
  `.meta/mp3-manager.json` (kept in the library by the app), the walk, then `library/inspect`
  for each song not in it or changed, six at a time, each reading the song's head and its cover on
  the server. The `.meta/` misses often (songs uploaded elsewhere or imported by the server, one's
  own layers, a listener who cannot write it), and through a tunnel each answer is a round trip.
- **Server.** `GET /library/tracks` (`?shelf=`): every song as the person sees it, in one JSON:
  its path, size, date, checksums (the version with the layers), tags, its audio (length, rates)
  and its cover by reference (`jackbird-cover:<name>`, the name the app gives a picture). The
  audio and the file's cover's name are kept in memory by blob (a blob's bytes never change), so
  a second answer reads no file. Gzip, and an `ETag` (a `304` when nothing changed).
  `GET /library/cover` (`?path=`, `?shelf=`): the picture a song shows as the person sees it,
  cached by the browser (its URL holds its name).
- **App.** A library source has `tracks()`; `hydrateRoot` takes the songs from it in place of an
  `inspect` each (the walk stays, for the playlists); a cover named by the server is read from
  `/library/cover` when shown (`lib/covers.ts`). The `.meta/` stays for folders and iPods.
- **Tests**: the store's `tracks_as` (the layers, a shelf's, the cover's name), the endpoint
  (its `304`), and the app's helpers.

### 2026-11-19 — Done: a server's library in one answer

- **`jackbird_tags`**: `Cover::name` (the interface's name for a picture; `jackbird_api::cover_name`
  calls it), so the store names covers too.
- **The store** (`crates/store`): `Library::tracks_as(viewer)`, every song as `inspect_as` finds
  it (`Listed`: its path, stat, both checksums, tags, cover by name, audio). Each blob's audio and
  its own cover's name kept in memory (`heads`), and a layer's cover's name by its hash (`named`):
  a second listing reads no file (a layer that removes the cover still reads the song).
  `cover_as(path, viewer)`: the picture a song shows. Not stored in the catalogue, unlike the
  plan's "in the database": the blob's bytes never change, so memory is enough and needs no migration.
- **The service** (`library_api.rs`): `GET /library/tracks` (`?shelf=`): `TrackJson`, the paths
  the shelf's, the cover `jackbird-cover:<name>`; gzipped (`CompressionLayer`, tower-http's
  `compression-gzip`), an `ETag` and `private, no-cache`, a `304` for the same. `GET
  /library/cover` (`?path=`, `?shelf=`, `&name=`): the picture, `immutable`.
- **The app**: `RootSource.tracks` and `.cover` (`types.ts`, `lib/sources/service.ts`); in
  `hydrateRoot` (`lib/roots.ts`) the listing comes with the walk, a song's version that differs
  from the `.meta/`'s makes it stale, and each stale or new one is taken from the listing (an
  `inspect` only for one it lacks, or when the listing fails); the covers it names are fetched
  from `/library/cover` when shown (`coverFrom`, `lib/covers.ts`).
- **Checked**: the store's tests (`tests/tracks.rs`: every song as inspect finds it, one's own
  layer, a cover set and removed), the service's (`the_whole_library_comes_in_one_answer…`: as
  inspect, the cover from its address, the `304`, a retitle changing the `ETag`, gzip, an unshared
  shelf refused), clippy and fmt; `npm run test:types`, layers, styles, the unit tests (483 of 485:
  the two wasm tests need the wasm build, which does not build here); the service run here with
  the web build and seven songs, opened in Chromium as a Pixel 7, twice: one walk, one `tracks`,
  no `inspect`, the cover fetched once and drawn, the second time revalidated (a `304`).
  **Not checked**: a large library through the tunnel; editing a song's cover in the app against
  the new path (the save is unchanged).

Commit: `feat(library): the server's whole library in one answer, covers by their own address [ci mobile]`

### 2026-11-19 — CI: the Android build's buildSrc no longer hangs on the plugin portal alone

The mobile workflow's Android build failed twice today (14:00 on f3ef83b, 16:07 on 1af29e8 in
the PR's run, the same commit passing in the push's) before compiling anything: buildSrc's
`kotlin-dsl` could not resolve `kotlin-stdlib:2.3.21` "in https://plugins.gradle.org/m2". The
buildSrc that `tauri android init` makes has no `settings.gradle.kts`, so its plugins come from
the portal alone, which hands Kotlin on from Maven Central and sometimes fails to (both serve
those files, checked). `src-tauri/android/buildSrc.settings.gradle.kts` (`pluginManagement`: the
portal, Maven Central, Google) is copied in after `init` in `mobile.yml` and `phone-release.yml`.
Checked: a buildSrc with `kotlin-dsl` and that file configured here (Gradle 8.14); the workflows
parse.

Commit: `fix(ci): the Android build's buildSrc takes its plugins from Maven Central too`

### 2026-11-19 — CI: Maven Central through Google's mirror (the release's APK failed the same way)

The earlier fix (buildSrc's `pluginManagement` with Maven Central and Google) did not hold: the
0.1.39 release's APK failed with Gradle having looked in all three ("Could not find
kotlin-stdlib:2.3.21" in the portal, Maven Central and Google). The cause, seen here: Maven
Central answered `kotlin-stdlib-2.3.21.pom` with **429 Too Many Requests** and a moment later with
200 (the files have been there since April). It limits by address; a runner's is shared, and
Gradle reports a 429 as "Could not find"; the portal sends Kotlin on to Central and fails the same.
- `src-tauri/android/maven-central-mirror.init.gradle.kts`: an init script, copied into
  `~/.gradle/init.d/` by `mobile.yml` and `phone-release.yml` before the build, which points every
  `mavenCentral()` of every build (the app, its plugins, buildSrc) at Google's mirror of Central
  (`maven-central.storage-download.googleapis.com/maven2`, which serves these files).
- buildSrc's settings put Maven Central (so, the mirror) first, the portal for plugin markers.
- **Checked** here with Gradle 8.14, a buildSrc with `kotlin-dsl`, a fresh Gradle home and
  `--refresh-dependencies`: Kotlin fetched from the mirror alone, the build successful; the
  workflows parse. The release is to be run again once it is merged.

Commit: `fix(ci): Maven Central through Google's mirror for the Android build (Central's 429s)`

  more than 720px): each step opened (an open level, Details in view, a dialog, sheet or menu,
  in the order they opened) is one entry of the browser's history. Opened in the app, an entry
  is pushed; closed in the app, as many are gone back over; the browser's back (Android's
  gesture or key) closes the top step. A dialog is asked with an `mm-dismiss` event, which
  `Modal`, `SubModal` and `Menu` take as Escape: its owner decides (an import busy stays, and its
  entry is pushed again once it has had the time to go).

  entries; back leaves the album, the ‹ the artist; a level's name opens Details and back returns;
  back closes the preferences and a row's menu; at 1280px the history is not touched. Not tried

The owner's request: an automatic list of the songs liked, liked from a heart (empty / full) at
the right of the title on the display.

1. **Where the likes live** — a playlist file of the open tab's own, `Liked.m3u8` at the folder's
   root, written through `PlaylistStore` (lib/playlists.ts) like any other: it goes wherever the
   folder goes (local, cloud, the service, an iPod sync), other players read it, and nothing new
   is stored. Its file name is fixed (not translated), so it is found again whatever the
   language; the library shows it by its translated name ("Liked" / "Me gusta").
   (Alternatives: a rating written into each file's tags, POPM / FMPS_Rating, which rewrites the
   audio files; or the app's own storage, IndexedDB, per device and lost with it.)

   outline heart, full (in the accent) when the song playing is in the list; a tap toggles it.

   and line-sizer leave its room. A label for each state (`likes.like`, `likes.unlike`).
3. **The list** — `lib/likes.ts`: `isLiked(trackId)` (a Set of the list's paths, derived from the
   root's playlists, not a `$computed` of a `$computed`), `toggleLike(trackId)` (adds at the end,
   or takes the entry out; the file made on the first like). In the Playlists view the list is
   first, with a heart instead of the playlist icon, and can be played, reordered and edited like
   any other.

### 2026-11-23 — Android: paused, then back in the app, the music would not play again (the owner)

- **The owner.** On Android, paused and back in the app later, ▶ often did nothing.
- **The cause.** Paused, Media3's service is no longer in the foreground, and Android stops a
  background app's service a minute or so later: `PlayerService.onDestroy` releases the player and
  its queue. Back in the app, ▶ (`play`) started the service again, with a new, empty player:
  `prepare()` and `play()` on no songs played nothing, while the page took the call's answer as
  playing (its ‖ shown, its polls ignored for another key). The next press paused it. And had
  Android refused to start the service (the app in the background), `PlayerService.withPlayer`'s
  call would have waited for ever, holding up every call of the page after it (`#calls` runs them
  one after the other).
- **What changed.**
  - `PlayerPlugin.kt`: the queue as the player last had it (its songs, the one it is on, where)
    is kept at each change (set, upcoming, pause, seek, play or pause from anywhere, next song),
    and a player made anew gets it back, paused where it was, before the call's work; `stop`
    forgets it.
  - `PlayerService.kt` `withPlayer`: a service that cannot start tells every call waiting for it,
    which is rejected, and the next call tries again.
  - `lib/phone-audio.ts`: a play that fails leaves it paused (the next press plays); back on
    screen (`visibilitychange`), it asks the player where it is and whether it plays.
- **Not checked on a phone here** (no Android SDK in this session): the `mobile` workflow builds
  it and plays a queue in an emulator with the app in the background (`[ci mobile]`).

Commit: `fix(phone): paused and back in the app, the player gets its queue back and plays again`

### 2026-11-23 — Proposal: how one song follows another (the owner's ask; awaiting their decisions)

- **The owner.** Three ways to play, to choose from: **as now**, **gapless** (no pause between
  songs), and **crossfaded**.
- **How it is now, by player.**
  - **The browser** (`lib/player.ts`, one `<audio>`): the next song is loaded when the one playing
    ends (`ended` → `goTo`), so there is a short silence between them.
  - **The desktop's native output** (`crates/audio`, `engine.rs`): already gapless when the next
    song has the same format (it is handed ahead: `set_next`); with another format, the output is
    opened again for it (a short break).
  - **Android** (Media3, `PlayerPlugin.kt`): it has the queue ahead, and ExoPlayer goes from one to
    the next without a pause (an MP3's encoder padding is cut only when the file says how much).
- **The proposal.**
  - A setting in Preferences › Sound, `preferences.transition`: `normal` | `gapless` |
    `crossfade`, saved as `jackbird.transition`, `normal` by default; with crossfade, its length
    (see the decisions below). Shared by the three players.
  - **The browser.** *Gapless*: a second `<audio>` element loads the next song ahead and is started
    at the first one's end, both through the existing AudioContext (the analyser follows the one
    sounding). Not sample-exact (a few milliseconds at most), but no audible pause. *Crossfade*:
    the same two elements, each through a `GainNode`; the next one starts the crossfade's length
    before the end, one fading out as the other fades in (equal-power curves).
  - **The desktop.** *Gapless*: as now, and a song of another format is resampled to the one
    sounding rather than reopening the output (the shared output only; exclusive and bit-perfect
    still reopen it). *Crossfade*: the engine decodes the next song alongside for the length of
    the fade and mixes the two into the pipe; never bit-perfect during the fade (the route says
    "mixer" meanwhile).
  - **Android.** *Gapless*: as now. *Crossfade*: a second ExoPlayer in the service plays the next
    song for the length of the fade, their volumes ramped, then becomes the one the session
    controls. Bit-perfect to a USB DAC cannot mix two players: with it on, the crossfade is off.
  - Neither gapless nor crossfade applies while repeating one song or after a manual skip (the
    next song starts at once, as now).
- **To decide** (asked in the conversation): the crossfade's length; whether songs of the same
  album crossfade or follow gapless; the order of work by player.

#### 2026-11-23 — The owner's decisions

- The crossfade's length is **adjustable, 1 to 12 seconds**, 6 by default.
- Songs **always** crossfade, those of one album too.
- **The three players at once**, in one pull request.

#### 2026-11-23 — Done: Standard, Gapless and Crossfade, in the three players

- **The setting.** Preferences › Sound (now shown everywhere), "Between songs": Standard |
  Gapless | Crossfade (`helpers/transition.ts`, tested; `preferences.transition`,
  `preferences.crossfadeSeconds`, saved as `jackbird.transition` and `jackbird.crossfadeSeconds`);
  with Crossfade, its length, 1 to 12 s (6 by default). On the desktop's native output and on
  Android, a line says when a song follows without a fade (below).
- **The browser** (`lib/twin-audio.ts`, the embedded output's player now): two `<audio>`
  elements, shaped like one for `lib/player.ts`, as the native players are. With Gapless or
  Crossfade, the next song is **loaded ahead** in the second element (`setNext`, from
  `sendNext`). Gapless: a timer set from `timeupdate` starts it as the first ends (its `ended`, if
  the timer is late). Crossfade: it starts `overlapOf(seconds, both lengths)` before the end (at
  most half of either song), and the two cross at equal power (`fadeLevels`) through a `GainNode`
  each (the elements' volume where there is no Web Audio). The queue moves on as it starts
  ("advance", as the native players say it); the media of the song ending is released once it is
  let go. A pause pauses both; a seek, or another song chosen, ends the fade at once. Standard is
  as it was: nothing loaded ahead, the next song loaded at the end.
- **The desktop** (`crates/audio`): `Transition` (`Normal`, `Gapless`, `Crossfade { seconds }`),
  `Player::set_transition`, the app's `audio_transition` command (`NativeAudio.setTransition`).
  Gapless is what it already did. Crossfade: the engine holds the song's last frames back (`tail`)
  and mixes them with the next song's first ones (equal power) as it decodes, in the same opening
  of the output; a next song changed before the mix sounds takes it back, and the tail is mixed
  again with the new one. Only between songs of the same rate and channels, and no more bits
  (fewer are shifted up, exactly); another follows as before (the output opens again). Tests:
  the mix, sample by sample, with the capture output; a next song changed after the mix; another
  rate followed unfaded.
- **Android** (`crates/player`): `set_transition` (the crossfade's length; 0 for the other two:
  ExoPlayer's playlist already goes on without a pause). `PlayerService` watches the song's end;
  a crossfade's seconds before it, it makes a second player with the songs after it, silent,
  moves the session (the notification, the lock screen) and the page to it (`switched` →
  `PlayerPlugin` sends "advance"), and crosses their volumes while the first plays its last
  seconds, then lets it go. The song ending gives up the audio focus first, or the new one would
  pause it. A pause, a seek, another queue or a stop during the fade end it. Not with "Bit-perfect
  to a USB DAC" on.
- **Not done from the proposal:** resampling a song of another rate into the desktop's open
  output for a gapless change. A song of another format still opens the output again, as before.
- **Checked.** In Chromium, with the sample music and a 3-second crossfade: the next song starts
  3 s before the end, both play, their levels cross (0.97/0.23 … 0.27/0.96) and the song ending
  is let go, the third song then loaded ahead; Gapless changes element at the end; Standard keeps
  one element. A pause during the fade pauses both and the fade goes on from where it was; a song
  chosen during it ends it. `npm test`, `cargo test -p jackbird-audio` and `make desktop-test`
  pass. Android's side is built and checked in CI's emulator only (no Android SDK here); the
  crossfade itself needs a phone.

Commit: `feat(playback): Standard, Gapless or Crossfade between songs, the next one loaded ahead, in the browser, the desktop and Android`

#### 2026-11-23 — Resampling on the desktop (the owner's ask)

- **The owner.** Add the resampling left out above: a song of another rate following without the
  output opening again.
- **`crates/audio/src/convert.rs`** (`Converter`): a song's samples made into the open output's
  format, block by block. Only the depth upward: shifted, exactly (not a conversion). Mono to
  both sides of stereo, stereo to its mean (other layouts: not converted). The rate: `rubato`
  5.1.0's synchronous FFT resampler (MIT, pure Rust, a new dependency of `jackbird-audio` only),
  its delay taken off the start and its padding off the end, so the song keeps its length to the
  frame at the new rate. Tests: the exact shift, the channels, a 1 kHz sine from 44.1/16 to
  96/24 within one 16-bit step of the ideal (the worst sample is 192 at 24 bits, about −93 dBFS),
  the same output whatever the blocks it is given in.
- **The engine.** With Gapless or Crossfade, **in the shared mode**, a next song of another
  format is converted and follows in the same opening (gapless, or mixed with the tail); its
  `started` event says its own format and a route `converted`. In the exclusive mode each song
  keeps its own rate (the point of it), so one of another rate still opens the output again, as
  with Standard. A seek in a converted song opens the output again at its own rate.
- Tests (`tests/player.rs`): gapless 96 kHz → 44.1 kHz in one opening, the first song bit for bit
  and the second at 96 kHz to the frame; the same crossfaded; exclusive still reopening. The
  test that expected a crossfade to another rate to reopen now expects it converted.
- Preferences › Sound's line for the native output says so (shared: resampled; exclusive: a short
  break, no fade), for Gapless and Crossfade.
- `cargo test -p jackbird-audio`, clippy (with and without `device`), `make desktop-test` and
  `npm test` pass.

Commit: `feat(audio): gapless and crossfade across sample rates on the desktop's shared output, resampled with rubato`

### 2026-11-24 — The sign-in check read an email half written (the owner)

- **The failure.** The same release's `signin-check.cjs` (run by `service.yml`, from
  `release.yml`): `Unterminated string in JSON at position 2582`, just after the owner's
  invitations were sent.
- **The cause.** `scripts/smtp-sink.mjs`, another process, appends each email to `mail.jsonl` as
  a line; the check reads the file every 100 ms while it waits for a code. An invitation is long
  (2.5 KB and more): read in the middle of its write, the last line was cut, and `JSON.parse`
  threw. `library-check.cjs` reads it the same way.
- **The fix.** Both checks' `mails()` take the whole lines only (up to the last newline); a line
  not ended yet is read the next time. The counts the checks keep (`mails().length` before a code
  is asked) count whole emails, as before.
- **Checked.** `signin-check.cjs` and `library-check.cjs` pass here with the service built from
  `main`; a mailbox ending in half a line reads as the emails before it.

Commit: `fix(checks): the sign-in and library checks read whole emails only, not one the SMTP sink is still writing`

### 2026-11-25 — Proposal: the rename tab completes albums from an online database (the owner)

- **The ask.** In the rename assistant (the selection dialog's Rename tab), look the album up in
  a database and complete what its tags lack.
- **What there is.** The tab works from the tags alone (`lib/rename.ts`, `helpers/rename.ts`):
  tags → name, or name → tags. Nothing looks anything up online. The family library's schema
  reserves MusicBrainz ids (RECORD/2026-08-11.musikmatch-rust-migration.WIP.md, D4) for later.
- **A first sketch, to be chosen by the owner.**
  - **The database: MusicBrainz** (free, no key, open data, its Cover Art Archive for covers).
    Discogs needs each person's token; it could come later behind the same interface.
  - **The match: by text** (the selection's album and artist, its number of tracks and their
    lengths to rank the releases), not by the sound (AcoustID needs a key and `fpcalc`).
  - **The flow:** a "Look up" key in the tab when the selection is one album (or one disc):
    a short list of candidate releases (title, artist, year, country, tracks, and how well the
    lengths agree); choosing one shows, per track, what would change (title, artist, album
    artist, number, disc, year, and optionally the cover) before anything is written; the
    writing goes through the existing `saveMetadata`, then the rename as today.
  - **Where it runs:** a helper turns MusicBrainz's JSON into a plan (pure, tested on saved
    responses as fixtures); `src/lib/lookup.ts` fetches (`musicbrainz.org/ws/2`, at most one
    request a second, a `User-Agent` naming the app, as MusicBrainz asks). The web app fetches
    from the page (MusicBrainz allows it); the desktop and Android apps the same, through the
    webview.
- **Questions for the owner** before any plan in detail: MusicBrainz alone to begin with? Only
  whole albums, or single songs too? Should it also bring the cover, and the MusicBrainz ids
  into the tags (`MUSICBRAINZ_*`)?

No commit: a proposal.

### 2026-11-25 — Proposal: a track's "Edit tags…" leaves the selection alone (the owner)

- **The ask.** "Edit tags…" from a track's ⋮ must not select the track.
- **What happens now.** `lib/menus.ts` → `lib/editor.ts` `openSelectionFor(trackId, tab)` calls
  `lib/workspace.ts` `selectUnlessChosen`: a track not chosen becomes the whole selection (the
  others unticked), because the selection dialog edits `selectedTracks`. "Track info", "Rename"
  and "Remove" go the same way.
- **The plan.**
  - `lib/ui.ts`: `selectionTargets: string[] | null`, the tracks the dialog works on when it was
    opened from a menu; null when it was opened from the selection bar (as now).
  - `lib/workspace.ts`: `dialogTracks` (a `$computed` from the source store, not from
    `selectedTracks`): the targets when set, else the selection. `openSelectionFor` sets the
    targets to `menuTargets(trackId)` (the selection when the track is in it, else that track
    alone) and no longer calls `selectUnlessChosen` (kept only if still used elsewhere).
  - The dialog and what it calls use `dialogTracks` in place of `selectedTracks`:
    `views/selection-dialog.html`, `lib/editor.ts` (the form, save), `lib/rename.ts` (the plan),
    and the removal (`removeSelectedTracks` gets the ids). Closing it clears the targets. The
    selection bar's count and boxes are untouched.
  - A track renamed or removed from the dialog leaves the targets as the selection does today
    (renamed ids followed, removed ones dropped).
- **To decide.** Only "Edit tags…", or also "Track info", "Rename" and "Remove" (the same dialog;
  proposed: all four, so the menu never changes the selection)?
- **To check.** `npm test`; `?embed`: two tracks chosen, the ⋮ of a third → Edit tags… edits
  that one, the two stay chosen; the ⋮ of a chosen one edits the two, as now.

Commit: `fix(menus): a track's menu opens the dialog on it without selecting it`

### 2026-11-25 — Plan: the rename tab completes albums from MusicBrainz and Discogs (the owner)

- **The owner's choice.** Both MusicBrainz and Discogs (the proposal above).
- **Checked from here (2026-11-25).** `musicbrainz.org/ws/2`, `coverartarchive.org` and
  `api.discogs.com` (a release, and `database/search`) all answer with
  `access-control-allow-origin: *`: the web app can ask them from the page, as the desktop and
  Android apps can from their webview. Discogs answered a search without a token here, though its
  documentation asks for one; its rate is 25 a minute without a token, 60 with one, and its
  pictures' addresses need one.
- **The flow, in the Rename tab.** A "Look up the album" key when the dialog's tracks are of one
  album (one album tag, or one folder):
  1. **Search.** The album and its artist from the tags (else from the folder's name), editable,
     and the source: MusicBrainz, Discogs, or both (the default: both, side by side in the list).
  2. **Candidates.** Up to ten releases: title, artist, year, country, format, how many tracks,
     and a score of how well they fit (the number of tracks, and each length against the file's,
     within 3 s). The best first.
  3. **The match.** Chosen one, each file paired with a track of the release (by number, else
     by length and title, helpers' work), and per file what would change: title, artist, album,
     album artist, track and disc (`n/total`), year, genre (Discogs' styles; MusicBrainz's genres).
     Each field has its box, ticked where it differs; a file left unpaired is said so.
  4. **Apply.** Through `saveMetadata`, as Edit does; then the rename's plan shows the new names,
     to be run as today (Rename) or not.
- **The code.**
  - `src/helpers/lookup.ts` (pure, tested on responses saved in `fixtures/lookup/`): a common
    `Release` from MusicBrainz's and Discogs' JSON; `scoreRelease` (tracks and lengths);
    `pairTracks`; `lookupPlan` (per file, the tags that change).
  - `src/lib/lookup.ts`: the requests, one source at a time at its pace (MusicBrainz 1 a second,
    Discogs 25 or 60 a minute), JSON only, a timeout, errors said in the tab. No `User-Agent` can be
    set from a page; MusicBrainz's `fmt=json` and Discogs' own address are enough.
  - `src/views/lookup-panel.html` (a view in the Rename tab) and `src/lib/locales/{en,es}.ts`.
  - **Discogs' token**: optional, in Settings → a "Discogs" field (stored as the other
    preferences, never sent anywhere but `api.discogs.com`).
- **Defaults I'd take unless the owner says otherwise.**
  - Whole albums (or discs) only, to begin with; a single song later.
  - The cover: offered as one more box (Cover Art Archive's front, 1200px; Discogs' first image
    with a token), unticked when the files already have one.
  - The ids: MusicBrainz's release, release group, artist and recording ids written to the tags
    (`MUSICBRAINZ_*` / `TXXX:MusicBrainz …`, as Picard writes them) and Discogs' release id
    (`DISCOGS_RELEASE_ID`), so a later lookup goes straight to the release; only if the tag crates
    already write those frames, else a step of its own.
- **Steps.** L1 helpers and fixtures; L2 MusicBrainz end to end; L3 Discogs; L4 cover and ids;
  each with `npm test` and a check in `?embed`.

No commit yet: waiting for the owner's approval of the plan and the defaults.

### 2026-11-25 — Album lookup, L1: the helpers and their fixtures (approved by the owner)

- **The owner.** "Ok, adelante con L1": the plan above and its defaults.
- **Fixtures.** `fixtures/lookup/` (README: each request): MusicBrainz's search for "OK Computer" by
  Radiohead and one of its releases with recordings; Discogs' search and its 2008 CD. As the
  services gave them (pretty-printed); both CC0.
- **`src/helpers/lookup.ts`** (no requests):
  - `Release` / `ReleaseTrack`, one shape for both sources. `musicBrainzSearch`,
    `musicBrainzRelease` (credits with their join phrases, media as discs, the length of the track
    or else its recording's, the genre most said of the release or else its group,
    capitalised), `discogsSearch` (the artist out of "Artist - Title"), `discogsRelease` (headings
    and indexes left out; "A1/B2" sides numbered through, "2-3", "CD2-3", "2.3" as disc and
    track; "Name (2)" and "Name*" cleaned; the alias where credited by it).
    `parseDiscogsPosition`, `parseDuration`.
  - `pairTracks`: by the files' disc and track numbers when they all have one and none repeats;
    the rest by title (folded: case, accents, "(Remastered)" left aside), then by a length within
    3 s when only one track fits, then, nothing paired and as many files as tracks, by the files'
    order. Each pair says what told it.
  - `scoreRelease` (0–1): a summary by its number of tracks; a release with its tracks 40% that,
    60% the share of files paired with a track of a length within 3 s.
  - `lookupPlan`: per file, the fields that change (title, artist, album, album artist, track
    `n/total`, disc `n/total` only for several discs, year, genre), only where the release says
    something different; a file not paired changes nothing.
- **Checked.** `npm test`: 661 pass; `lookup.ts` 100% lines, 95.8% branches, 100% functions.
- **Next.** L2: `src/lib/lookup.ts` (MusicBrainz's requests at one a second) and the Rename tab's
  panel.

Commit: `feat(lookup): read MusicBrainz and Discogs releases, pair them with the files, plan the tags`

### 2026-11-25 — Proposal: from a jackbird server's page, a folder of the device listening (the owner)

- **The ask.** On the web a jackbird server serves, the folders of the computer one listens on
  cannot be opened.
- **Why.** `lib/adding.ts` `sourcesHere` offers "A folder of this device" in a browser only when no
  service serves the page (`adding.served === false`); and `lib/sources/index.ts` `pickSource`,
  with no server given, opens the service's own folder chooser whenever the page is served
  (`isService()`), never the browser's picker. Only an iPod's drive reaches the browser's picker there.
- **The plan.**
  - `lib/adding.ts`: "device" also on a served page, where the browser can open a folder
    (`deviceAccess() !== "none"`), for anyone signed in (a listener too: the files are theirs, not
    the server's). "A folder of {server}" stays for its owners, after it.
  - `lib/sources/index.ts`: `pickSource(server, { device: true })` goes to the browser's picker
    (`pickInBrowser`) whatever serves the page; `addFrom("device")` asks for it.
  - A tab of such a folder comes back after a reload as one picked on a static page does (its handle
    kept by the browser); to be checked on a served page.
  - A server reached at `http://` (not `https://`, nor `localhost`) is not a secure context: Chromium
    then has no `showDirectoryPicker`, and the folder opens to be read only, as in Safari and
    Firefox (`folderAccessHere`), and is said so.
- **To check.** The service built here (`npm run build`, `cargo build -p jackbird-service`), its page
  in Chromium: Add → "A folder of this device" → a folder of this machine opens as a tab, and comes
  back after a reload; the server's folders still offered to its owner.

Commit: `fix(sources): a server's page opens folders of the device listening too`

### 2026-11-25 — Proposal: shuffle mixes what is left in the queue (the owner)

- **The ask.** Shuffle mixes what is pending in the queue, and the queue stops being presented as
  the list chosen.
- **Now.** `lib/player.ts` `toggleShuffle`: a mode; the queue keeps its order and its name
  (`sourceLabel`, "Queue: Sample music, track 1 of 4"), and an order of chance is kept beside it
  (`playback.order`), the track playing first; off, the queue's own order again.
- **The plan.**
  - Shuffle mixes the queue itself: the tracks after the one playing, in an order of chance
    (`helpers/queue.ts`, a `shuffleUpcoming(position, random)`, tested); the ones played stay where they
    are. The songs put there on purpose (play next, add to queue) are mixed with the rest.
  - The queue's name goes: `sourceLabel` empty, so it reads as the queue ("Queue, track 1 of 4"),
    not as the list it came from.
  - A list's (or Details') Shuffle key: the list played in an order of chance, as now, under the
    name "Queue" too.
- **To decide.**
  1. The deck's key: **an action** (each press mixes again; no lit state, no `playback.order`), or
     **a mode** (on: mixes now and mixes each new queue played; off: leaves the order as it is, since
     the list's order is gone)?
  2. Should "play next" songs keep their place before the rest (mixed only among the rest)?
